## U-316 follow-up (dbo-only identity fast path ROWVERSION hardening) — deferred, not scope-creeped in (2026-08-25)

- [x] **⚠️ CORRECTED 2026-08-31 (U-336 Gate-1): this booking's "future uniform caller-side DELETION" premise is WRONG.** Ground truth (verified in-code): ALL SIX families KEEP their caller-side `if outcome.entity is None: raise` backstop guard — the 4 siblings (`item/connector/cost_code:93`, `sub_cost_code:144`, `attachable/connector/attachment:92`, + item's) kept theirs as **tested backstops**, they did NOT delete them. So the uniform shape is *upstream falsy-id guard + KEPT tested backstop*, and U-336's amended scope = **add the 2 upstream guards to `_upsert_vendor`/`_upsert_customer` + KEEP all 3 caller-side guards, adding Item-style comments + backstop tests** to bring vendor/customer/project to parity with the siblings. → **✅ SHIPPED U-336 (`f174a7fa`), /em Gate-2 PASS 2026-08-31** (2 upstream guards added, all 3 backstops KEPT, mutation-proven; P2 caught+fixed by Codex: `QboVendor` re-parented off `QboVendorUpdate` onto `QboVendorBase` with Optional id/sync_token so a no-Id record parses to None + skips record-level instead of aborting the whole vendor pull — push contract untouched. NOT yet deployed, batchable).** ~~Vendor/Customer/Project QBO pull staging lacks an explicit falsy-id guard that Item/Attachable already
  have.~~ `_upsert_vendor` (`integrations/intuit/qbo/vendor/business/service.py`) and `_upsert_customer`
  (`integrations/intuit/qbo/customer/business/service.py`) pass their staged object's id straight through to
  `run_identity_fastpath_dbo_only` with no truthiness check, unlike `QboItemService._upsert_item`
  (`integrations/intuit/qbo/item/business/service.py:126-127`, `if not qbo_item.id: raise ValueError(...)`) and
  `QboAttachableService._upsert_attachable` (`integrations/intuit/qbo/attachable/business/service.py:254-255`,
  same shape) — both isolated by a try/except so a falsy-id record never reaches its connector. Because of this
  gap, U-316 (`docs/design/u316.md`) could NOT prove the caller-side `if outcome.entity is None: raise
  RuntimeError(...)` guard in `VendorVendorConnector.sync_from_qbo_vendor` /
  `CustomerCustomerConnector.sync_from_qbo_customer` / `CustomerProjectConnector.sync_from_qbo_customer`
  (`integrations/intuit/qbo/{vendor/connector/vendor,customer/connector/customer,customer/connector/project}/business/service.py`)
  is dead code — it was kept, not deleted, unlike the equivalent guard in `ItemCostCodeConnector`/
  `ItemSubCostCodeConnector`/`AttachableAttachmentConnector`, which IS provably reachable only via each
  connector's own tested falsy-`qbo_id` defensive contract (`test_cost_code_no_qbo_id_raises`,
  `test_sub_cost_code_no_qbo_id_raises`, `test_no_qbo_id_raises_without_ever_downloading`), never via a live
  production race path. Closing this gap — adding the same upstream `if not id: raise ValueError(...)` guard to
  `_upsert_vendor`/`_upsert_customer` — would make the equivalent "provably dead" claim hold for these 3
  families too, letting a future unit finish the uniform caller-side deletion U-316's design doc originally
  intended. Out of scope for U-316 per two-phase design-gated discipline: this would change the pull's
  error-vs-skip contract on a genuinely-missing QBO Id, which deserves its own consideration, not a drive-by
  add alongside a concurrency-primitive hardening unit. DONE 2026-08-31 (U-336): upstream guards added to
  `_upsert_vendor`/`_upsert_customer`; all 3 caller-side guards KEPT as tested backstops (uniform with the 4
  sibling families — the uniform-deletion idea was dropped: the siblings kept theirs as pinned defensive
  contracts).

The previously-UNGUARDED CostCode gap is closed. ~~`run_identity_fastpath_dbo_only` has no
  structural hook for a `stamp_identity`-returned-`None` ROWVERSION race~~, unlike its HIT-branch
  `on_apply_returned_none` (which the primitive itself auto-invokes). U-310's `_stamp_customer_identity` had to

## U-303 follow-ups (CustomerProjectConnector name-match bind CustomerId write) — deferred, not scope-creeped in (2026-08-22)

U-303 fixed one gap: the "no mapping, name-match bind" branch of `sync_from_qbo_customer`
(`integrations/intuit/qbo/customer/connector/project/business/service.py`) now writes the resolved parent
`CustomerId`, same as every other resolution branch. Discovered during that unit's Codex-degraded, adversarially-
verified Claude Workflow hunt (workspace out of Codex credits this session — see the U-278/U-280/U-281/U-282/
U-286/U-287/U-289 board rows for the same episode) but deliberately not folded in — different call site / broader
blast radius than this unit's narrow scope:

- [x] **✅ DONE — closed by U-311 (dbo-only repoint) + U-328/U-331 (verified 2026-08-31).** The stale-mapping heal-repoint branch was ELIMINATED: it existed only because the mapping table was a second, independently-writable store that could go stale, and dbo-only mode has no second store left (see the connector's own comment at `service.py:171-174`). The surviving MISS-branch stamp (`_stamp_project_identity`) now routes through the shared `stamp_dbo_identity_with_lock`, which owns the apply-fields-then-write-identity ordering + the None-race guard — so the `set_qbo_identity`-before-field-write ordering bug no longer exists. ~~"Healed repoint" branch (lines ~238-251, unchanged by U-303) has the identical `set_qbo_identity`-before-
  field-write ordering bug U-303's own first draft had — and it's still live.~~ When a `qbo.CustomerProject`
  mapping exists but its bound Project reads empty, and a same-name local Project is found to repoint to, the code
  calls `set_qbo_identity(id=replacement.id, ...)` (line 238) BEFORE `_apply_project_fields_and_sync(replacement,
  ...)` (line 243), whose internal `update_by_id` reuses `replacement`'s RowVersion captured earlier at the
  `read_by_name()` on line 210. Reaching this branch always means the top-of-method `run_identity_fastpath` missed
  on this exact `(qbo_id, realm_id)` for every Project row, so `SetProjectQboIdentity`'s WHERE guard is
  unconditionally true and its UPDATE always bumps `replacement`'s RowVersion first — the field-write then sends a
  stale RowVersion and deterministically raises `raise_concurrent_write_race("healed repoint")` on every real
  occurrence of this branch (the documented OHR2-CHAPEL missing-Project scenario). Self-limiting, not data-lossy:
  the mapping repoint + identity stamp already committed before the raise, so the *next* sync of that QboCustomer
  hits the dbo-identity fast path directly and succeeds — but that's up to a 4-hour delay
  (`build.one.scheduler`'s `sync_qbo_customer` timer runs `0 10 */4 * * *`), plus a guaranteed spurious error-log
  on every occurrence, and the Description/Status/address refresh this branch's comments promise ("HEAL in
  place") don't actually land until that delayed retry. Masked by a false-positive test today:
  `tests/test_qbo_customer_project_heal.py::test_heal_repoints_mapping_when_project_missing_but_name_match_unbound`
  and `tests/test_u276_customer_project_qbo_identity_repoint.py`'s healed-repoint tests both stub
  `update_by_id.side_effect = lambda p: p` / a bare `Mock()` `set_qbo_identity` with zero RowVersion modeling, so
  neither can see the bug. Fix mirrors U-303's own: reorder to field-write-first / identity-stamp-second (matching
  the "existing mapping" update path a few lines above, which already gets this order right), and port
  `test_u303_customer_project_namematch_customerid.py`'s `_build_rowversion_realistic_connector` state-machine
  fake onto the healed-repoint happy-path test so a reversion is actually caught. Confirmed independently by 4
  separate agent traces across U-303's two review rounds (2 hunt lenses + 2 round-2 verifiers), not a single
  unverified claim.
- [ ] **`heal_missing_mapping()` (lines ~718-762, used by the invoice-pull connector to auto-heal a missing
  mapping) has a broader version of the same class of gap U-303 fixed** — it binds an unmapped local Project by
  name (`create_mapping` + `_sync_addresses`) but never calls `_apply_project_fields_and_sync` at all, so it
  writes none of Name/Description/Status/CustomerId, not just CustomerId. It also never resolves a parent
  Customer in the first place (`_get_parent_customer_id` isn't called here), unlike `sync_from_qbo_customer`.
  Flagged at U-303's Gate-1 and deliberately deferred — different connector caller (invoice pull, not the
  QboCustomer pull `sync_from_qbo_customer` itself), and fixing it well means first deciding whether it should
  resolve+write CustomerId too or intentionally stay field-inert (it's a "just make the invoice resolvable"
  heal, not a full sync) — a design call worth its own Gate-1, not a piggyback on this unit.

## U-281 follow-ups (account Phase-4 prerequisite, AP-account dbo-native cache) — deferred, not scope-creeped in (2026-08-20/21)

Confirmed by a Codex-degraded, adversarially-verified fallback hunt (workspace-wide Codex outage this session — see the U-280/U-277/U-278/U-282/U-286/U-287 board rows for the same episode). Both are pre-existing architectural characteristics this unit's new write joins, not new categories of risk it invented — logged rather than patched as a bandaid inside a narrowly-scoped unit.

- [x] **✅ SHIPPED U-337 (`2bc593fa`), /em Gate-2 PASS 2026-08-31 (P0-surface, Opus builder) —** `POST /api/v1/sync/qbo-accounts` now wraps the sync in `qbo_app_lock` (409 on contention). **P1 caught+fixed by Codex:** both this route AND the admin `sync/qbo/{entity}` path now compute the lock resource via a shared `qbo_entity_sync_lock_resource(entity)` helper (`base/locking.py`) → identical `qbo_sync:account` key → they genuinely serialize (the original API key was realm-suffixed and never contended). Full suite green, mutation-proven RED, follow-up booked (sibling routers still unlocked). NOT yet deployed, batchable.** — no app lock, no drain secret, unlike the admin-path `sync/qbo/{entity}` endpoint which wraps every call in `qbo_app_lock`. Two overlapping calls to it for the SAME realm can race `QboAccountService.sync_from_qbo`'s read-full-mirror-then-write shape; the new `SetCompanyApAccount` sproc (U-281) is a deliberate plain overwrite (NULL is a valid answer) with no RowVersion/OCC guard, so the run that reads earlier but writes later wins — last-writer-wins. Pre-existing exposure for the account upserts themselves too, not introduced by U-281's own write. Real-world probability is low (a company's AP account essentially never changes), but the endpoint's lack of locking is worth its own unit regardless of this one finding.
- [ ] **`SetCompanyApAccount`'s UPDATE bumps `dbo.Company.RowVersion`** (a real SQL Server `ROWVERSION` column that auto-advances on ANY update to the row, not just the columns written) — every ~4h (`sync_qbo_account` timer cadence). An admin with the Edit Company form open (`PUT /update/company/{public_id}`, optimistic-concurrency via `WHERE RowVersion = @RowVersion`) could get an unexplained conflict-on-save if the timer lands mid-edit, with nothing in the UI connecting it to AP-account syncing. Same exposure class the pre-existing `SetCompanyQboIdentity` already carries (daily `sync_qbo_company_info` pull) — now also present on U-281's ~4-hourly cadence. Fixing it "properly" would mean moving cache-only fields off the ROWVERSION-tracked row entirely (a real schema change) — judged out of proportion to the actual risk (a rarely-edited admin-only screen, and the failure mode is a benign retry-prompt, not data loss) unless it starts showing up in practice.

## U-289 follow-ups (item CostCode+SubCostCode Phase-4 repoint) — deferred (2026-08-20)

U-289 repointed `ItemCostCodeConnector`/`ItemSubCostCodeConnector`'s own identity resolution off `qbo.Item`/
`qbo.Item{CostCode,SubCostCode}` onto `dbo.{CostCode,SubCostCode}`'s native `QboId`/`RealmId`, via the shared
`run_identity_fastpath()` helper (U-287). Confirmed by a Codex-degraded, adversarially-verified Claude Workflow
5-lens hunt (workspace-wide Codex outage this session — see the U-278/U-280/U-282/U-286/U-287/U-281 board rows for
the same episode); 7 raw findings confirmed, all either fixed or booked here:

- [ ] **`ItemSubCostCodeConnector`'s parent-CostCode resolution has no fast-path/conflict protection** — unlike
  its own SubCostCode identity resolution (which this unit hardened), the parent lookup (`qbo_item_repo.read_by_qbo_id`
  → `cost_code_mapping_repo.read_by_qbo_item_id`, `sync_from_qbo_item` lines ~96-114) is pre-existing, unchanged code
  that still hops the legacy `qbo.Item`/`qbo.ItemCostCode` mapping table with zero identity-conflict detection. If a
  parent CostCode's dbo-native identity and its `ItemCostCode` mapping row drift apart — the exact "identity theft"
  class this unit's own fast path exists to catch, and which caused a live-prod P0 on 2026-08-20 across other
  families — a *direct* sync of that parent CostCode now correctly hard-stops, but a SubCostCode synced under it
  will silently resolve `cost_code_id` from the stale/conflicting mapping row anyway, with no exception and no
  recorded reconciliation issue. Fix: route the parent lookup through `CostCodeService.read_by_qbo_identity()` (or
  through `ItemCostCodeConnector` itself) before trusting the mapping-table hop, mirroring this unit's own pattern.
  Deliberately not fixed in U-289 itself — real scope expansion beyond "wire the fast path into each entity's own
  `sync_from_qbo_item`", same class of deferral U-276 used for its 4 pull-resolvers.
- [ ] **`dbo.costcode.sql`'s `CREATE TABLE` block has no idempotent `ALTER TABLE ADD [CreatedByUserId]`** (campaign-wide
  pre-existing gap, not introduced by U-289 — already tracked below at "Base SQL files are not self-contained from
  scratch"; `CreateCostCode`'s `@CreatedByUserId` param this unit added is base-file reconciliation to match what's
  already live in prod via `gap2_created_by_user_id.sql`/`_finalize.sql`, mirroring `entities/company/sql/dbo.company.sql`'s
  identical, explicitly-scoped-out precedent from U-277).

## Packet compression before SharePoint/Box upload — SCOPED, not built (2026-08-18)

**Ask:** shrink the invoice draw packet before it's uploaded to SharePoint + Box (future packets). **Findings from probing the MR2-MAIN-09 packet (42,096,582 bytes, 143 pages):**
- `_generate_invoice_packet` (`entities/invoice/api/router.py:828-843`) already runs a **pikepdf stream-compression** step, but it only touches PDF *streams* — it does NOT recompress/downsample embedded images, so it barely moves the needle here.
- The bulk is **images**: 361 image XObjects ≈ 41% of the file; the largest are **12-megapixel phone photos of receipts** (3024×4032, ~1–1.4MB each). No packet needs those at full res.
- **Pure-Python probe (pikepdf + Pillow — both already in requirements; NO Ghostscript / NO Dockerfile change needed):** downsample each image whose longest side > ~1700px (~150 DPI on letter) + re-encode JPEG q65, then the existing pikepdf save → **42MB → 11.2MB (73% smaller)**. Legibility verified fully intact on a thermal Lowe's receipt (line items/prices/QR/VISA auth) and a Hoover Paint invoice photo (quantities/prices/fine print).
- **Recommended build:** add the downsample loop to `_generate_invoice_packet` right before the existing pikepdf save (single point → both SharePoint + Box + the stored Attachment blob all get the smaller file). Skip already-small images + vector/text content; **failure-isolate** (any image that won't recompress is left as-is; whole-step failure falls back to today's stream-only packet). Expose max-px + JPEG-quality as tunable module constants. Fidelity options probed: **1700px/q65 → ~11MB (recommended)**, 2200px/q75 → ~16–18MB (max fidelity), 1400px/q60 → ~8MB (most aggressive). Probe script pattern lived in the session scratchpad (`compress_probe.py`).

- [ ] **`#` in a filename breaks the SharePoint small-file upload URL — dead-letters silently.** `upload_small_file` (`integrations/ms/sharepoint/external/client.py:432-459`) embeds the filename raw into the Graph colon-addressed path (`drives/{id}/items/{id}:/{filename}:/content`) with no percent-encoding. A `#` in the filename is a URL fragment delimiter — everything after it (including the trailing `:/content` segment) is silently dropped before the request is sent, so the PUT lands on the DriveItem's own metadata endpoint instead of its content endpoint, which then rejects the binary body with `MsValidationError: Entity only allows writes with a JSON Content-Type header`. `entities/invoice/business/naming.py`'s `_FILENAME_SANITIZE_RE` (`r'[<>:"/\\|?*\x00-\x1f\x7f]'`) strips Windows-illegal characters but not `#` — same family as KI-45 (URL length) / KI-47 (control chars) in the invoice playbook, but a distinct gap: URL-reserved characters, not filesystem-illegal ones. **Found 2026-08-18** running the OL-14 InvoiceAgent playbook: 2 of 66 line-item PDFs (Vulcan Construction Materials stone deliveries, filenames containing `#7`/`#67`) dead-lettered on attempt 1 (non-retryable, so no amount of outbox retry would have fixed it). Box succeeded on the identical files (different upload mechanism, no URL path-embedding), confirming this is SharePoint-path-specific. Recovered manually for OL-14 by re-uploading with `#`→`_`. Fix: either percent-encode the filename segment in `upload_small_file`/`upload_large_file`'s URL construction, or add `#` (and likely `%`, `?`, `&` — also URL-special) to `_FILENAME_SANITIZE_RE`. The latter is cheaper but would change historical filename conventions going forward; the former fixes it at the transport layer without touching naming policy. Worth auditing for other URL-reserved characters while in there.
- [ ] **`WorksheetReconcileService` / `InvoiceDrawPushService.push_draw` can't read a DETAILS worksheet whose billable-amount header isn't exactly `"BILLABLE"`.** `entities/invoice/business/worksheet_reconcile.py:8-18`'s `_KNOWN_HEADERS` maps the exact string `"BILLABLE"` → `billable`, but the real header text on at least project OL's (`925 Overton Lea`, id 23) DETAILS tab is `"BILLABLE AMOUNT"` (confirmed live: row 1, col N). Since `detect_header_and_columns` requires an exact-string match, `"billable"` never lands in `col_map`, and `push_draw` halts hard at the `worksheet_reconcile` step with `HTTPException("Worksheet missing columns: billable")` — even though the column plainly exists at the same index (13) the manual playbook recipe already documents. **Found 2026-08-18** running the OL-14 InvoiceAgent playbook; had to fall back to the playbook's manual Step 6/7/9 SQL+Graph recipe (position-based column access, immune to header text) to complete the draw. Fix: broaden `_KNOWN_HEADERS`'s billable-column match to accept both `"BILLABLE"` and `"BILLABLE AMOUNT"` (and maybe do a `startswith`/substring match generally, since the same brittleness likely affects other `_KNOWN_HEADERS` entries across the ~135-project DETAILS-tracker fleet — worth auditing header text variance across a sample of mapped workbooks before picking the exact fix shape). Until fixed, any project whose header differs even slightly forces the manual fallback path.

## U-278 follow-ups (vendorcredit header/reference Phase-4 repoint) — flagged 2026-08-19

U-278 repointed `VendorCreditBillCreditConnector`'s header identity resolution off `qbo.VendorCredit`/
`qbo.VendorCreditBillCredit` onto `dbo.BillCredit`'s native `QboId`/`RealmId`, mirroring U-276's pattern.
Line-level fingerprint staging is explicitly out of scope (see `docs/staging_removal_phase4_5_scoping.md` §2/§7/§8
item 4) — own follow-on unit now that U-274 unblocked it. Two items surfaced building it:

- [ ] **PRIORITY — U-276's already-shipped `CustomerCustomerConnector`/`CustomerProjectConnector` likely carry the
  same identity-theft-on-conflict-fallthrough bug this unit's fix-loop found and fixed for BillCredit, and — unlike
  BillCredit's fix, which was caught before any deploy — U-276's SQL AND Python code are BOTH already live in prod
  (`ReadCustomerByQboIdAndRealmId`/`ReadProjectByQboIdAndRealmId` confirmed live via direct `sys.objects` query,
  modify_date 2026-08-19 15:03).** The bug: on a detected identity `"conflict"` (dbo-identity match exists on one
  row, but the `qbo.Customer{Customer,Project}` mapping table points at a DIFFERENT row), `sync_from_qbo_customer`
  records a reconciliation issue then **falls through** to the legacy mapping-table path, which calls
  `set_qbo_identity(qbo_id=..., realm_id=...)` on the CONFLICTING row — `Set{Customer,Project}QboIdentity`'s own
  theft-detection `UPDATE ... WHERE [Id] <> @Id AND [QboId] = @QboId AND (RealmId match)` then silently NULLs the
  dbo-identity-matched row's QboId/RealmId, corrupting exactly the row the conflict check just confirmed legitimately
  holds that identity — in the same call that logged "Not auto-repointed — investigate which side is correct."
  Confirmed reachable via direct code trace (`integrations/intuit/qbo/customer/connector/customer/business/service.py`
  and `.../project/business/service.py`); independently flagged as PLAUSIBLE by a second reviewer during this unit's
  round-2 re-review, who read the same code from a different angle. U-278's fix (raise `ValueError` immediately after
  recording the conflict issue, never fall through) is a direct template — same shape needed in both files. This
  needs its own Gate-1'd unit (SQL is live, so any Python-only fix ships hot — treat as P0-surface, verify a live
  conflict scenario isn't already silently corrupting Customer/Project data before scoping the fix).
- [x] **DONE — U-287 (2026-08-20). Shared helper `integrations/intuit/qbo/base/identity_fastpath.py` built; all 6
  copies deleted.** `resolve_mapping_state()` (pure, owns the both-directions algorithm) + `run_identity_fastpath()`
  (owns the control flow: check-before-write ordering, the conflict hard stop, the self-heal create-race re-check),
  parameterized by each family's mapping-repo accessors, field-apply callback and message builders. Each connector
  keeps a thin `_resolve_mapping_state` wrapper delegating to the shared function (preserves the 20+ existing tests
  that call it directly) and its own `_raise_identity_mapping_conflict_issue` (the message names family-specific
  downstream readers — deliberately NOT collapsed). **conflict → RAISE is structural in the helper: there is no
  opt-out parameter, so the 2026-08-20 fall-through P0 cannot be reintroduced by a caller.** Net −602/+446 lines.
  **Also closed a live P0 the hotfix had missed:** `company_info` + `physical_address` (U-277, shipped one day
  BEFORE the hotfix) still fell through on conflict behind a `protected_*_id` guard that only covered the
  re-resolves-to-the-same-row case — leaving the different-row identity theft (physical_address's trailing
  `set_qbo_identity` stamp firing on the wrong Address) and the duplicate-mint path open. Both now raise; the dead
  guards deleted; 4 of their tests deliberately rewritten to demand the raise. Verify: 2442 pytest green; the
  U-276/278/279 suites pass UNCHANGED (the equivalence proof); mutation matrix 12/12 caught in an isolated worktree.
- [ ] ~~The `_resolve_mapping_state`/`_raise_identity_mapping_conflict_issue`/`_apply_*_fields_and_sync` fast-path
  trio is hand-copied~~ (superseded by U-287 above; original text kept for provenance)
  — hand-copied a 3rd time (Customer, Project, BillCredit — U-276 built it twice, U-278 a third), with
  ~7 more repoints confirmed queued (`docs/staging_removal_phase4_5_scoping.md` §8: company_info+physical_address,
  attachable, account, term/vendor/item, bill/purchase, invoice). Flagged independently by 3 separate `/simplify`
  review angles (reuse, simplification, altitude) during this unit's Pass-2 — the fragile invariant this trio exists
  to protect is exactly what went wrong in the finding above, and hand-copying it again is how a fix like that goes
  unnoticed 3x over. Right-depth fix: lift into a shared helper in `integrations/intuit/qbo/base/` (parameterized by
  the mapping repo's by-local-id/by-external-id accessors + entity/drift-type strings) as its own Track-A-style
  prerequisite before the next repoint unit, mirroring how `docs/staging_removal_phase4_5_scoping.md` §3 already
  carves out shared blockers. Not done here — deliberately kept this unit tightly scoped per the standing Gate-1
  discipline; the fix above (raise-on-conflict) should land in the shared helper too, once one exists, rather than
  being hand-patched into 3 separate copies again.

The Pass-1/Pass-2 review surfaced these **larger structural items, deliberately not done in the same unit**:

Built the seam anyway (Chris-approved at Gate-1): `QboInvoiceService.cost_coded_lines_for_invoice(invoice_id)` resolves each QBO invoice line's cost code by ID (QboItem -> ItemSubCostCode/ItemCostCode -> SubCostCode/CostCode) instead of parsing `ItemRefName`; `draw_financials.py` now calls it and the string parser (`_cc_number_from_itemref`/`_norm_cc_number`) is deleted. See U-292 follow-ups below.

Have `all_draws_for_project` also return the coded subset it already builds (it's captured pre-merge) so the router does ONE invoice pass; lock it with an equivalence test (`all[0] == coded_draws_for_project(...)`). ~0.3s on typical projects, seconds near the 500-invoice cap.

Today a QBO-mirror is dropped by `(date, billed total)` while a re-issue (`-04-2`) is merged by a label regex guarded on "base exists in set" — which fires by numeric coincidence and hard-codes one project's numbering. A `supersedes_invoice_id`/revision FK (or the QBO DocNumber revision convention captured at pull time) collapses both into one identity rule.

Either distribute QBO markup back into sibling cost codes, or make markup a first-class labeled line in both paths; also split "markup (expected)" from "unresolved cost code (anomaly)" so a parse failure surfaces instead of hiding in `Uncoded`.

Converge on one helper (cost_code entity or `shared/`). (`draw_financials.py`'s own `_norm_cc_number` copy is gone — U-292 deleted it along with the ItemRefName parser it supported.)

### U-293 follow-ups (bill_line_item Phase-4 line-identity repoint, Bill pilot, 2026-08-21) — deferred, not fixed in this unit

- [x] **✅ SHIPPED U-339 (`5338373b`), /em Gate-2 PASS 2026-08-31 (P0-surface) — stamp moved into the `try/except ValueError/else:`, so identity is stamped only when create_mapping succeeded (DatabaseConstraintError still propagates). Root cause chain, pre-existing not introduced by U-293: the CREATE branch in `integrations/intuit/qbo/bill/connector/bill_line_item/business/service.py` calls `stamp_line_identity_or_warn` unconditionally even when the preceding `create_mapping()` call raised (swallowed `ValueError`) — this is exactly how the "5 stamped-but-unmapped anomaly rows" found in live prod at this unit's Gate-1 arose. If such a row's QBO-side content later drifts before its next pull, the fast path correctly refuses to blind-overwrite it (MISSING → falls through), but Shape-B's content-fingerprint match then WON'T match the drifted content either, so a **duplicate** BillLineItem is created instead of the original being updated. This is the accepted trade-off of the P1 fix (favor never-silently-corrupt over never-duplicate) — not a flaw in U-293's own execution — but is worth its own follow-up: either fix the CREATE branch's swallowed-mapping-failure to not stamp identity when the mapping wasn't actually created (closing the root cause), or give Shape-B a "same recycled QboId, content changed, adopt-with-a-warning" heuristic. Low volume today (5 rows total in live Bill data as of 2026-08-21) — not urgent.

The shared helper, the MISSING-never-self-heals design, and the parent-scoped `Read*LineItemBy{Parent}IdAndQboId` sproc pattern are all already proven — each sibling family's own unit should be mostly mechanical wiring (new sproc, `apply_fields` extraction, connector wiring) rather than new design work, per this unit's own Gate-1 map (all 4 families found structurally identical: same dual-write shape, same 2-hop read pattern, same fallback-fingerprint pattern, near-zero backfill gap everywhere).

## `/bill/create` Complete Bill misreports "offline" on large PDF uploads (found 2026-07-29)

- [x] **SHIPPED 141491d4 + DEPLOYED 2026-08-17 — U-240 — watermark commit correctness cluster (P1, FLEET-WIDE, live in prod). IN FLIGHT 2026-08-16.** Booked from a 32-agent adversarial review (4 lenses, 0 agents errored, 19 confirmed findings). (a) **The hold-bound force-advance defeats the contract it bounds, on the FIRST commit** — `_held_duration` (`scripts/sync_helper.py`) anchors on `dbo.Sync.ModifiedDatetime`, the age of the ROW, not how long the current hold has lasted; a `--skip-sync-update` run never stamps it, so on the first committing run `held` is already past the 7200s bound and the watermark force-advances PAST FAILED RECORDS. Confirmed live: the reimburse_charge Sync row's `ModifiedDatetime` was **4 days stale**, which is why its watermark was set BY HAND rather than through `WatermarkRun.commit`. (b) **A TxnDate is written into a field consumed as `Metadata.LastUpdatedTime`** (`commit()` writes `f"{end_date}T23:59:59"`) — unrelated time axes. (c) **The watermark write is not monotonic** — the only comparison sits on the RowVersion-conflict fallback branch, so a clean single-writer run can drive it BACKWARD. (d) **QBO REJECTS `TxnDate` filters on ReimburseCharge entirely** (`Invalid query`, measured 2026-08-16 on both `SELECT *` and `COUNT(*)`), so `--start-date`/`--end-date` are dead for that entity and the CLI epilog documents a mode that cannot work; `Metadata.LastUpdatedTime` and `HasBeenInvoiced` filters DO work. (e) **The CLI exits 0 on total failure** — no `sys.exit(1)`, so a scripted chunk loop cannot detect a dead chunk.

- [x] **SHIPPED 1c3aa9b7 + DEPLOYED 2026-08-17 — U-241 — Invoice's qbo mapping-cleanup gap + the line-item topology U-226 did not cover. IN FLIGHT 2026-08-16.** U-226 (`a4c2b768`) taught Bill/BillCredit/Expense delete services to clear their own header mapping via `delete_own_qbo_mapping_before_header`, but Invoice was left out, and the helper is **header-only by name and docstring** while the largest measured dangling group is at the LINE level (122 `qbo.InvoiceLineItemInvoiceLine`, vs 6 BillLineItem and 9 header; U-225 independently measured 141 orphans fleet-wide). Line deletes also happen without a header delete. ⚠️ Once U-225's FK migration is applied, any delete path that does not clear its mapping starts raising **SQL 547** where it used to silently orphan.

- [x] **SHIPPED 7d533d53 + DEPLOYED 2026-08-17 — U-242 — KI-32's premise does not hold: QBO exposes NO `LinkedTxn` on ReimburseCharge (P1 for invoice linking; measured 2026-08-16).** Verified three ways against the live realm — the `SELECT *` query response, a direct `GET /reimbursecharge/{id}`, and **minorversions 65 / 70 / 75** — including for `HasBeenInvoiced=false` records, which is exactly when the reverse pointer is supposed to exist. All **26,582** staged rows therefore carry a NULL source pointer, and `parse_reimburse_charge`'s `_SOURCE_TXN_TYPES = ("Bill","Purchase")` extraction is dead code against this surface. This invalidates the stated rationale for the 15-minute cadence ("data is IRRECOVERABLE if missed") that appears in `build.one.scheduler/function_app.py`, the RC service docstring, and this file. The staged inventory is still correct and worth keeping current (CustomerRef, Amount, TxnDate, HasBeenInvoiced, and the line's `ReimburseLineDetail.ItemRef` = cost code). **Deterministic Tier-0 source linking needs a different signal** — fingerprint matching on (customer, amount, date, item) is the obvious candidate. Re-evaluate the 15-min cadence while you are there; it is currently buying less than its comments claim.

- [x] **SHIPPED dcf87132 + DEPLOYED 2026-08-17 — U-243 — mapping-cleanup double-submit race + closure factory.** Closed the P2 race U-241's hunt confirmed but deliberately did not fix: `delete_own_qbo_mapping_before_header` was a read → delete → (on failure) recreate sequence with no lock. Fixed at the right altitude — the `sp_getapplock` went **inside the shared helper**, keyed `qbo_mapping_delete:{entity_label}:{entity_id}`, so **all 7 call sites are covered transitively** and the 3 `*_line_item` services needed no change. Same-entity deletes serialize, different-entity deletes do not, and a contended lock **fails closed** with a raise. Urgency was real: since `u225_qbo_mapping_fk_gaps.sql` landed (qbo.* FKs 14 → 36), a lost race raises **SQL 547** at the user instead of silently orphaning. Also folded the 4x-duplicated `_on_*_mapping_restore_failed` closures into `make_restore_failed_recorder`.

- [x] **SHIPPED 74203bac 2026-08-17 (u244-tierc-source-linking) — U-244 — wire the Tier C fingerprint into invoice source linking.** Implements `docs/rc_source_linking_signal_2026_08_16.md` Section 5 items 1/2/3/5/6 (item 4, the scheduler cadence, is DONE — RC relaxed to hourly in scheduler `54869fa`). ⚠️ **Tier C is NOT deterministic** — 97.14% unique / 1.06% ambiguous / 1.80% unmatched over the full 26,582-row population. The ~1% ambiguity and ~2% misses are design inputs, not edge cases; nothing may silently pick a winner among ambiguous candidates or present a fingerprint match with the same confidence as a real mapping.

- [x] **SHIPPED c1c79e40 2026-08-17 (u246-reconciliation-issue-lifecycle) — U-246 — `qbo.ReconciliationIssue` is write-only.** Measured against prod 2026-08-17: **19,426 rows, every one `Status='open'`** — zero ever acknowledged or resolved, despite the table already carrying `Status`, `AcknowledgedAt`, `ResolvedAt`, `ReconcileRunId`. The machinery exists and has never been used. This matters beyond housekeeping: this table is the durable follow-up mechanism the ENTIRE QBO wave leans on — every heal-don't-delete path, every orphaned-mapping recorder, the partial-delete recorder, and U-234's new upload-failure type all assume someone can find and act on these. At 19,426 undifferentiated open rows, nobody can. Scope: make the lifecycle real, triage the existing population (propose but do NOT execute a bulk-resolve), and fold in U-227's drift-type registry. ~12 known-bogus fixture rows are in there from earlier testing.

- [x] **SHIPPED 8d591f29 2026-08-17 (u237-upload-worker-timeout) — U-237 — Blocking I/O in `/upload/attachment` + no gunicorn `--timeout` → worker killed mid-upload → client mislabels it "offline" (P1, user-facing on Complete Bill).** Traced from a Chris report: clicking Complete Bill on the reworked `/bill/create` page (web) surfaces "Not saved — you are offline." even though the browser has connectivity. Root cause is a server-side connection reset misread as a network failure, not an actual offline state.
  - **Chain:** `POST /api/v1/upload/attachment` (`entities/attachment/api/router.py:282`, `async def`) runs several **synchronous, blocking** calls directly on the event loop, never `await asyncio.to_thread(...)`: `compact_pdf()` (`shared/pdf_utils.py:83`, CPU-bound PDF rewrite) and `storage.upload_file()` (`shared/storage.py:151`), which itself makes TWO sync `httpx.Client` calls — `_ensure_container_exists()` (`shared/storage.py:361`) and the blob `PUT` (`shared/storage.py:193`), each configured with a 120s client-side timeout. `startup.sh` launches gunicorn with **no `--timeout` flag**, so the default 30s worker timeout applies — if the blocking chain runs ≥30s (a large or slow-to-upload PDF), the arbiter SIGKILLs the worker **mid-request**. The client's in-flight `fetch()` then gets an abrupt connection reset, which the browser reports as `TypeError: Failed to fetch`. `build.one.web/src/api/client.ts:161-169` catches ANY `TypeError` from `fetch()` and unconditionally reshapes it into `OfflineError` + fires "Not saved — you are offline." (`client.ts:34-39,152-156`) — regardless of actual connectivity. The class's own doc comment (`client.ts:141-144`) names the tradeoff: it collapses "TypeError's many causes" into one bucket, which is exactly the blind spot here.
  - **Second bug riding along:** because the blocking blob calls run on the shared asyncio event loop (not offloaded to a thread), a slow/large PDF upload also stalls every OTHER in-flight request on that worker process for however long the blocking I/O runs — not just the uploading user's own request.
  - **Why it surfaced now:** the rebuilt `BillCreate.tsx` flow (`build.one.web/src/pages/bills/BillCreate.tsx`) uploads the dropped PDF as the FIRST network call in the Complete/Submit sequence (`upload/attachment` → `create/bill` → `create/bill_line_item`* → `complete/bill/{id}`), so larger scanned PDFs now reliably hit this path on a hot, frequently-used action. The endpoint's blocking-I/O problem likely predates this session; the new drag-and-drop UX is what made it reliably reachable.
  - **Verify:** repro with a multi-page/larger PDF vs. a tiny one-pager (only the large one should fail); browser Network tab shows `net::ERR_EMPTY_RESPONSE` / `net::ERR_CONNECTION_RESET` on repro — NOT what an actually-offline browser produces (the request wouldn't even fire); Azure App Service log stream around the failure timestamp for `[CRITICAL] WORKER TIMEOUT (pid:...)`.
  - **Fix (proposed, not yet approved to implement):**
    1. Immediate mitigation — add `--timeout 90` (or higher, sized to worst-case upload) to the gunicorn invocation in `startup.sh`.
    2. Real fix — wrap `compact_pdf()` + `storage.upload_file()` in `asyncio.to_thread(...)` inside `upload_attachment_router`, or migrate `AzureBlobStorage` to `httpx.AsyncClient` so these calls actually await instead of blocking the shared event loop.
    3. Client-side hardening (`build.one.web`) — in the `TypeError` catch in `client.ts`, check `navigator.onLine` at catch-time; if true, surface a distinct "Upload failed — please try again" message instead of the misleading offline copy. Doesn't fix the root cause but stops mislabeling the symptom for any future blocking-I/O bug on another route.

## Re-adjudication follow-ups (2026-08-12) — booked, NOT rushed

Source: `docs/readjudication_u218_2026_08_12.md` (35 CONFIRMED / 1 REFUTED / 1 UNADJUDICATED).
⚠️ **Read that confirmation rate skeptically** — 95% is the mirror image of the original panel's over-refuting, and the adjudicator prompt likely over-corrected. The items below carry real evidence but are LEADS, not a settled backlog; re-verify each at its unit's Gate 1. Fixed immediately (commit below): reconcile_project system intent + the operator guidance that created the live P1 trap, and reclaim un-gated from the write gate.

- [x] **SHIPPED 40fec02f (2026-08-16) — U-232 — Hard guard on the dead-letter re-push path (P1, live in prod).** `POST /sync/bill-to-qbo` → `QboOutboxService.enqueue()` mints a fresh RequestId (`outbox/business/service.py:107`) because the coalesce sproc matches only `Status IN ('pending','failed')` (`qbo.outbox.sql:286`), so a **dead-lettered** row never coalesces. A bill whose push reached QBO but whose mapping write failed is then pushed a SECOND time under a requestid Intuit has never seen → duplicate A/P bill. This unit's own runbook forbids exactly this substitution (`qbo-outbox-dead-letter-replay.md:87`). **Interim mitigation SHIPPED:** `reconcile_project.py` no longer routes operators here and names the replay script instead. The hard guard (refuse, or reuse the dead-lettered RequestId) needs a new read — `ReadDeadLetterQboOutboxByEntity` — hence a SQL step, which is why it is its own unit rather than a rushed patch.
- [x] **SHIPPED — U-233 — Retry budget is not larger than the per-request timeout it must survive (P2, FLEET-WIDE).** `max_total_budget_seconds` defaults to 30.0 while tier A read/write is also 30.0 (`base/client.py:44`), and `execute_with_retry` measures the budget across ALL attempts including the first (`retry.py:189-191`). So a single timed-out request consumes the whole budget and `for_writes`/`for_reads` deliver **exactly one attempt** — retry is defeated for precisely the failure it exists for. `for_uploads_single` already handles this via `TIER_C_REQUEST_CEILING_SECONDS + 10`; the general policies do not. Deliberately NOT patched inline: this changes retry semantics on every QBO call in a request path bounded by the 240s gateway timeout, so it needs its own two-pass review, not an appended edit. **RESOLVED:** the production fix landed earlier in `e2813d86` (`_budget_for_tier`, sizing the budget from the actual per-request timeout tier) and is ALREADY LIVE in the deployed image `ba266543` — verified 2026-08-16. `7d10fcca` then closed the U-235 half by making the covering tests non-vacuous (they previously injected a fixture whose fake clock exhausted the budget, so they could not fail); mutation matrix confirms the original bug is now genuinely caught. **Residual (low):** the budget-exceeded comparison in `retry.py` has a boundary-only mutation gap with no covering test.
- [x] **SHIPPED 3b3c7a09 (2026-08-16) — U-234 — Make the at-most-once upload honest (P2 cluster: 4 findings, 3 lenses).** `RetryPolicy.for_uploads_single` and `post_multipart` both justify `max_attempts=1` with "Transient resilience lives one level up: the outbox row retries with a mapping guard." **That control does not exist** — `_sync_attachments_to_qbo` catches per-attachment exceptions and re-raises only budget/write-refused, so the outbox never sees the failure and never retries. **Verified NOT a regression:** the pre-U-218e client did a bare `upload_client.post()` with no retry, so at-most-once was already the behavior — what U-218e added is a false justification and an invisible failure. Fix = correct the comments AND record a durable `ReconciliationIssue` on upload failure so a dropped attachment is tracked rather than silent. Related: the mapping guard is written two steps after the irreversible QBO create and is not crash-safe (`attachable/connector/attachment/business/service.py:449`).
- [x] **SHIPPED e7ded8c3 2026-08-17 — U-235 — Test-vacuity cluster (P2/P3).** `tests/test_u218b_bill_push_surface.py:326` asserts the captured call's args against themselves (reduces to a length check); the write-refused park test pins both operator-facing constants against themselves though the prefix is a documented runbook SQL predicate (`:219`); the router draft-guard and 404 are exercised only on their pass-through branch (`:46`); `report_db_to_qbo_bills`' headline mock assertion cannot fail. Same class in `test_u218e_attachable_shared_client.py:292` — every test that could detect a restored upload retry injects a `deterministic_retry` fixture whose fake clock exhausts the budget, so the headline at-most-once fix has no protection.

- [x] **U-220 — Converge `sync_from_qbo` on returning `SyncOutcome` — DONE 2026-08-10.** All eleven pull services now RETURN `SyncOutcome[T]` (`Generic[T]` carrying `synced: list[T]`); `outcome=` and `coerce_outcome` deleted; routers read `.synced`; `reimburse_charge`'s dict + its adapter retired; `attachable`/`physical_address` pinned as the only exempt `sync_from_qbo` definitions. `record_synced` was already overloaded (staging in services, projection in 3 scripts) → split out `projected_count`/`record_projected()`. **Enforcement moved twice, and that is the lesson:** an AST guard asserting "ever bound from the service" (ANY check) was defeated by rebinding — passing the guard AND all 1158 tests while committing an empty outcome; hardened to an ALL check, it was then defeated three more ways (helper name-laundering, `push_run` aliasing, filename-glob escape). Enforcement is now a **runtime provenance stamp** — `SyncOutcome.for_service_pull()` + `WatermarkRun.commit` refusing an unstamped outcome *before* the skip branch — plus `commit_push()` as a real verb (deleting the dummy `SyncOutcome()`, the `allow_unstamped` hatch, and the test policing it). Pass 2 collapsed the 219-line binding analyzer to **one flat rule** (no script may construct a `SyncOutcome`), which is *strictly stronger* — it catches the laundering case the analyzer missed — and removed a fail-open non-vacuity check that re-parsed its own error prose. **Mutation matrix 7/7 caught.** Codex **PASS** (no P0/P1/P2); 5-lens hunt 9 raw → 0 survived refutation; every real finding came from mutation-testing the guards. **1165 pytest green (+14).** 34 files, +766/−406. **No SQL step.**

- [x] **U-221 — Fan-out idempotency guards (U-217 follow-up (e)) — DONE 2026-08-10.** Two guards, one per surface that mints a duplicate artifact. **Box** (`BoxFileService.push_blob_to_box`, drain tier): skip the upload when exactly one `box.File` registry row matches folder ∧ name ∧ attachment ∧ `Sha1`; **SharePoint** (`MsOutboxService.enqueue_sharepoint_upload`, enqueue tier): skip the enqueue when a `Status='done'` `ms.Outbox` row's payload matches drive ∧ parent ∧ filename ∧ blob_path ∧ attachment. **Map corrected the brief on three points:** (1) the fan-out is driven by the pull *scripts'* own post-projection loop, not `sync_to_modules` (all eleven scripts do pass `False`); (2) **the "existing-DriveItem check" the brief assumed does not exist** — `ms.DriveItemAttachment` has **0 rows** in prod, `link_driveitem_to_attachment`/`is_attachment_synced` have **zero callers**, and the MS worker takes `attachment_id` in the payload and never uses it, so a registry-based SP guard would never fire (the completed outbox row is the only durable proof a SharePoint write happened — its docstring's linkback claim was false and is now corrected); (3) only **two of four** legs mint junk — Box Excel and MS Excel already no-op at content level (col-Z) *before* writing. **Prod sizing before building:** 10 days = **695 redundant SharePoint uploads** (213 groups/908 rows, 77% waste) + **358 junk Box versions** (231 groups/589 pushes, 61%); since the U-217 deploy = 28 + 31, with same-file gaps of 7-35 min matching the 15-min cadence. Only **6** files had a genuinely different sha1 in that window, so a hash guard suppresses 31 and preserves all 6. **The reviews found the inverse failure twice, both through human repair paths:** the guard broke `POST /sync/invoice/{id}/box` (the documented repair for a missing Box file, whose docstring *relies* on re-upload) → fixed with a `force` payload bypass threaded only from that endpoint; and then `force` was silently revoked by the Box coalesce's wholesale "latest target wins" payload replacement (window = `Status IN ('pending','failed')`, i.e. the whole retry lifetime) → fixed by making force **sticky**, never revocable by a later non-forced caller. A third pass found the SP surface had **no** bypass at all → `shared/fanout_guard.py::idempotency_guards_disabled()` (`DISABLE_FANOUT_IDEMPOTENCY_GUARDS`, default OFF = guards on) as the one operator lever covering every surface, documented in the new runbook `docs/runbooks/fanout-guard-suppressed-upload.md`. **Mutation matrix 19/19 caught** (both polarity inversions, every identity field dropped individually, kill switch ignored/inverted, sticky force removed, `same_attachment_id` relaxed to a wildcard, each guard's exception re-raising instead of falling through). Codex **PASS** (round 3, zero findings, ordering verified by runtime probe); 5-lens hunt + 3-lens fix-round re-hunt, 8/8 lenses returned. **1216 pytest green (+51).** ⚠️ **SQL step** (`ms.outbox.sql`, all 11 existing procs verified base==live) — but **not load-bearing**: a missing `ReadCompletedMsOutboxByEntity` makes the guard fail open (raises → except → enqueue), i.e. exact pre-unit behavior.

Worth remembering: the fix's FIRST iteration compared the destination only when `attachment_id` was absent — which never happens in production — so it left the bug open and, with its payload refresh, inverted which copy was destroyed. Four independent review lenses caught that before it shipped. Mutation-verified. Original text: `MsOutboxService.enqueue()` (`integrations/ms/outbox/business/service.py:108-119`) still coalesces `upload_sharepoint_file` on (EntityType, EntityPublicId, Kind) only — `ReadPendingMsOutboxByEntity` (`ms.outbox.sql:212-241`) has no payload/attachment discrimination — and on a coalesce hit only extends `ReadyAfter`, never refreshes `Payload`. Two attachments enqueued for the same bill in one debounce window still collapse to one row; the second document is silently dropped. (b) **bill_credit and invoice upload to SharePoint synchronously, bypassing the outbox** — still real, same line numbers confirmed (`entities/bill_credit/business/complete_service.py:625`, `entities/invoice/business/service.py:1622,1648` — shifted slightly from 1582/1608 due to intervening commits, same defect). (d) **SharePoint has no document-level drift detector at all** — still real, **triaged by U-258 (2026-08-18), deliberately NOT built there.** Confirmed live: `_handle_upload_sharepoint_file` (`integrations/ms/outbox/business/worker.py`) never writes an `ms.DriveItem`/`ms.DriveItemAttachment` row on a successful upload, so (unlike `box.File`) there is no registry to diff against even if a scan job existed. Two structural reasons this is its own unit, not a same-unit add-on: (1) populating those rows means editing `ms/outbox/business/worker.py`'s upload handler, which sits in other units' territory (U-251/U-253) and was explicitly out of scope here; (2) a real detector additionally needs a new scan/diff job — MS has no enumerate-and-compare mechanism today the way Box's registry spot-check does, so this is a schema (new registry columns/migration) + connector (populate-on-upload) + reconciliation (new scan job) unit, not a control-flow consolidation like (b) above. Own unit — needs its own Map/Gate-1 pitch. (e) ~~Box guard's registry read is unbounded, O(N²) on fan-out~~ — **DONE (U-256, 2026-08-18).** `ReadBoxFilesByEntity` gained optional `@BoxFolderId`/`@Name` NULL-or-match params (parameterized in place rather than forking a second sproc, per Pass-2 reuse review); the guard's `read_by_entity` call now passes both, scoping a 40-PDF draw's per-row read to ≤2 rows instead of the full ~40-row entity history. SQL committed, NOT applied. (f) **Box leg DONE (U-256, 2026-08-18).** New `box.WorkbookEntityPush` content-hash cache, checked BEFORE `BoxExcelUpdateService` acquires its cross-process lock or calls Box; skips the whole lock+download+upload round-trip when an entity's rows (or an invoice's draw-stamp pairs) are unchanged since the last recorded push. Row/pair order canonicalized before hashing (the feeder queries have no stable `ORDER BY`) so the cache can't false-miss on incidental SQL ordering. A review round also caught and fixed a partial-landing edge case: a stamp covering multiple source rows must NOT be cached as fresh unless *every* row in the plan actually matched a workbook row (`skipped == 0`) — a total-loss guard alone (`matched == 0`) let a partially-landed stamp poison the cache and permanently skip the still-missing row's future stamp. **MS leg still open** — SharePoint GETs the whole `usedRange` before every write with no freshness signal at all (no last-push timestamp exists there today, per the original finding); still its own unit. Deferred (logged here, not a blocker): the freshness-record step's per-entity `upsert` loop on a large batch is still N sequential round trips — a proper fix needs a SQL Server table-valued parameter, which this codebase has zero existing precedent for; left as a follow-up rather than introducing a new binding pattern in a Pass-2 cleanup. (g) **Suppressed uploads still count as uploads in the metrics** — still real. `entities/{bill,bill_credit,invoice,expense}` all increment `synced_count` on ANY non-None `enqueue_sharepoint_upload` return, freshly-queued and guard-skipped alike — the `.skipped_*` log lines are the only observable proof the guard fired. **Blocked — fix changes `shared/api/admin.py` response shapes, U-247's boundary** (same class of change as the U-220 lesson). Two more gaps were introduced **by Pass 2 itself**. Final matrix **34/34** real mutations caught (1 excluded as an equivalent mutant). **1507 pytest green (+74).** ⚠️ SQL step (`qbo.outbox.sql`) — **fails open**: a missing sproc logs `qbo.outbox.reclaim.failed` and the drain continues at pre-unit behavior.
- [x] **U-222 — Outbox blanket-handler classification (P1, carved out of U-215's review) — DONE 2026-08-14.** `QboOutboxWorker._process_inner`'s `except Exception` dead-lettered **every** non-`QboError` unconditionally on attempt 1, so a transient SQL timeout/deadlock/dropped-connection anywhere in a handler burned a money-path row permanently — the same failure U-215 fixed for auth, one layer out. Map resolved the brief's three open questions rather than assuming: the right seam is `_process_inner`'s bare except (`_handle_qbo_error` only ever sees `QboError`, structurally the wrong place); no handler converts infra failures into `QboError` (`push_to_qbo`'s own docstring promises QboError-or-subclass but the ~6 local DB round-trips inside `sync_to_qbo_bill` raise their native pyodbc/SQL type); and the retry cost is bounded + idempotency-keyed (≤`MAX_ATTEMPTS` re-runs, same `RequestId` reused so a QBO call that already landed dedups). Shipped `_handle_unexpected_error`, classifying via the existing `base/errors.py::is_retryable_error` (built by U-217). **Pass-1 Workflow hunt (Codex unavailable both this unit's rounds — workspace out of credits, not run) found a real design defect during the /simplify altitude pass, not the correctness hunt:** the initial two-way check (`is_retryable_error(error)` → retry, else dead-letter) silently collapsed "genuinely unknown, never-seen exception" into the same bucket as "confirmed permanent" — since `is_retryable_error` itself defaults `False` for anything unrecognized, an `AttributeError` from a code bug or a transient SQL error whose message just didn't match the hardcoded signature list would still dead-letter on attempt 1, contradicting this unit's own asymmetry argument. Traced against the sibling seam that already uses the same classifier — `base/sync_outcome.py::record_projection_error`'s documented three-rule policy (retryable → hold; plain `ValueError` → skip/permanent; **everything else → hold**) — and mirrored it exactly: retryable → retry; plain non-retryable `ValueError` (the connectors' permanent-data-issue convention, matches the 3-row live-prod fixture) → dead-letter; anything else unrecognized → retry, favoring the cheap-wrong-retry over the expensive-wrong-dead-letter. Pass-1 hunt (4 lenses, 0 dead — verified via journal, the "2 empty results" the harness flagged were genuine `{findings:[]}` clean lenses not failures) also found the retry-scheduling tail was a near-verbatim ~40-line copy of `_handle_qbo_error`'s own tail; extracted a shared `_schedule_retry_or_dead_letter(row, *, last_error, error_class_name, retry_after_seconds=None, extra_log_fields=None)`, byte-identical behavior preserved at the `_handle_qbo_error` call site (same log fields, same `qbo_fault_code`, same `retry_after_seconds` honor). **Mutation matrix: 10/10 caught** across two rounds (initial 6: polarity invert, MAX_ATTEMPTS off-by-one both directions, dropped `+1` on `attempts_so_far`/`next_attempt`, missing `return` fallthrough, except-clause reorder letting `QboError` reach the generic handler; post-classification-fix 4: dropped the `ValueError` distinction, dropped the retryable-chain-walk distinction, fully-inverted double-negation, shared-helper boundary off-by-one) — hermetic harness, `__pycache__` cleared every mutation, confirmed green-on-unmutated before and after. **1669 pytest green.** Booked out: **U-239** (the retry can now re-invoke a bill/expense/invoice push handler from scratch after its QBO call already succeeded but a later local write failed transiently — a pre-existing exposure shared by every already-retryable `QboError` today, widened to more triggers by this unit, not introduced by it; real fix is connector-level and out of this unit's scope). No SQL step, no `/docs` refresh owed (internal worker classification, no API surface change). Deploy: container-only; coordinate with the four other undeployed commits (`bc6bc1f`, `00a3dec`, `a4edde3`, `e2813d8`).
- [x] **SHIPPED 104a0541 2026-08-17 (u239-push-retry-idempotency) — U-239 — Bill/expense/invoice push retry can re-invoke a handler after its QBO write already landed but a later local write then failed (booked out of U-222's Pass-1 hunt).** `BillBillConnector.sync_to_qbo_bill()` (and its expense/invoice siblings) call `client.create_bill()` — which durably creates the Bill in QBO and returns a SyncToken — and only AFTER that do several local-only DB writes (`qbo_bill_repo.create()`, `_store_qbo_bill_line`, `line_connector.create_mapping`) that persist the "already synced" mapping; none of these local writes are guarded against a generic transient exception (only `except ValueError`). If one throws after the QBO create succeeded, the outbox worker retries the row (U-222 widened which triggers reach this — a transient local SQL error now retries where it used to dead-letter; a `QboTimeoutError`/`QboServerError` already retried the identical way before U-222), the retry finds `existing_mapping` still `None`, and re-invokes `sync_to_qbo_bill()` from the top — re-calling `client.create_bill()` with the SAME `row.request_id` and relying entirely on Intuit's `requestid` dedup (documented in CLAUDE.md as "real at-least-once idempotency," with `_recover_duplicate_qbo_bill`/`QboDuplicateError` as a second line of defense) to avoid a genuine duplicate vendor Bill. **Not introduced by U-222** — this exact re-invocation-from-scratch/requestid-dedup reliance is the pre-existing contract every already-retryable `QboError` (timeout, 5xx, rate-limit) has always exercised; U-222 only widens which failure types reach it. Real fix is connector-level (durably record "QBO accepted this" — e.g. persist the returned QBO id — before any further local writes that could throw) and out of U-222's scoped surface (worker.py + error modules only). Verify the actual duplicate-detection behavior first (does the per-company "warn on duplicate bill number" QBO preference matter here, and does `requestid` dedup reliably cover a full re-run of the handler) before deciding the fix shape.
- [x] **SHIPPED 3b01a8f7 (U-259) — U-223 — QBO auth read hardening (P3s booked out of U-215, both verified NON-regressions).** (a) `QboAuthRepository.read_all()` decrypts `IdToken`/`AccessToken`/`RefreshToken`, so a Fernet key mismatch raises before `realm_id` — the only field the enqueue path needs. Low value on its own (a key mismatch equally breaks the worker's push, so the row would just dead-letter) but a `realm_id`-only read would decouple them. (b) `ReadQboAuths` has **no `ORDER BY`**, so `read_all()[0]` is non-deterministic if a second auth row ever exists. Deliberately left matching `ensure_valid_token()`'s own choice — making the enqueue path pick *differently* would diverge the enqueue realm from the refresh realm, which is worse than a shared nondeterminism. Fix is the `ORDER BY` in the sproc, not in Python.

- [x] **SHIPPED c1c79e40 2026-08-17 — FOLDED INTO U-246 (u246-reconciliation-issue-lifecycle) — U-227 — move the drift-type registry out of the test suite into production (found by U-218a's Pass 2).** `tests/test_qbo_reconciliation_recorder.py::_KNOWN_DRIFT_TYPES` is a 12-entry frozenset of every legal drift type in the system — a real domain registry that exists only in `tests/`. - [x] **U-228 — bound the unbounded QBO watermark hold — DONE 2026-08-12.** Booked as a latent VendorCredit risk; **went live during the build** — `bill` had been held at 19:15 UTC for 90+ min on 11 Walker Lumber "Service Charge" `AccountBasedExpenseLineDetail` bills (qbo_ids 75071-75083) when this unit started. Map re-verified rather than trusted: a real, transaction-isolated execution of `BillBillConnector.sync_from_qbo_bill` against the live prod rows (single pyodbc connection, never committed, explicit `rollback()` at the end — zero writes) projected all 11 cleanly under correct `system_authz()` context, so **the connector logic has no bug** for this data. Root cause is almost certainly the pre-existing, already-documented P0 two sections below (invoice-sync duplication ratchet): `POST /api/v1/admin/sync/qbo/{entity}` has no `sp_getapplock`, and the scheduler's client-side `REQUEST_TIMEOUT_SECONDS=60` doesn't cancel the server-side `asyncio.to_thread` work on timeout — so a run re-processing an ever-growing held window can still be executing when the next 15-min tick fires, and two overlapping runs racing to `create_mapping()` for the same qbo_id would hit real `UNIQUE` constraints on both `qbo.BillBill` and `qbo.BillLineItemBillLine`. **Not fixed here** (a concurrency-control change to a live write-path endpoint deserves its own adversarial pass, mirroring why U-221 split out of U-220) — booked as follow-up (a) below, tied to the existing P0 entry.
  **Shipped:** `WatermarkRun.commit()` (`scripts/sync_helper.py`) now bounds any hold **time-based**, not tick-counted — no schema change, reuses `Sync.ModifiedDatetime` (falling back to `CreatedDatetime`), which already freezes at exactly the last successful advance since a hold-commit was always a pure no-op. Anchored against `query_start` (already captured once at `open()`) rather than a fresh `datetime.now()` read. Default `QBO_WATERMARK_HOLD_BOUND_SECONDS=7200` (2h) — sized to let a genuinely transient hold self-clear (purchase held ~47min the same day and did) while bounding worst-case silent-loss exposure. Below the bound: **byte-identical** to pre-unit behavior (pure hold, zero writes). At/past the bound: writes one **critical** `qbo.ReconciliationIssue` per blocking id, then force-advances exactly like a clean success (respects `end_date`). `staging_failed_ids` (real QBO ids) are recorded at face value; `projection_failed_ids` (internal `qbo.<Entity>` staging PKs — a pre-existing, documented id-keyspace distinction, see `feedback_qbo_dbo_id_keyspaces.md`) are resolved back to the real QBO id via a new `_QBO_SYNC_ENTITY_META` registry's `read_by_id(id).qbo_id` (works for 10 of 11 entities; `reimburse_charge`'s repo has no `read_by_id`, and any lookup failure, fall back to a details string that's honest about being an unresolved staging id rather than silently mislabeling it). `BillBillConnector.sync_from_qbo_bill`'s header `create_mapping()` guard widened to also catch `DatabaseConstraintError` (a sibling of `ValueError`, not a subclass, that a concurrent-race INSERT can raise) — proven safe by the hunt, since `dbo.Bill`'s own `UQ_Bill_VendorId_BillNumber_BillDate` blocks the duplicate-header scenario before that except clause is ever reached. The identical widening was also tried on `BillLineItemConnector`'s line-level `create_mapping()` and **reverted** after the hunt proved it harmful: `dbo.BillLineItem` has no uniqueness constraint of any kind, so silently swallowing that race would convert a self-healing failure (rollback on the new-bill CREATE path, watermark hold on the existing-bill UPDATE path) into a permanent, undetectable duplicate line item — left as `except ValueError` only, with a comment recording why, and a mutation-tested regression test (`tests/test_qbo_bill_line_item_mapping_exceptions.py`) pinning that a `DatabaseConstraintError` there must propagate.
  **Two Pass-1 hunts (Workflow, refute-by-default).** First: 3 lenses, 21 agents, 0 dead/errored — 5 confirmed (the two above; an id-keyspace bug in the new issue-writer conflating `projection_failed_ids`/`staging_failed_ids`; zero test coverage on the whole new branch; a cosmetic lowercase `EntityType`) + 1 correctly **refuted** (a mirror finding on the header connector — three independent skeptics traced the exact same race through `dbo.Bill`'s unique index and showed the loser fails at `bill_service.create()`, never reaching the widened except at all). One workflow-authoring bug on my own side (a mis-nested `pipeline()` call flattened per-lens results, crashing the aggregation JS after all 21 agents had already completed) — caught by the failed task-notification, fixed, and resumed from cache via `resumeFromRunId` at zero additional agent spend. Fix round re-hunted 2 lenses/14 agents scoped to just the fix diff: 4 more confirmed, all P2/P3 (still-zero-coverage on the *new* id-split logic — closed with 13 new tests; a log line that kept mislabeling the same two id lists it had just fixed in the DB write; a one-character comment typo; a genuine "missed opportunity" — 10 of 11 entities' staging repos already exposed a cheap `read_by_id().qbo_id` path that hadn't been used — built out into the registry above rather than deferred). One transient hallucination (two verify agents independently reported seeing injected file-write code in `sync_helper.py`) directly re-verified against the real file (byte count, md5, grep for `scratchpad`/`exec`/`eval`/`subprocess`) and confirmed **not present** — a model artifact, not a real injection, and the agents correctly refused to act on it either way.
  **Hermetic mutation matrix, 4/4 caught** (`__pycache__` cleared between every mutation, confirmed green-on-unmutated first): `and`→`or` polarity (the exact U-217-class inverted-default shape), whole-block deletion, neutered `_record_bound_forced_advance`, and an off-by-one (`>=`→`>`) that **initially survived** — the existing tests only exercised holds orders of magnitude past the bound, never the exact boundary — closed by adding boundary-precise tests (`held == bound` and `held == bound - 1s`), then re-verified caught.
  **Pass 2 (4 angles):** merged two parallel entity-keyed dicts (`_QBO_SYNC_ENTITY_TYPE_LABELS` + `_QBO_STAGING_REPO_MODULES`) into one `_QBO_SYNC_ENTITY_META` registry, mirroring `integrations/intuit/qbo/base/field_ownership.py`'s `_REGISTRY` precedent (two independent lenses converged on this); switched the registry's staging-repo lookup from a stringly-typed `importlib.import_module` dispatch to plain static top-level imports (no circular-import risk — verified empirically, not just by reading); reused the existing `integrations/sync/persistence/repo.py::_parse_sync_last_sync` instead of a self-acknowledged duplicate; converted a test helper's awkward 4-item manual patcher list into a proper `@contextlib.contextmanager`; collapsed a duplicated 7-kwarg `record_mapping_issue(...)` call shape into one closure. **Declined:** factoring `_watermark_hold_bound_seconds`/`_watermark_overlap_seconds`'s shared env-int-parsing shape into a helper — only 2 call sites, both already in this one file, rule-of-three not met.
  **Verify:** **1620 pytest green** (1605 baseline, **+15**: `tests/test_qbo_watermark_runner.py` +13, `tests/test_qbo_reconciliation_recorder.py` guard extended, new `tests/test_qbo_bill_line_item_mapping_exceptions.py` +2), `py_compile` clean, confirmed no circular import from the Pass-2 static-import switch. **No SQL step** — reuses existing `dbo.Sync` columns, no migration. Container-only deploy.
  ⚠️ **Owed and booked, not done here:** (a) the missing-`sp_getapplock` root cause (tie to the P0 two sections below — do not open a fresh unit); (b) the same `except ValueError` → also catch `DatabaseConstraintError` widening, evaluated per-table for a uniqueness constraint before applying, across the ~13 other `create_mapping()` call sites in the QBO connector fleet (customer, item/cost_code, item/sub_cost_code, term, invoice, invoice_line_item, vendor, attachable, customer/project) — U-228 only did bill's header (safe) and line (evaluated, reverted); (c) `CLAUDE.md` needs `QBO_WATERMARK_HOLD_BOUND_SECONDS` added to its App Service env-var list — blocked by the same parallel-session foreign WIP as U-217/U-220; (d) a per-entity `QBO_WATERMARK_HOLD_BOUND_SECONDS_<ENTITY>` override, mirroring U-217 follow-up (e)'s not-yet-built per-entity overlap idea — not needed today since the bound is time-based (scales naturally across cadences) but worth having if a very-slow entity ever needs a different tolerance than 2h; (e) once deployed, watch whether the currently-stuck `bill` watermark self-clears (transient-overlap theory) or force-advances cleanly at the 2h mark with a critical issue recorded for the 11 Walker Lumber bills — the first live exercise of this code path.
- [x] **SHIPPED 1139178e 2026-08-17 (−81% reads, measured; SubCostCode index SQL pending apply; collation lookup + JOIN deliberately reverted after real risk found in live data) — U-229 — run-scoped item-ref cache for the VC line resolver (efficiency, found by U-218a's Pass 2).** Only **83 distinct** item refs across 429 VendorCredit lines. `VendorCreditBillCreditConnector` is built once per run, but `_sync_line_items` constructs a fresh `VendorCreditLineItemConnector()` **per credit**, so an instance-level memo buys ~nothing (1.02 lines/credit). Hoisting the line connector to a run-scoped attribute plus a `{item_ref: scc_id}` dict cuts the resolver from 429×3 = 1,287 reads to 83×3 = 249 on a full re-pull (**−81%**). Precedent: `invoice/connector/invoice/business/service.py::_project_cache`. Cheaper still with SQL (out of U-218a's pure-Python scope): make `ReadItemSubCostCodeByQboItemId` `INNER JOIN dbo.SubCostCode` so the existence probe costs **zero** extra round-trips. Also booked here: `read_by_number_and_cost_code_id` for SubCostCode mirroring the `(Number, CostCodeId)` predicate already canonical in `UpsertSubCostCode`'s MERGE `ON` clause (U-218a filters in Python instead, which measured cost≈0 because **neither `Number` nor `CostCodeId` is indexed** — so `CREATE INDEX IX_SubCostCode_CostCodeId ON dbo.SubCostCode(CostCodeId) INCLUDE (Number)` is the real hygiene win, helping that sproc's other callers too). NB SQL's default collation is case/accent-insensitive while the Python `==` filter is not, so the two "same number" answers can disagree.
- [x] **U-216 — QBO hygiene + security (P1-13/14) — DONE 2026-08-11.** Two independent items, **no SQL step and no deploy of its own** (see below). (b) Deleted the trailing ad-hoc batch from `qbo.bill.sql` (a diagnostic `SELECT` + a bare `UPDATE bli SET bli.[IsBillable]` back-coded from `qbo.BillLine.BillableStatus`) and the bare `SELECT COUNT(Id)` tail from `qbo.attachable.sql`. **Map answered the deploy question first: this needs NO prod apply** — the file is inert until an operator runs `run_sql.py`, prod already ran the backfill, and lines 1-840 are **byte-identical to HEAD** (sha256 verified, all 13 sprocs untouched), so applying would be strictly *more* risk (the U-037 stale-base class) for zero gain. **Deleting it also leaves no gap:** `BillableStatus` ownership flows local→QBO (the connector *derives* it from `is_billable`, `connector/bill/business/service.py:928-947`) and no pull-side writer of `dbo.BillLineItem.IsBillable` exists — the tail was pushing the flag *backwards*. Rule-of-three sweep: `qbo.bill.sql` was the **only** file repo-wide with a bare mutating tail (5 entity files flagged by a first pass are false positives — DML inside a proc body whose last batch lacks a trailing `GO`). (c) `QboClient.to_dict()` was a plain `asdict()` returning the **decrypted** Intuit OAuth secret through five routes to any `QBO_SYNC can_read` holder (incl. a read-only `UserModule` grant); it now returns `{app, client_id, client_secret_set: bool}`. **`router.py` needed no change** — all five handlers already serialize via `to_dict()`, and the OAuth callers (`auth/external/client.py:88,191,289`) read the dataclass **attribute**, so the serializer was the whole leak surface; a swept search of all five repos found **zero** consumers of `client_secret` from those endpoints. Chose key-omission over the spec's `"********"` mask because a mask round-trips — a GET→edit→PUT would write the literal mask as the secret; omission fails loud on pydantic instead. **The unit's own guard test was its biggest finding:** v1 classified each `GO`-batch by its FIRST keyword, and the deleted tail begins with the diagnostic `SELECT` — so it would **not** have caught the defect it exists to prevent (proven by running its own parser against `git show HEAD:`). An inactive unmapped item matched a LIVE `CostCode`/`SubCostCode` by number, overwrote its name/description (sub_cost_code also **re-parented** it via the dead item's `cost_code_id`), and `create_mapping`'s failure was swallowed to a warning so the run reported SUCCESS. Vendor/customer/project adopt by NAME, which carries the suffix and safely misses — item was uniquely exposed. Fixed at altitude: the guard covers the whole no-mapping region, not just create. (2) **Pass 2 caught a wrong premise in the unit's own Gate-1 brief**: `Vendor` was the ONE entity already protected (`_apply_vendor_fields_and_sync` routes through `preserve_human_edited_name`), so the approved "let QBO's rename through" decision had been taken on the only entity where it could not happen, while `Customer`/`Project`/`CostCode`/`SubCostCode`/`PaymentTerm` — never presented — wrote the `" (deleted)"` name unconditionally. Re-decided: all five now route through the same helper on the **mapped** branch only (the adopt branch is left alone — the guard already blocks inactive there, and changing it would alter ACTIVE-record behaviour). Zero live impact today; forward-looking. Also added an env kill switch `QBO_VENDORCREDIT_SYNC_ATTACHMENTS` — the VC attachment path had never executed in prod and BOTH recurring callers take the `True` default, so `--skip-attachments` could not reach them. Pass 2 also collapsed six copy-pasted guards into one helper (their shared comment was factually wrong in 4 of its 6 homes) and moved the five reference clients onto the `where_clauses` pattern bill/purchase already use — **all 10 emitted query strings proven byte-identical** by executing both versions against a capturing stub. **Mutation matrix 28 caught / 0 gaps / 1 equivalent mutant**, including the guard moved back below the adopt lookup (the P1 shape) in all four adopt-path connectors. **1433 pytest green (+49** over the 1384 baseline at `af15964`**)**. Since `BillCreditService.delete_by_public_id` destroys line items + attachments + blobs FIRST and never touches the mapping, every rollback would have left a full-value BillCredit with **zero lines** and no mapping — strictly worse than the zombie it prevents, and the next pull would mint a duplicate credit. Fixed by deleting the mapping first (also fixes the identical latent bug in the Purchase/Expense sibling; `qbo.BillBill` has no FK so Bill is unaffected) plus an `on_header_delete_failed` callback recording a critical issue. Also fixed: the heal branch **bypassed the U-219 deactivation guard** (re-opening a confirmed P1); `SubCostCode` was matched by **globally unscoped** `read_by_number` (`fetchone()`, one arbitrary row) while `UpsertSubCostCode` keys on `(Number, CostCodeId)` — so a same-number row under another parent could be **re-parented**, fixed with a parent-scoped enumeration via the already-live `ReadSubCostCodeByCostCodeId`; and the VC resolver handed back a **stale `sub_cost_code_id`** from a dangling mapping (FK 547 → `RuntimeError` → permanent watermark hold).
    **Two findings were introduced BY the fix round** (the U-217 pattern, again): the header-failure callback fired even when the *mapping* delete had failed — and because the FK is NO_ACTION a failed mapping delete **guarantees** the header delete fails, so it deterministically wrote a false "mapping was deleted, needs manual resolution" critical issue for a state that self-heals on the next pull; and the deactivation guard's message asserted "has no local mapping" from inside `if mapping:`.

Literals shortened and the guard widened to raw repo writers; it now also cross-checks its widths against the `.sql` schema so a manually-measured constant became a checked invariant.
