## 🔴 P0 BUG — invoice-sync duplication ratchet: overlapping ticks + ambiguous-fingerprint fallthrough (2026-08-07, found on TB3-20)
- [x] **CLOSED 2026-08-18 — reconciliation backlog fully triaged: 19,429 open → 16 open + 46 acknowledged.** U-265 (`0e463db5`, `fa4cad20`) built the two tooling gaps U-249 documented, then they were used: bulk-acknowledge on the 46 real `qbo_voided` rows (26 Bill + 20 Expense) and `--keep-newest-per-group` dedup on the recurring summary rows (51 Bill + 29 Invoice-draw + 21 Expense + 17 BillCredit = 118 resolved).
  - **The remaining 16 are correct, not residue:** 4 live summary rows (the newest of each group, deliberately retained as active signal) + the 12 known-bogus `watermark_hold_bound_exceeded` fixture rows.
  - **The grouping key is the safety property, and `QboId` is why.** Key is `(RealmId, DriftType, EntityType, QboId, EntityPublicId, Severity, Action)`, newest = `MAX(CreatedDatetime)` tie-broken by `MAX(Id)`. Summary rows carry NULL `QboId` with a distinct `ReconcileRunId` each; every `qbo_voided` row carries its own `QboId`. So keep-newest is a **provable no-op** on per-entity drift — verified live: `qbo_voided`/Bill 26 matched → 26 survivors → 0 resolved. **Without `QboId` it would have silently resolved 25 of 26 real voided bills.**
  - ⚠️ **`--entity-type` alone does NOT protect the 12 fixture rows** — SQL Server's default collation is case-insensitive and 10 of them store `EntityType='bill'`, so `--entity-type Bill` matches all 12. **Scope by `--drift-type`**; `--severity`/`--action` are a second independent guard. Encoded as a passing test.
  - Two P3s found in review and fixed before use: `--created-before-days 0` resolved to "now" (matching the whole table) while still satisfying the blast-radius guard; and the keep-newest empty-preview message asserted "the filter matched something", which is exactly wrong for a mistyped filter — it now re-previews without keep-newest to tell the two cases apart.

- [x] **(superseded) EXECUTED 2026-08-18 — qbo.ReconciliationIssue bulk resolve: 19,429 → 179 open.** Ran U-249's documented policy item (a) via `manage_qbo_reconciliation_issues.py bulk-resolve --drift-type qbo_missing_locally --entity-type Bill --created-before-date 2026-06-21 --status open`, dry-run first, in four capped batches (5000/5000/5000/4250). Counts re-verified as exactly matching U-249's measurement (19,250) before any mutation. U-246's lifecycle sprocs had gone live in the 2026-08-17 deploy, which is what unblocked it.
  - **The 179 remainder is characterised, and the 12 rows marked `critical` are NOT real signal** — all dated 2026-08-12 with `qbo_id=vc-1` / `staging-only`, one claiming "held for 223 days". These are the ~12 known-bogus fixture rows U-246 flagged from earlier testing. Clear them when convenient; do not treat them as a live watermark-hold incident.
  - Genuinely actionable remainder: ~89 low-severity summary-dedup rows (Bill 52 / Expense 22 / BillCredit 18, keep-newest) and 46 `qbo_voided` (Bill 26 / Expense 20) needing acknowledgement, plus 29 `invoice_draw_mismatch`. **Both are blocked on tooling gaps U-249 documented:** there is no bulk-resolve filter for a Severity/Action combination, and no bulk-acknowledge sproc (46 single-row commands otherwise). Build those before the next sweep.

- [x] **EXECUTED 2026-08-18 — invoice-line repair complete: 186 unmapped Manual rows → 5.** Ran U-264's tooling (`scripts/repair_invoice_line_duplicates.py`, 106a9eab) against a reviewed, pinned manifest with `--expected-count 181`: **181 deleted, 0 skipped**, row count 31,013 → 30,832 (exactly −181). The 5 held-back rows (all `no_sibling`) remain intact: KA2-06 "Hardscapes REFUND", TB3-17-2 ×2, WVA-17 "13%", HP2-12-2 "Stormwater".
  - **Why it was safe to run, after being refused on 2026-08-18 morning:** the predicate is a QBO ground-truth cross-check, not "unmapped = delete" — a row is repairable only when every `qbo.InvoiceLine` sharing its fingerprint is already covered by an **identity-verified** mapped sibling (the sibling's *mapped QBO line* must share the fingerprint, not just its local Description/Amount). Invoice push is disabled, so most unmapped ILI rows are unmapped BY DESIGN; that naive predicate would have returned far more than 186 and deleted real invoice lines.
  - **The 185→186 delta was answered better than by reconciling it:** every one of the 186 rows predates the U-247 fix commit (`797320a0`), proving the ratchet is stopped. Seven invoices self-healed as later pulls mapped previously-unmapped duplicates (17 → 10 invoices with activity).
  - **Invoice totals were never inflated** — `Invoice.TotalAmount` already tracked the *mapped* line sum, so the repair brought line data INTO agreement with totals rather than changing any client-facing figure. OL-14's line-sum discrepancy went from **$601K → $2.8K**.

- [ ] **Stored invoice totals sit consistently ABOVE their line sums — small, pre-existing, unexplained (found 2026-08-18 during the U-264 repair verification).** After the repair, all six affected invoices show a negative gap: OL-14 −2,820.32 · OHR2-GUEST-12-3 −630.00 · HP-23.02 −1,733.76 · HP2-08-2 −387.83 · WVA-18 −6,148.50 · TB3-19 −3,708.13. The consistent sign across six invoices makes this a pattern, not rounding noise. **Not caused by the repair** — it only removed provably-redundant rows and moved every invoice much closer to agreement. Candidates: the held-back rows, lines legitimately excluded from the sum, or a `Price`-vs-`TotalAmount` derivation difference. Read-only investigation first; do NOT adjust totals ad hoc.

  - ⚠️ **The count MOVED: 185 → 186** between U-247's measurement and 2026-08-18. U-247's fix is live and all 5 QBO watermarks are healthy, so the ratchet should be stopped and the likeliest cause is a legitimately-created Manual line — but that must be EXPLAINED before anything is deleted.

U-264 supplies the missing tooling (dry-run-by-default, per-invoice before/after, hard refusal on a count mismatch) and only then runs it. Original measurement: 185 unmapped `Manual` InvoiceLineItem rows across 17 invoices, 184 excess, OL-14 worst at 126.

- [x] **INVESTIGATED AND CLOSED AS NOT-A-FIX 2026-08-17 — do not re-open without new data: the SharePoint/Box filename "collision" on multi-line bills.** `_build_module_filename` (`entities/bill/business/service.py:2560-2618`, plus a near-duplicate at :884-906) derives the name from BILL-level fields only — the sole attachment-derived input is the file extension — so several attachments on one bill resolve to one name. Both SharePoint AND Box use this builder (Box via `_enqueue_box_uploads` :1787), failing differently: SharePoint silently replaces, Box 409s and re-versions. **Measured before acting, and the measurement killed the fix:** 3,273 bills (99.5%) carry exactly ONE distinct attachment, and 190 (attachment, bill) pairs span >1 line item — i.e. the normal shape is one support file referenced by every line, already deduped at enqueue on `(project_id, attachment_id)` and uploaded once. Only 18 bills have >1 line AND >1 distinct attachment; 2 of those span projects (different folders, cannot collide), leaving 16. Of those 16: **7 are byte-identical duplicates** (same `FileHash` — bill 17531 stores the same PDF four times), where one shared name is CORRECT and nothing is lost; **3 are revisions** (`462953.pdf` vs `462953 (1).pdf`; same name, new bytes) where collapsing to one name is the DESIRED behaviour; only **6 are genuinely distinct documents**, almost all contract-labor bills. Adding a filename discriminator would churn the naming convention across 3,273 bills to serve 6, and would actively defeat the 10 cases where sharing a name is right. Two real findings were extracted from this instead — see the next two entries.

- [ ] **Duplicate `Attachment` rows for byte-identical content (measured 2026-08-17, 7 bills).** The "one file, persisted once, referenced by many line items" invariant is violated on a handful of bills: same `FileHash`, same `FileSize`, multiple `dbo.Attachment` rows. Bill **17531** holds the same PDF **4×** (hash `58fc1b304888`, 113,408B); **17474** likewise (`96df9e857f36`, 4×); **17262 / 17178** 2× each; and **17337 / 17359 / 17360** each pair a UUID-named row with a human-named row of byte-identical content — the same document bridged twice by different paths. `EmailAttachmentBridgeService` already hash-dedups, so these either predate that guard or reach persistence by a path that bypasses it. Data hygiene, not document loss — nothing is lost, the same bytes are just stored several times and uploaded to the same destination name. Find the bypassing path, then decide whether to dedupe historically. Read-only reproduction: group `dbo.Attachment` by `FileHash` within a bill via `BillLineItemAttachment`.

- [ ] **U-261 — Attachment hash-dedup bypass + BillCredit attachment backfill feasibility (INVESTIGATED 2026-08-18; do NOT build here).** ⚠️ **This U-261 is intentionally reused** — `build.one.team/BOARD.md` carries a separate unrelated U-261 row for the WatermarkRun move; this entry is the OTHER U-261 (Chris's explicit call 2026-08-18). Read-only scripts: `scripts/analyze_attachment_duplicates.py` (live bill-scoped duplicate groups + timing/filename-shape heuristics) and `scripts/analyze_billcredit_attachment_backfill.py` (Part A always; Part B gated `--check-qbo`). **(1) Duplicate-Attachment bypass — three gaps, one correct prior art.** `EmailAttachmentBridgeService.bridge()` (`entities/email_message/business/service.py:1348-1354`) and `AttachableAttachmentConnector.sync_from_qbo_attachable()` (`integrations/intuit/qbo/attachable/connector/attachment/business/service.py:177-180`) both call `AttachmentService.read_by_hash` and reuse the existing row. Three intake paths bypass it entirely: **`POST /upload/bill-line-item-attachment`** (`entities/attachment/api/router.py:409-444`) computes `file_hash` but never calls `read_by_hash` — total bypass: uploading the same PDF once per line item (N separate calls, seconds-to-minutes apart per script 1's live run — all 8 groups measure `timing=spread`, none `burst`, so this is N distinct upload actions, not one drag-N-files gesture) mints N Attachment rows; **`POST /upload/attachment`** (`entities/attachment/api/router.py:318-326`) calls `read_by_hash` but the 409 raise is commented out, so the dead guard still uploads a fresh blob and `create()`s a duplicate; **SharePoint bill-folder auto-intake** (`entities/bill/business/folder_processor.py:525-541` in `process_single_item`, `:678-695` in `_process_single_file`) hashes with `hashlib.sha256` and `attachment_service.create()` with no `read_by_hash` check — UUID blob names (`bills/{uuid}.pdf` at :526/:679) vs human filenames on the upload endpoints explain the uuid-shaped/human-shaped pairs script 1 surfaces — a proxy signal only, not proof of the exact endpoint (confounded in principle by any other UUID-named-blob path, e.g. contract_labor's generated bill PDF at `entities/contract_labor/business/bill_service.py:635`, though that path always sets `file_hash=None` so it can never appear in script 1's output). **Forward-fix (propose only):** wire `read_by_hash` into all three gaps, returning/reusing the canonical row exactly like the email bridge + QBO attachable connector; on `/upload/attachment` uncomment or replace with an explicit reuse response (409 or return-existing — product choice). **Historical dedupe (separate authorized unit):** for each group script 1 finds, pick the earliest `CreatedDatetime` Attachment as canonical, repoint all `BillLineItemAttachment` rows from redundant Attachment Ids to it, then delete the excess Attachment rows + Azure blobs (mirror `BillCreditService.delete_by_public_id`'s blob cleanup pattern). Dry-run-by-default, per-bill before/after, hard refusal on FK/link count mismatch — do NOT fold into the forward-fix unit. **(2) BillCredit attachment backfill — feasible, not QBO-time-bounded.** Part A joins `dbo.BillCredit` ← `qbo.VendorCreditBillCredit` ← `qbo.VendorCredit` and treats "no attachment" as zero `dbo.BillCreditLineItemAttachment` rows reachable via `BillCreditLineItem` (same join family `scripts/sync_qbo_vendorcredit.py::_link_attachments_to_bill_credit_line_items` uses). U-219 follow-up (e)'s ~443 baseline is the right order of magnitude; script 2 re-measures live and compares delta. The gap is **not** QBO-side time filtering — only this codebase's own incremental watermark ever excluded pre-U-219 VendorCredits; once a BillCredit exists locally, attachments only arrive when the VC re-enters the watermark window (or via manual/QBO-side upload). Part B (`--check-qbo`) runs **one** `QboAttachableClient.query_all_attachables()` (`integrations/intuit/qbo/attachable/external/client.py:97`) and filters `AttachableRef` to `entity_ref_type=VendorCredit` + Part-A `qbo.VendorCredit.QboId` values — distinguishes "still retrievable in QBO" vs "legitimately never had one / since removed". **Forward-fix (propose only, own authorized unit):** a dry-run-by-default backfill script calling `QboAttachableService.sync_attachables_for_vendor_credit(realm_id, qbo_id, sync_to_modules=True)` per known `qbo.VendorCredit.QboId` from Part A, **bypassing** the full VC resync/watermark (targeted per-id, same as `scripts/backfill_qbo_bills.py`'s per-`--qbo-id` shape). Reuse `scripts/sync_qbo_vendorcredit.py`'s `_link_attachments_to_bill_credit_line_items` after each sync. Pace `BATCH_SIZE=10` / `BATCH_DELAY=0.5` (same convention as U-219 follow-up (e) and sibling backfill scripts). Per-item failure isolation — one bad VC must not abort the batch. Gate on `ALLOW_QBO_WRITES` + explicit `--apply`; metered cost ≈ Part-A count × (1 attachable list amortized + N per-entity downloads when apply runs) — size with a `--limit` validation batch first. JSON snapshots land under `scripts/_reports/billcredit_attachment_backfill_*`.

- [ ] **Should a contract-labor bill carry several distinct supporting documents? (measured 2026-08-17, 6 bills).** The only genuinely-multi-document bills in the corpus are almost all contract labor — e.g. bill **17037** (`TB3 - Emilson O. Cordova Tercero - … - We i…` / `… - Clea…` / a third) and **17450** (`… - Trim` / `… - Inst`). These are the sole cases where a per-attachment filename discriminator would be justified. Product question before engineering: is one CL bill with N documents the intended shape, or should those be N bills? If the shape is intended, the discriminator must be a PURE FUNCTION of the attachment (not a counter) — Box's 409→re-version depends on a re-push producing the SAME name, so an ordering-derived suffix would mint duplicates instead of versions.

Regression cover: `tests/test_ms_outbox_coalesce_discrimination.py::test_coalesce_payload_refresh_failure_creates_new_row`. This entry was still open when U-267 was dispatched to (re-)fix it 2026-08-18; Gate-1 caught the duplication via `git log` before any code — see BOARD.md.

- [x] ~~LIVE DOCUMENT-LOSS BUG — surfaced by U-250's triage 2026-08-17, tracked in full as U-221 follow-up (a) below~~ — **DONE, SHIPPED U-251 (`336f3457`) 2026-08-17, DEPLOYED.** Duplicate of the fuller U-221-follow-up-(a) entry above, which already carries the strikethrough/SHIPPED note — this standalone copy was never removed when U-251 landed, and its "own unit, urgent" framing is exactly what caused U-267 to be dispatched 2026-08-18 to re-fix an already-deployed bug (caught at Gate-1 before any code; see BOARD.md).

- [x] **SHIPPED 797320a0 (U-247), 2026-08-17 — MINTING STOPPED, PROD REPAIR STILL OWED (see below).** All four compounding defects fixed: per-entity `qbo_app_lock` on `POST /sync/qbo/{entity}` (held inside the same offloaded thread, so a cancelled request cannot release it while the orphaned thread keeps mutating); deterministic lowest-id-unclaimed fingerprint adoption; an explicit `_caches_preloaded` flag replacing 7 wrong `if self._line_item_cache:` truthiness checks (the in-process cache can be non-empty but INCOMPLETE mid-pass for a non-preloaded caller — the outbox worker's invoice-refresh path); and all three create-then-map rollback sites broadened from `ValueError` to also catch the `DatabaseConstraintError` the real unique-constraint race actually raises. 5 Codex rounds + an independent 5-lens hunt (24 findings, 18 confirmed) converged on the same defects. 1980 pytest green, no SQL. — A large QBO invoice can be re-projected forever, minting duplicate `dbo.InvoiceLineItem` rows on every pass.** Found running TB3-20 (qbo invoice 988, 236 lines): dbo held **433 ILI rows summing $430,174.29** against a real QBO invoice of **236 lines / $118,757.68**, still growing after 7.5 hours. 299 rows were pure duplicates (every one matched a mapped row's `(amount, description)`; zero unique content) and 102 QBO lines had no dbo row at all. Two defects compound:
  - **No applock on the sync endpoint.** `sync_qbo_invoice` fires every 15 min (`0 4,19,34,49 * * * *`, `build.one.scheduler/function_app.py:133`) and `POST /api/v1/admin/sync/qbo/{entity}` (`shared/api/admin.py:395`) takes no `sp_getapplock`. A 236-line invoice projects slower than the tick interval, so runs overlap and concurrently project the same invoice. (Contrast the outbox drains, which DO applock.)
  - **`_find_and_match_manual_by_fingerprint` returns `None` on *ambiguous* matches** (`integrations/intuit/qbo/invoice/connector/invoice_line_item/business/service.py`). Its own docstring: "Returns None on zero or ambiguous matches." So the moment two unmapped ILIs share `(description, amount)`, adoption can never succeed again and the connector falls through to `create()` — **the first duplicate guarantees a duplicate on every subsequent pass.** Self-amplifying, never self-heals.
  - **Create-then-map is non-atomic**: the ILI is created and committed, then `create_mapping` failures are swallowed (`except ValueError: logger.warning`), stranding the row as an unmapped phantom.
  - **It also degrades O(n²)**: the fingerprint helper calls `read_by_invoice_id()` (ALL rows on the invoice) per line, so as duplicates accumulate each pass gets slower — observed decaying from 26 rows/10min to ~1 line per 2.5 min at 439 rows.
  - **Blast radius**: any invoice large enough to outlast a 15-min tick. Also blocks the realm — the `invoice` watermark can't advance while the sync never completes (TB3-20 froze it for ~8h; no invoice pulled realm-wide in that window).
  - **Fixes, in order:** (1) `sp_getapplock` on `/sync/qbo/{entity}` keyed per entity (single-writer, matching the drain pattern); (2) make the fingerprint deterministic under ambiguity — order candidates by id and take the first unclaimed rather than bailing, or key on `qbo.InvoiceLine.LineNum` via the mapping table; (3) wrap ILI-create + mapping-create in one transaction so a mapping failure can't strand a row; (4) hoist the `read_by_invoice_id` call out of the per-line loop (pass the caller's cached set — the connector already accepts `line_item_cache`).
  - **Manual recovery used (safe, verified):** delete unmapped rows (`ili.Id NOT IN (SELECT InvoiceLineItemId FROM qbo.InvoiceLineItemInvoiceLine map JOIN qbo.InvoiceLine il ON il.Id = map.QboInvoiceLineId WHERE il.QboInvoiceId = ?)`) — the mapped set is authoritative. Detection query for other invoices: compare `COUNT(dbo.InvoiceLineItem)` vs `COUNT(qbo.InvoiceLine)` per mapped invoice. **A fleet-wide sweep has NOT been run** — the daily `invoice_draw_mismatch` reconcile issue already names 22 drifting invoices (incl. TB3-17-2 `dbo=97 qbo=95`), which is the natural starting list.
  - **Second live occurrence, 2026-08-11/12 (TB3-20 again, small scale this time):** a single new line (one QBO Bill added to the invoice, $119,460.00 "Cabinets") got duplicated exactly twice — `dbo.InvoiceLineItem` 33180 (2026-08-11 15:54:45 UTC) and 33181 (15:57:36 UTC), both `SourceType='Manual'`, both unlinked. Same signature as the 2026-08-07 incident (ambiguous-fingerprint fallthrough on two identical-content `Manual` rows) but caught in minutes instead of hours because the invoice is small. Confirms the bug isn't retired by invoice size — any add during an overlapping-tick window reproduces it. Recovered manually: kept 33180, deleted 33181, fingerprint-linked 33180 to its source `BillLineItem` (24080 / Bill 74280, Ash Cabinetry LLC) so a later tick can't re-duplicate it. No fix applied — still the same four-part fix above.

## BUG — the worksheet-reconcile header detector can't parse TB3's `DETAIL` tab, disabling reconcile + the U-193 delta path for that project (2026-08-07)
- [ ] **`detect_header_and_columns` raises `400 Worksheet missing columns: billable` on project 73 (TB3)**, so everything routed through `worksheet_reconcile.reconcile()` is unusable there: `GET /invoice/{id}/reconcile`, the U-178 Phase-1 draw audit, and `InvoiceDrawDeltaService.propose_removals` / `apply_removals` (U-193). Hit while propagating a line removal on TB3-20 — the delta path aborted before proposing anything.
  - **Why it's project-specific:** `_detect_header_col_map` scans for a row with ≥3 `_KNOWN_HEADERS` hits, then requires `date`, `draw_request_date`, `billable`, `description`. TB3's tab is named `DETAIL` (singular — see SESSION_NOTES, along with HRL/ML's `Tracking Budget DETAILS`) and its header row doesn't expose a label mapping to `billable`, so the required-column check fails even though the sheet is structurally fine and the data is in the standard columns (H/I/J/K/L/N/Z).
  - **Not a data problem — the col-Z primitives work fine on this sheet.** `sync_to_excel_workbook` (write-time dedup + insert), `unstamp_draw_from_excel` (H clear), and the Box `OP_CLEAR_DRAW_REQUEST` mirror all key strictly on column Z and ran correctly on TB3 in the same session. Only the *header-dependent discovery* layer breaks. That asymmetry is the workaround: when the caller already knows the col-Z keys, call the primitives directly (Box enqueue first, then SharePoint inline — the ordering `apply_removals` uses so SP is never cleared while Box stays stale).
  - **Fix options:** (a) fall back to fixed column positions when header detection fails but the sheet matches the known A..Z layout; (b) widen `_KNOWN_HEADERS` with TB3's actual labels; (c) make `billable` optional when column N is present positionally. Worth auditing which of the 27 mapped workbooks parse — a header-detection census across all of them would show whether TB3 is alone or the reconcile surface is quietly dark for several projects.
- [ ] **Playbook Delta section is stale on the Box side.** It says Box has "no row-delete/cell primitive, so its side is a manual openpyxl fill-in-place edit under the production lock lifecycle." U-193 shipped `OP_CLEAR_DRAW_REQUEST` + `InvoiceService._enqueue_box_excel_clear`, so the Box untag is now a first-class outbox operation (verified on TB3-20: `box.Outbox` 4011 → `done`). Correct that paragraph.

## BUG — `ContractLabor.calculate_billing_period_start()` returns the period END, not the START (2026-08-06)
- [ ] **`calculate_billing_period_start(work_date)` in `entities/contract_labor/business/model.py` computes the period END (the 15th for day 1–15; the last day of month for day 16–EOM), but the entire rest of the system keys on the period START** (the 1st for the first half; the 16th for the second). Every existing `ContractLabor` row uses start dates (`BillingPeriodStart` = `2026-06-01` / `2026-06-16`), and the billing sproc `ReadContractLaborsByStatus` filters `cl.BillingPeriodStart = @BillingPeriodStart` **exactly**. So any record created through `ContractLaborService.create()` (which calls this method) is stamped with the 15th/EOM and is then **invisible to `generate_bills_for_vendor(billing_period_start='YYYY-MM-01')`** — it silently won't bill with its period.
  - **Discovered 2026-08-06** while recovering 14 uncaptured June 1–15 iOS-TimeEntry days: the 14 service-created records came out with `BillingPeriodStart = 2026-06-15` and had to be manually `UPDATE`d to `2026-06-01` before they'd bill. Impact today is limited because most CL rows are import/aggregation-created (which set the start correctly); the bug bites the `ContractLaborService.create()` path specifically.
  - **Root cause:** method name says "start" but the body returns the period-end date (docstring even says "billing period is the 15th"). Convention mismatch with the data + sproc + invoice-number derivation (`_period_end_for` already derives the END from `line_date` independently, so the header field is meant to be the START).
  - **Fix (preferred, small):** change the method to return the period START — `day <= 15 → YYYY-MM-01`, `day >= 16 → YYYY-MM-16`. Matches 100% of existing rows and both billing paths; no data migration. Add a unit test. Grep `calculate_billing_period_start` first to confirm no caller depends on the end-date behavior. (Alternative — make END the canonical semantic — would require migrating all existing rows + the sproc filter; not worth it.)
- [ ] **Related: the iOS `TimeEntry → ContractLabor` conversion silently drops days.** Same 2026-08-06 recovery found 14 June 1–15 clocked days across 4 workers (Mac 7, Jack 3, John 2, Weston 2) that were logged in the app but never became ContractLabor records → never billed until manually recovered. If that conversion is meant to be automatic it's losing days; if it's manual it needs a periodic TimeEntry-vs-ContractLabor day-count reconciliation (query used in the recovery is a good basis). Worth a standing check before each period's billing.

## U-210 — Consolidate ContractLabor review email to one-per-(project,date) crew draft (2026-08-05) — BUILT; follow-ups
- [ ] **Fold employees (EmployeeLabor) into the crew draft (deferred SCOPE, Chris' call 2026-08-05).** Target model: every TimeLog (employee, vendor, intern) becomes a **ContractLabor** record → reviewed/coded → Bill → invoiced. Today aggregation splits `EmployeeId → EmployeeLabor` (no `submitted` state, not in the Review pipeline), so W-2 labor is NOT in the consolidated email yet. This unit stayed on ContractLabor only. The follow-up unifies labor under ContractLabor (nullable `VendorId` + `EmployeeId` on ContractLabor, reroute the aggregation `EmployeeId` branch, migrate EmployeeLabor's invoicing consumers) — billing-touching, its own review/test pass.
- [ ] **Stale `verify_*` scripts for the reviewer-reply flow.** `scripts/verify_contract_labor_apply_reviewer_decision.py`, `scripts/verify_contract_labor_find_for_reviewer_reply.py`, `scripts/verify_contract_labor_reviewer_reply_e2e.py`, `scripts/verify_reviewer_reply_fuzzy_fallback.py` are built around the OLD per-(worker,project) notification + `apply_reviewer_decision(contract_labor_public_id=…)` signature. They're DB-integration manual scripts (not in CI); they need a rewrite for the consolidated `(project, work_date)` crew contract before re-running. Automated coverage of the new body/grouping now lives in `tests/test_cl_review_notification_consolidation.py`.
- [ ] **Notification claim vs enqueue ordering.** `cl_notification_service._do_enqueue` claims the `(project,date)` token BEFORE `enqueue_send_mail`; if the outbox enqueue then fails, the token blocks any retry (dup guard) so the crew draft is silently never sent (logged only). Matches the pre-existing register-before-enqueue behavior, but consider claim-after-enqueue or a repair sweep if it ever bites.
- [ ] **Deploy step:** apply the base SQL to prod — `python scripts/run_sql.py entities/contract_labor/sql/dbo.contract_labor.sql` (idempotent: adds `ContractLaborNotification.WorkDate` + `IX_CLN_ProjectWorkDate`, (re)creates the 3 new sprocs + rewrites `FindContractLaborForReviewerReply`). **Never backfill WorkDate on legacy notification rows by hand** — a non-NULL WorkDate on a legacy row acts as a consolidated-draft claim and permanently suppresses that (project, date)'s first crew email; legacy rows must keep WorkDate NULL. Verify prod `ContractLabor.Status` distinct values are still the pre-vocab-rename set (`pending_review/submitted/ready/billed`) before/after — the gate keys on `'pending_review'`/`'submitted'`.
- [ ] **Filtered unique index on the notification claim (highest value/effort).** Add `UQ_CLN_ProjectWorkDate ON dbo.ContractLaborNotification(ProjectId, WorkDate) WHERE WorkDate IS NOT NULL`. Today the dedup the whole consolidation rests on is an app-level NOT EXISTS under a NON-unique index, and the "never backfill WorkDate" rule is defended only by a comment plus a regex that greps one file. The filtered index makes the claim a real DB invariant and makes a hand-run backfill FAIL LOUDLY instead of silently suppressing crew emails. The WHERE clause is what makes it possible — the ~297 legacy rows share (ProjectId, NULL) and SQL Server treats NULLs as equal in a unique index, so an UNfiltered unique index would not work. Needs a prod duplicate check on non-NULL rows first.
- [ ] **Split-brain definition of "ready" (product decision needed).** Legacy `mark_as_ready` validates parent-row vendor_id/sub_cost_code_id/hourly_rate/total_hours; the new `mark_as_ready_via_review_approval` validates line-item coding; `ContractLabor.is_ready_for_billing` is a third. The legacy one is live behind POST /{id}/mark-ready and the React bulk-mark-ready button. Decide whether legacy is dead (delete) or both should consume one `readiness_blockers` predicate. Also note `repo.bulk_update_status` / `update_status_and_link` write 'ready' with no check at all.
- [ ] **ContractLaborNotification has no repository.** claim/release are hand-written pyodbc INSERT/DELETE in a business service, violating router->service->repo->sproc and invisible to the sproc single-source guard. Right home: `entities/contract_labor/persistence/notification_repo.py` + Claim/Release sprocs, mirroring how `ClaimNextPendingMsOutbox` is done in the MS outbox repo.
- [ ] **Make the two crew sprocs agree by construction.** ReadSubmittedContractLaborLinesByWorkDate and ReadReviewableContractLaborByProjectAndDate must return the same crew; today that is two copies of a predicate plus a regex pin that only checks the Status literal (the ProjectId IS NOT NULL clause is unpinned). Define the crew once as an inline TVF both select from; the grep pin then retires.
- [ ] **Evaluate the ready-flip predicate in SQL.** `mark_as_ready_via_review_approval` reads every line item through the ORM to answer one boolean — 3 round-trips per approval, multiplied by crew size on a reply. Fold it into the flip: `UPDATE dbo.ContractLabor SET Status='ready' WHERE Id=@Id AND Status NOT IN ('ready','billed') AND NOT EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM dbo.ContractLaborLineItem WHERE ContractLaborId=@Id AND ProjectId IS NOT NULL AND SubCostCodeId IS NULL)` — one round-trip, and it closes the read-then-write window.
- [ ] **A deferred ready-flip is terminal.** Nothing re-evaluates it: coding the other project's lines via PUT /{id}/bill triggers no re-check, and legacy mark_as_ready raises for TimeEntry-sourced records. A multi-project worker can sit in 'submitted' indefinitely with only an INFO log. Needs a re-drive.
- [ ] **Test-harness duplication.** The consolidation test file hand-writes the `_Outbox` stub ~10x and repeats the same 5-stub monkeypatch preamble; `_crew_apply_patches` / `_apply_single_cl_patches` / `_choke_point_svc` re-patch overlapping targets. Fold into one parameterized fixture. Also: the `cl_line_item` fixture in tests/conftest.py has zero users — adopt or delete it.
- [ ] **Share one SQL lexer.** `strip_comments_and_strings` (test_sproc_nocount_shape_guard) and `_sproc_body` (test_sproc_single_source) are two lexers in two guard-test modules that other tests now import as a library. Give one a `strip_strings: bool` knob and relocate to tests/helpers/.
- [ ] **`enqueue_drafts` still speaks the pre-consolidation noun.** It takes a ContractLabor but reads only .work_date/.public_id; the unit of work is a DATE. Every project's outbox row is stamped entity_public_id = an arbitrary trigger CL that may have no line on that project. Rename to `enqueue_drafts_for_work_date` and give the outbox row an identity matching its scope (the per-project representative CL, or a uuid5 of (project, date) as the time-entry digest does).
- [ ] **MS tenant resolution runs per enqueue.** `MsOutboxService.enqueue_send_mail` calls `_resolve_tenant_id()` -> `MsAuthService().ensure_valid_token()` (DB read + Fernet decrypt, possibly an HTTP refresh) on EVERY enqueue, so a P-project tick pays P extra auth reads. Cache the single-tenant id per process with a short TTL. Lives in integrations/ms/outbox, not this unit.

## Retroactive-approval Bill backfill — suppress the [Review] re-notification (2026-08-02)

Surfaced during Aug-1 unread hunt: 3 UR invoices ($13,310.21) had full PM approvals sitting on the original email threads for 15-28 days (Tanner 7/7, Tanner 7/8, Cassidy 7/16) but no local Bills had ever been drafted. Standard fix was to draft + complete the Bills retroactively — but the standard `create_bill` code path fires a **[Review] notification email** to the PMs (via `BillService.create` → `ReviewService.create` → `ReviewNotificationService.enqueue_for_bill`), asking them to re-review invoices they already approved weeks ago. That's a footgun for this legitimate use case.

**What actually happened this run:**
- Bill C (Cassidy) triggered the notification chain: 1 `send_mail` outbox row (Id 4502, `status=done`, `mode='draft'`) landed as a draft in invoice@ Outlook Drafts folder addressed to Cassidy/Zach/Austin — undelivered, deletable.
- Bill A + B (Tanner) somehow skipped the notification path entirely (0 Review rows, 0 send_mail rows) — same code path should have triggered; this is a separate mystery worth investigating.

**Correct existing recipe (no code change required today):**
1. `create_bill(..., submit_for_review=False)` — the `submit_for_review=False` param on `entities/bill/api/schemas.py::BillCreate` (line 98-109) gates OFF the auto-Submit chain at `entities/bill/business/service.py:479`. Zero notifications.
2. `apply_reviewer_decision(bill_public_id, decision='approved', reviewer_email=<PM_email>, sub_cost_code_public_id=..., reviewer_email_message_public_id=<UUID of the historic PM reply>)` — writes an Approved Review row directly via `ReviewRepository.create()`, bypassing `ReviewService.create` (which is where the notification trigger lives). Zero outbound mail. Binds Review.EmailMessageId to the original PM reply for audit.
3. `complete_bill(...)` — as usual.

**Blockers to using the recipe safely without code changes:**
- **`submit_for_review=False` reads as "Save For Later"** per its docstring — overloading it for "already approved elsewhere" is fragile. A future refactor that reinterprets Save For Later semantics could silently re-enable the notification. Named-intent flag would be safer.
- **`apply_reviewer_decision` refuses on non-draft Bills** (`service.py:1110-1116`: *"Bill … is no longer a draft … reviewer decisions cannot be applied"*). So step 3 (complete) must happen AFTER step 2. Get the order wrong and you can't retroactively apply.

**Hardening proposals (from `/code_analysis`):**

**(2) Named-intent flag — `suppress_review_notification: Optional[bool] = False`** on `BillCreate` schema (alongside `submit_for_review`). Thread through `BillService.create`, gate the `ReviewService().create(...)` block at `service.py:479` on `and not suppress_review_notification`. Removes the double-negative footgun. Optional companion: require `prior_reviewer_email_message_public_id` when `suppress_review_notification=True` so we always know WHERE the approval lives. Two-line schema + service change; regression test: no `[ms].[Outbox]` `send_mail` row enqueued when this flag is True.

**(3) Dedicated `POST /apply/prior-reviewer-decision` endpoint / service method.** Takes `bill_public_id + prior_email_message_public_id + optional decision override`; resolves the EmailMessage → sender email + ConversationId; validates the sender against `ReviewRecipientService.resolve_for_bill` (same PM/Owner authorization apply_reviewer_decision already runs); writes BOTH a synthetic 'Submitted' Review row (audit) AND the Approved row bound to the prior EmailMessage in one transaction; explicitly does NOT touch any outbox. Concentrates the "retroactive approval" concept behind one verb the agent + UI can point at, enforced server-side. Audit story reads as `Submitted → Approved (retroactive, from thread <ConversationId>)`.

**Companion investigations:**
- **A/B mystery** — why did Bill A + Bill B skip the auto-Submit path when the gate at `service.py:479` (`is_draft AND user_id is not None AND line_project_public_id is not None AND submit_for_review is not False`) should have fired? All three Bills passed all four conditions per my read of the payloads. Investigate before shipping (2)/(3) — there may be a latent bug on the auto-Submit gate that's silently swallowing notifications for legitimate first-time Bills too.
Also captured: incident-recovery TODO — Bill C's [Review] draft is still sitting in invoice@'s Outlook Drafts folder addressed to Cassidy/Zach/Austin (Graph message id `AAMk...AARwBlHiAAA=`, per outbox payload of Id 4502). Delete before manual send if we don't want it going out.

Recommended combo: ship (2) as the two-line defensive rename now; then build (3) for the human-in-the-loop UI to have a single obvious verb.

## U-196 — money-coercion defect CLASS on entity routers (2026-08-02) — SHIPPED; spawned + deferred
U-196 closed the falsy-`Decimal(0)` truthy-guard class on the 5 entity routers (`bill_line_item`, `bill_credit`, `invoice`, `bill`, `bill_credit_line_item`), added `shared/api/money.py::to_decimal_or_none`, and landed `tests/test_router_money_coercion_guard.py` — a default-ON, type-driven AST guard so a 4th occurrence cannot ship. **The guard scans `entities/*/api/router.py` ONLY**, so every item below is invisible to it.
- [ ] **🟡→P3 SPAWNED → U-199 (symptom CORRECTED at Gate-1, em-verified 2026-08-02 — NOT a 🔴, nothing is lost).** The same `float(x) if x else None` shape lives on COMPLETION paths — `entities/invoice/business/service.py:346` (`finalize` total_amount), `:379-381` (line-item amount/markup/price), `entities/bill_credit/business/complete_service.py:125`. **But `update_by_public_id` PRESERVES-on-None in Python (`service.py:236` reads the stored row, `:258` `if total_amount is not None:` only overwrites when provided), and `finalize` re-passes the value it just read — so `None→preserve-stored` and `pass-stored-value` produce the identical DB write. A $0.00 invoice completes with $0.00 intact.** ~~a genuine $0.00 invoice still loses its total when completed~~ — that earlier claim (and the matching board note) was WRONG. The real defects are **(a)** the `float()`-on-money CLAUDE.md violation (×4, latent — exact for ≤15 sig digits, `Markup DECIMAL(18,4)` reaches 18 but not at realistic markups) and **(b)** the falsy-`Decimal(0)` guard as a LANDMINE — it goes live the day `finalize` computes a header (e.g. recompute-from-line-items on complete), and the sprocs ARE clear-on-None (`dbo.invoice.sql:275`, `dbo.invoice_line_item.sql:282-284`, `dbo.bill_credit.sql:236` — all unconditional), so a future real `None` at the repo nulls the column. Fix = route the 5 sites through `to_decimal_or_none`. → **U-199** (P3 hygiene + insurance, ~6 lines + a completion-seam test). Guard extension to `business/` deferred to its own unit (completion code has no annotated param + would need ~15 rendering-site allowlist entries, gutting the ratchet).
- [ ] 🔴 **SPAWNED — same class in the QBO layer (Codex Pass-1 P2, CONFIRMED).** `integrations/intuit/qbo/vendorcredit/connector/bill_credit/business/service.py:146,159` use `if qbo_vc.total_amt` where `total_amt` is `Optional[Decimal]` (`external/schemas.py:89`): a QBO VendorCredit with `TotalAmt = 0.00` updates to stale (preserve-on-None) and creates as SQL NULL. Plus 4 more in `integrations/intuit/qbo/vendorcredit/persistence/repo.py:92,154,177,183`. **6 sites.** The guard is already structured to retarget (`collect_money_coercion_violations(root, glob)` takes the glob) — it needs `"integrations"` added to `_SCHEMA_ROOTS`, which the new fail-loud assertion in that function will demand rather than silently passing.
- [ ] 🟡 **SPAWNED (U-199 correction) — `entities/invoice/business/push.py:179` `price=float(li.amount)` is a genuine 6th same-class site, NOT the disabled QBO push.** The U-199 Gate-1 map wrongly dismissed it; it's a **live KI-16 backfill** write with a `float()`-on-money in kwarg position. Out of U-199's completion-path scope (booked, not fixed). This is the concrete reason a **business-layer money-coercion guard would be RED on arrival** — the strongest argument for building that guard as its own unit (type-driven inference over `business/` locals + return-annotation/loop-target resolution, per the U-196 guard-extension analysis). Fold this site + the QBO cluster above + the completion-annotations into that guard's first green.
- [ ] **Money params still annotated `float` across the services the fixed routers call** (annotation-only, zero runtime change; deliberately excluded from U-196 so Gate-2 never had to re-verify preserve-on-None): `entities/invoice/business/service.py:232`, `bill_credit/business/service.py:165`, `bill/business/service.py:997`, `bill_credit_line_item/business/service.py:123,124,127`, `bill_line_item/business/service.py:156,157,160,161`. They now receive a `Decimal` from every caller, so the annotations are actively misleading — and are plausibly why the completion-path defect above looked acceptable when written.
- [ ] **Consistency: 24 hand-written `Decimal(str(body.X)) if body.X is not None else None` sites never migrated to the helper** — `entities/invoice_line_item/api/router.py` (10), `entities/expense_line_item/api/router.py` (8), `entities/expense/api/router.py` (6). All behavior-CORRECT (`is not None`), so not a bug and invisible to the guard, but they contradict the "single money-coercion seam" the helper claims. Migrating drops 3 more dead `from decimal import Decimal` imports. Purely mechanical.
- [ ] **Retire the guard's single allowlist entry.** `entities/email_message/api/router.py:371` annotates `confidence: Optional[Decimal]`, but it is an agent classification score in `[0,1]`, not money — so `float(body.confidence)` at `:518` is the correct boundary cast and had to be exempted. Re-annotating the field `Optional[float]` deletes the exemption entirely and leaves the allowlist empty (`test_allowlist_entries_are_still_live` then passes vacuously). Small API validation-semantics change; do it deliberately or the allowlist becomes where future mis-annotations hide.
- [ ] **Promote the 9 pre-existing `_coerce_decimal` copies** (`entities/{contract,budget_line_item,employee_project_rate,vendor_project_rate,employee_labor,employee_labor_line_item}/business/service.py` module-level + `{employee,vendor,vendor_insurance_policy}/business/service.py` static) into the new shared helper. `_coerce_decimal` is a superset (also maps `"" → None` and wraps `InvalidOperation` as `ValueError`), so unifying means adopting those two behaviors — decide deliberately, don't just delete. Overlaps the pre-existing "lift `_dec`/`_coerce_decimal` into a `shared/` util" item under U-186/187/188.
- [ ] **Home of the helper — revisit `shared/api/money.py` → `shared/money.py`.** Every other `shared/api/*` module is fastapi-aware (raises `HTTPException` or is a router); `money.py` imports zero fastapi, and every pure `shared/*` helper (`labor_status.py`, `db_constraints.py`, `pdf_utils.py`) lives one level up. Cheap now (one `git mv` + 6 imports + one guard message string); it gets locked in by the QBO unit above, whose `persistence/repo.py` + `business/service.py` call sites would become the first non-api layer importing `shared.api.*`. Path was specified in the U-196 assignment, so this needs Chris's call, not a silent move.

## Bill total_amount + create-shape follow-ups (spawned by web U-173, 2026-07-30)

- [x] **✅ DONE — U-194 (`b8cbe5a`, 2026-08-02, em Gate-2: 933/933 pytest, mutation-RED, pushed; deploy owed).** `entities/bill/api/router.py:346` truthy → `is not None`; the CREATE path (:70) was already correct. NEW `tests/test_bill_total_amount_zero.py` (5 tests) pins the coercion on the real `TriggerContext.payload` seam. ~~$0 total lands as NULL~~. Deploy batches with U-195 (web) + whatever else lands.
- [ ] **🔴 U-196/U-197 — same truthy zero-drop on OTHER live UPDATE paths (grounded by em 2026-08-02, WORSE than U-194):** the CREATE paths are all `is not None`, the UPDATE twins were left truthy. (a) `entities/bill_line_item/api/router.py:126,127,130,131` — truthy on `rate/amount/markup/price`; `markup=0` (no markup) is routine, so clearing it silently retains the old inflated price. (b) **`entities/bill_credit/api/router.py:130`** and **`entities/invoice/api/router.py:905`** — `float(body.total_amount) if body.total_amount else None`: the zero-drop **PLUS a hard CLAUDE.md `float()`-on-money violation** on two live update paths. **Right depth (altitude, Composer + em agree): close the CLASS in ONE unit** — add `shared/api/to_decimal_or_none(x)` (`Decimal(str(x)) if x is not None else None`), migrate all UPDATE-path money coercions (bill router has the shape hand-written ~6×; + bill_line_item + bill_credit + invoice), kill the `float()` sites, and land a structural guard (house style, cf. `test_sproc_nocount_shape_guard.py`) that fails on `float(body.` for a money field or a truthy-guarded money coercion. NB Pydantic already hands the router a true `Decimal`, so `Decimal(str(...))` is redundant — decide that uniformly in this unit, not piecemeal. Own Gate-1 (blast radius: 4 routers + shared helper).
- [ ] **Altitude — accept `line_items[]` on bill create + recompute `total_amount` server-side** (mirrors the contract-labor bill flow). Today the web client computes total_amount, POSTs create/bill with an inline summary line, then POSTs each additional line one-by-one — which spawned U-173's overstated-total finding (a mid-loop line failure leaves the header > the persisted lines) and is an N+1 write. A create-with-line_items endpoint that sums lines server-side in one transaction eliminates the whole class (no client-supplied total, no partial-failure skew, atomic). Bigger unit; coordinate with the QBO/Excel push shape.

Make `due_date` optional (default to BillDate server-side) or document the constraint. Pairs with the router.py:346 total_amount fix.


## ✅ RESOLVED — won't-fix (U-172, closed 2026-08-18) — UpdateBillLineItem null-field contract

**Closed as won't-fix / decided-by-U-111.** em-verified in the deployed code (`01e35dd7`): **no live bug.** `bill_line_item/business/service.py::update_by_public_id` PRESERVES the existing `project_id`/`sub_cost_code_id` when the incoming public_id is `None` (it only overwrites when a value is provided), so the sproc's unconditional `[ProjectId] = @ProjectId` never receives a null to wipe — **no data loss occurs**. **U-111 already ratified** the unconditional-SET as intentional live behavior (`92bd7b7d`, single-sourced to match prod). The only genuinely-wrong artifact — the misleading `"allow None to clear the relationship"` comments (SubCostCode + Project) — is **FIXED** this close-out. A "clear a project from a bill line" path is deliberately NOT implemented (no user need); if that ever becomes a need, implement option (a): CASE-WHEN preserve-on-null + an explicit clear sentinel + a caller audit (BillEdit saveAll / `sync_from_qbo_bill_line` / `generate_bills`). Original scope, now moot, retained below:

_(original scope — 2026-07-29, spawned by web U-169)_

- [ ] `UpdateBillLineItemById` (`entities/bill_line_item/sql/dbo.bill_line_item.sql`) sets `[ProjectId] = @ProjectId` **unconditionally** — a null/omitted ProjectId WIPES the project. This is the server-side root cause of the web U-169 project-strip data loss, and it violates the CLAUDE.md rule "use CASE WHEN guards for fields that should preserve existing values when NULL is passed" (contrast `IsDraft` in the same sproc, which IS guarded). Every other column in this UPDATE is unconditionally set too — audit which should preserve-on-null.
- [ ] Decide the contract: (a) CASE-WHEN preserve-on-null + a distinct explicit "clear project" path/sentinel, or (b) keep clear-on-null and pin it with a test + doc. Then **audit all callers** for null intent before changing semantics — `BillEdit.saveAll` (web), `sync_from_qbo_bill_line` (QBO connector), `generate_bills` (contract labor) — a semantics change could break a caller that relies on the current behavior.
- [ ] Implement + verify base==live. ADDITIVE to U-169 (the web fix ships the user-facing preserve-original-project behavior regardless).


## U-154 follow-ups — DB-error → HTTP mapping (2026-07-27)

U-154 mapped SQL 547 (FK violation) to 422 with a clean message in `shared/api/responses.py::raise_database_error`. Three things it deliberately did NOT do:

- [ ] **The U-053 RBAC surface does not route through `raise_database_error` at all.** `role`, `module`, and `role_module` delete/create go `ProcessEngine.execute_synchronous` → the instant handler swallows the exception into `error=str(e)` → `raise_workflow_error`. Traced today: a 547 there returns **409 with the raw SQL text** (not a 500 — `map_database_error` matches 'conflict' in "conflicted" → `DatabaseConcurrencyError` → `raise_workflow_error`'s "concurrency" branch), and a 2627 duplicate grant returns **400 with raw SQL text**. So U-053's constraints still surface raw schema to admins. Give `raise_workflow_error` the same FK/UQ mapping + clean messages. Own unit — second shared handler, own blast radius.
- [ ] **Right-depth fix: preserve the SQL error number structurally instead of re-parsing prose.** `shared/database.py::map_database_error` is the only place that still holds the raw pyodbc object, and it discards the native error number into an f-string; `raise_database_error` then re-derives "this was a 547" from the flattened text. It works only because string concatenation preserves substrings — incidental, not designed. Fix: carry `sql_error_number` (and SQLSTATE) on `DatabaseError`, populate it in `map_database_error`, and branch on it in `responses.py`. **Gated on the audit below.**
- [ ] **Audit `map_database_error`'s loose substring keyword lists before touching them.** `'conflict'` matches "conflicted", so every FK 547 is already mis-classified as `DatabaseConcurrencyError`; `'lock'`/`'version'`/`'timeout'` have the same shape. ~35 entity repos and `is_transient_error`/retry classification consume this, so reclassifying is a behavior change needing its own sweep. Note `entities/time_entry/sql/migrations/004_cascade_delete_status.sql:6-8` already documents this exact misclassification and routed around it with `ON DELETE CASCADE` — that's now two units papering over the same root.
- [ ] **Delete-path pre-clean (block-vs-cascade) for role/module grants** — deliberately out of U-154's scope (error MAPPING only). Product decision: does deleting a role with live `RoleModule` grants block, or cascade the grants away? U-053's migration explicitly rejects a CASCADE FK, so today it blocks.

## ✅ RESOLVED (U-153 `1ba3afb` + U-160 `87103b1`, deployed 2026-07-29; re-verified live 2026-08-02) — QBO reconcile per-bill GET firehose

**The core P0 is FIXED and LIVE.** All three voided detectors now query-diff instead of per-record GET:
`_reconcile_bill_qbo_voided` (`query_all_bill_ids`, service.py:624), `_reconcile_purchase_qbo_voided`
(`query_all_purchase_ids`, :904), `_reconcile_vendor_credit_qbo_voided` (`query_all_vendor_credit_ids`, :1172) —
each pages `SELECT Id` once (~20 calls), computes `mapped − live` = candidates, gates on a candidate ceiling
(`_void_max_candidates`), and confirms only the candidates with a GET. Both commits are ancestors of the deployed
HEAD `7974d4b`. em re-verified the source 2026-08-02 (all three detectors carry the diff + ceiling). The ~19.4K/run
Bill firehose is gone. **Residual (NOT P0 — verification/tuning only):**
- [ ] **Verify the every-15-min `sync/qbo/{bill,invoice,purchase,vendorcredit}` timers are truly watermark-incremental** (page `query/*` by `Metadata.LastUpdatedTime`, no full re-fetch). They read as small `query/*` volume today, but confirm before trusting — a broken watermark would be a second firehose.
- [ ] **Consider a lighter reconcile cadence** — the voided-detector is drift-detection, not correctness-critical; daily may be overkill (weekly cuts residual cost further).
- [ ] **Before any Intuit Builder→Silver tier upgrade,** re-confirm the query-diff is holding (a regression here would devour the larger 1M budget too).

<details><summary>Original P0 writeup (2026-07-27) — kept for the incident record</summary>

The daily `reconcile_qbo` (03:00 UTC) voided-bill detector at `integrations/intuit/qbo/reconciliation/business/service.py:557-568` loops **every** mapped bill (`qbo.BillBill`, ~19,393 rows) and fires an **individual `client.get_bill(qbo_id)` per bill** — solely to detect 404 (voided/deleted in QBO). That's ~19.4K Bill API calls **per run** × ~22 runs month-to-date = **424,794 calls = 85%** of the Intuit **Builder-tier 500,000 CorePlus-calls/month HARD cap** (excess is *blocked*, not billed). The purchase voided-detector adds ~47K (9%). Combined, the reconcile detectors consume **~94% of the entire monthly QBO allowance** — vs. ~3% for all real business usage (`query/*`). Dashboard (App `build.one`, **Production**, month-to-date) read **488,203 / 500,000**. **Consequence:** once the cap is hit, ALL QBO ops (payment reads, bill/invoice pulls, expense coding, …) return `429 ThrottleExceeded` until the monthly reset — a recurring, self-inflicted QBO outage. Confirmed NOT a per-minute throttle (persisted with the scheduler paused 8 min; every request instant-429s). Blocked payment `1356499241` — had to hand-build the 7 remittances from QBO screenshots. Full incident detail: umbrella memory `project_qbo_reconcile_firehose_monthly_cap.md`.

- [ ] **Fix (right-depth, per-detector) — query-diff instead of per-bill GET:** page `SELECT Id FROM Bill` (1000/page ≈ 20 calls total) to collect all live QBO bill ids for the realm, then `mapped_ids − live_ids` = the voided/deleted set → flag those. ~1000× fewer calls, behavior-preserving (a deleted bill is absent from the query exactly as it 404s today; the detector only distinguishes 404 vs. success). Apply the same rewrite to the **purchase** and **vendor-credit** voided detectors.
- [ ] **Verify the every-15-min `sync/qbo/{bill,invoice,purchase,vendorcredit}` timers are truly watermark-incremental** (page `query/*` by `Metadata.LastUpdatedTime`, no full re-fetch). They read as small `query/*` volume today, but confirm before trusting — a broken watermark would be a second firehose.
- [ ] **Consider a lighter reconcile cadence** — the voided-detector is drift-detection, not correctness-critical; daily may be overkill (weekly + query-diff cuts cost further).
- [ ] **⚠️ Sequencing:** this fix MUST land **before** any Intuit **Builder→Silver tier upgrade**, or the bug just devours the larger (1M-credit) budget too (~471K/mo baseline). If QBO access is needed before the fix ships: upgrade the tier (accepting the waste) or use the no-QBO manual remittance path documented in the memory file.
- [ ] **Pipeline:** normal build → Pass-1 correctness (adversarial, refute-by-default) + Pass-2 quality → test → deploy. No SQL (connector-layer only). Adversarially verify the query-diff flags the **same** set the per-bill GET does — note the current code flags only 404s (hard deletes); confirm whether QBO-*voided* (present-but-zeroed) bills were ever caught (they return 200, so today they are **not**) so the rewrite doesn't silently change coverage.

</details>

## ✅ RESOLVED (U-053, 2026-07-27) — unique index on `dbo.Module.Name` (was U-139 deferral, 2026-07-24) — SCHEMA HARDENING

`dbo.Module.Name` has no unique constraint, so every module seed hand-rolls `IF NOT EXISTS`-by-Name and a concurrent double-apply could mint duplicate module rows (Codex P1 on U-139, accepted residual — migrations here are one-off manual applies; 006's post-apply cardinality assert fails loudly + rolls back on any committed duplicate). Right-depth fix is its own schema unit, NOT a data migration: dedupe/phantom check first (`entities/module/sql/cleanup_phantom_modules.sql` exists for a reason), then `CREATE UNIQUE INDEX UQ_Module_Name ON dbo.[Module]([Name])` in the module entity base file. RBAC resolves modules by name, so uniqueness is a real invariant the schema should own.

**ADDRESSED by U-053 (apply pending):** `UQ_Module_Name` is now declared in `entities/module/sql/dbo.module.sql`, with `entities/role_module/sql/migrations/001_rbac_join_integrity_constraints.sql` as the apply vehicle (guards on 0 duplicate names first). **The prod apply is separately gated and may not have run yet** — confirm with the U-053 read-back query below before treating the constraint as live.

## 🟢 U-120 — `coi_parser` live-ACORD calibration (2026-07-22) — DONE

**DONE:** `_parse_policies_from_tables` rewritten to parse the DI **coverage-grid table by header-name column mapping** (INSR LTR / TYPE OF INSURANCE / POLICY NUMBER / POLICY EFF / POLICY EXP — indices differ per cert), grouping multi-row coverage blocks, dropping blank sections (no policy#/dates), carrier via INSR-LTR → INSURER A–F table; content-scan kept as fallback when no grid table. Sanitized DI fixtures (`tests/fixtures/acord_{gl_auto,wc}_di.json`, scrubbed from the real certs) + `tests/test_coi_parser_grid.py`; existing content-path tests unchanged. 543 tests green. **LIVE-validated on A&A's real GL+WC DI:** GL #03750825 exp 2025-12-14 + Auto #5575082500 exp 2025-12-14 (no spurious WC) and WC #UB-5W154448-25-42 exp 2026-02-24 (no spurious GL/Auto) — auto-parses exactly what previously needed hand-correction.

_Original problem (kept for provenance):_ Proven necessary during the A&A Masonry test case: on a REAL ACORD 25 (A&A's GL cert), `entities/certificate_of_insurance/business/coi_parser.py` mis-parsed the coverage table — put the **limit `1,000,000` into `policy_number`**, **missed EVERY `POLICY EXP` date** (they're plainly in the DI text as `12/14/2025`), invented a **spurious empty WC row** (matched the blank "WORKERS COMPENSATION" section header), and only half-mapped carriers. The raw DI `content` is clean — it's the **table-mapping / insurer-letter→carrier + limits-vs-policy#-column logic** that isn't calibrated. Ground-truth to calibrate against (A&A's GL cert, attachment `6A657092…`): GL insurer-A OWNERS #**03750825** eff 12/14/2024 exp **12/14/2025** $1M/$2M; Auto insurer-B Auto-Owners #**5575082500** exp 12/14/2025 $1M CSL; WC section BLANK (not a policy). Fixes: (1) read `POLICY EXP (MM/DD/YYYY)` per coverage row; (2) don't put limit amounts in `policy_number` (require a non-limit alnum token from the POLICY NUMBER column); (3) drop coverage rows with no policy#/dates/limits (blank ACORD section headers); (4) resolve carrier by INSR-LTR → INSURER A–F block. Re-test on A&A's GL + WC certs (both in the folder). Until then, COI ingests need human correction of the drafted policies.

## 🟢 U-122 — Taxpayer W-9 ingest 500 on blank NOT-NULL columns (2026-07-22, found onboarding Engineered Comfort) — DONE (deploy HELD w/ batch)

**DONE:** `TaxpayerW9IngestService.ingest` now coerces every `dbo.Taxpayer` NOT-NULL column that took an `Optional`/blank-passable value, so a valid W-9 persists (taxpayer + TIN) instead of dying with a bare SQL 515 → HTTP 500. Service-side only, **no schema change**: `business_name` → legal `entity_name` when None/blank (a business with no DBA operates under its legal name); `classification` → `"Unspecified"` when None/blank (extract's `unresolved` already flags it for review); blank `entity_name` → clear `ValueError` (400, create branch only — reuse-by-TIN untouched); blank/digit-less `taxpayer_id_number` → `ValueError` (400) rejected **before** the dedup lookup (it's the identity/dedup key + NOT NULL, `TaxpayerService` coerces a falsy TIN to NULL — no sensible default). Correctness review: Codex gpt-5.5 high, refute-by-default — confirmed reuse path safe / ValueError→400 / no CHECK on Classification / tests assert via `create` kwargs, and it **caught the TIN case** (added). 7 new ingest tests (parametrized blanks), 560 green.
_Original repro: Engineered Comfort, Inc.'s W-9 has a blank line-2 business name → `Cannot insert NULL into 'BusinessName'`; the vendor-agent onboarding worked around it client-side, but the deployed endpoint (web UI / `w9_parser` path) would still 500._
- [ ] **DEPLOY (held):** rides the existing api batch (U-098/112/114/116/117/118/120/121) — deploying builds the whole working tree, and that batch is web-coordinated (U-116 web prereq). No independent deploy; ships when the batch does.

## 🐛 w9_parser mis-parses complex/holding-company W-9s (2026-07-23, found onboarding Walker Lumber) — CALIBRATION

On Walker Lumber & Supply's W-9 (Rev. March 2024; a US LBM / Bain "BCPE Ulysses Buyerco" holding structure), `entities/taxpayer/business/w9_parser.py::parse_w9_fields` mis-read three fields (hand-corrected at ingest; Taxpayer 33 is right):
- **entity_name (line 1) → `None`.** Failed to extract "BCPE ULYSSES BUYERCO, INC" (long line-1 value; the Rev-3/2024 layout puts a lot of instructional text between the "1 Name of entity/individual…" label and the value).
- **business_name (line 2) → got line 1** ("BCPE ULYSSES BUYERCO, INC") instead of the line-2 DBA text ("US LBM Holdings LLC & Affiliates … DBA Walker Lumber & Supply"). Entity/business line assignment is swapped when both lines carry long values.
- **classification → "LLC"** but the `:selected:` checkbox is **C corporation**. The parser matched the instructional `Check the "LLC"…` note text, not the DI checkbox-selection state (`:selected:` vs `:unselected:` markers are in the content).
- [ ] **Calibrate (candidate /em unit, mirror the coi_parser U-120/121 approach):** anchor line-1/line-2 by the "1 Name…" / "2 Business name…" labels and take the value AFTER the instructional parenthetical; assign classification from the `:selected:` marker adjacent to a class label, not from instructional text; add a sanitized DI fixture for a Rev-3/2024 holding-co W-9 + a swap/again-classification regression test. NB the TIN extraction was CORRECT here (Part I TIN last4 0477, not the line-2 EIN 27-0923448) — don't regress that. Interim: complex W-9s need hand-verified entity/business/classification at ingest.
- **Classification re-eval across the first 10 vendors (2026-07-23):** the parser essentially **always wrote "LLC"** (right only for the 4 that genuinely selected the LLC box, by luck). Re-reading each W-9's `:selected:` box found **2 genuine errors — Engineered Comfort (S corporation, stored LLC) and Smith Construction (Individual/sole proprietor / single-member LLC, stored LLC)** — both corrected via a surgical `dbo.Taxpayer.Classification` column update (NOT the service — avoids re-encrypting the TIN with the local key). **DI double-marks checkboxes:** Siteworks and Liaison each show TWO `:selected:` markers (Trust/estate+LLC; Partnership+LLC) — both are really LLC (Liaison = LLC electing "P" partnership tax). So classification parsing must (a) read the `:selected:` marker not label text, (b) tolerate multiple false `:selected:` markers — cross-check against the entity-name suffix (Inc./LLC) and the LLC sub-classification letter, and prefer a single clean selection. A naive "first `:selected:`" heuristic over-flags (my re-check tool did: 4 flags → 2 real after raw-box verification).
- **is_signed re-eval DONE (2026-07-23):** visually verified all 10 first-batch W-9 signature blocks (Read the PDFs — OCR can't read a handwritten/e-signature). **All 10 are signed** — the 3 `IsSigned=0` flags (Siteworks, Iron Grove, Harpeth) were **parser FALSE NEGATIVES**, now corrected to `IsSigned=1` (surgical column update). The 7 `IsSigned=1` were all genuinely signed (incl. Liaison = a service e-signature with a Signer ID). So the parser's signature detection is unreliable (misses handwritten + e-sigs); until it's fixed, **is_signed needs visual verification**, and Owner requires a signature on every W-9 (unsigned → chase; nothing-marked classification → inquire to vendor). Classification corrections (EC→S corp, Smith→Individual/sole-prop) also visually validated against the checkboxes.
- [ ] **Signature-detection fix** belongs in the w9_parser calibration unit: detect both handwritten (Part II signature region present) and e-signatures (a "Signer ID"/electronic-signature block), not just a signature date.

## 🐛 `GET /vendor-compliance/dashboard` is slow — times out ~90s (2026-07-23, found during the top-16 vendor sweep) — PERF

The dashboard endpoint recomputes **every** tracked vendor's coverage map (`resolve_coverage_map` across all that vendor's certs + policies) on each call. Now that ~16 vendors are compliance-tracked with many certs each (Green Acres 8, Townsend 8, G's Framing 13…), a single call intermittently exceeds a 90s client timeout — it will be equally slow (or worse) in the web `/vendor-compliance` UI. Repeatedly hit during onboarding; worked around by reading `dbo.VendorInsurancePolicy` (`MAX(ExpiryDate)` per `CoverageType`) for one vendor instead.
- [ ] **Fix:** cache/precompute the per-vendor coverage rollup (e.g. materialize on COI ingest), and/or add a single-vendor endpoint `GET /vendor-compliance/dashboard/{vendor_public_id}` so a one-vendor verify doesn't recompute the whole roster. Confirm the resolver isn't doing N+1 cert/policy reads per vendor.

## 🟢 U-109 — W-9 Ingestion Flow, Phase 1 (2026-07-21) — Taxpayer entity made usable

The Taxpayer/W-9 entity was built + had a React UI but was ZERO-populated with a BROKEN dedup (Fernet-encrypted the TIN then equality-matched the ciphertext → non-deterministic → never matched → duplicate TINs silently allowed). This unit: keyed **HMAC-SHA256 blind index** `Taxpayer.TaxpayerIdNumberHash` (+ filtered-unique index, now `WHERE …IS NOT NULL AND IsDeleted=0`) routes all TIN dedup/lookup deterministically; **TIN masked** in `to_dict` (last4) + `read_all`/list never full + audited `can_update`-gated reveal endpoint `GET /get/taxpayer/{id}/tin`; schema cleanup (soft-delete `IsDeleted`, nullable `SignatureDate`, `TaxpayerAttachment` cols NOT NULL, `XACT_ABORT ON`); `TaxpayerW9IngestService` (`extract`: upload OR vendor-folder fetch w/ prove-in-folder → Document Intelligence → masked draft; `ingest`: **shareable** dedup — reuse existing Taxpayer by TIN-hash else create → link `TaxpayerAttachment` → set `Vendor.TaxpayerId`); pure best-effort DI W-9 parser `w9_parser.parse_w9_fields`; `w9_hint` on folder listings. Owner decisions: masked+reveal, DI required, shareable cardinality. Codex xhigh: 2 real findings FIXED (classification.value→getattr pre-existing 500; unique-index IsDeleted filter). Fixed a **pre-existing latent bug**: `TaxpayerRepository` called `classification.value` on a string — any create-with-classification would have 500'd (never hit because 0 rows).
- [ ] **Parser tuning (needs real DI output):** classification is the least-reliable field — an "LLC" in a business name can outvote the checkbox; the deterministic parser is best-effort + human-confirmed. Tune against a live A&A W-9 DI dry-run (held until Owner's prod deploy).
- [ ] **Accepted residuals (Codex P2/P3):** (P2b) upload-mode `attachment_public_id` has no vendor-link proof — trusted `VENDORS can_create` role + matches the compliance-import pattern; add a vendor-scoped attachment check if the trust boundary tightens. (P2c) blind index needs a stable ENCRYPTION_KEY across restarts — prod-safe, documented on `blind_index`. (P3) `detail=str(e)` on 500 — house-consistent.

- [ ] **Web UI (follow-on):** the dashboard W9-slot "Import/Upload W-9" action + review form (`build.one.web`) that drives `w9/extract` → review (masked, reads full TIN off the PDF) → `w9/ingest`.

## 🟢 U-103 — Attachment blob-name dropped-dot fix (2026-07-21) — CODE FIX (data already backfilled)

Root cause: `POST /upload/attachment` AND `POST /upload/bill-line-item-attachment` built the Azure blob name by concatenating the UUID with a **dotless** extension (`AttachmentService.extract_extension` deliberately strips the dot for the `FileExtension` column) → malformed `<uuid>pdf`. Fixed via a new pure `AttachmentService.build_blob_name(public_id, ext)` (inserts the dot); both call sites route through it. The two `/temp/pending-*` handlers store in an in-memory token dict (no blob) → no bug. Regression test `tests/test_attachment_blob_name.py`. **Data:** all 396 pre-existing malformed prod blobs were migrated (download→verify SHA-256→dotted copy→repoint BlobUrl ROWVERSION-guarded→delete old) + reconciled to 0 remaining this session. Root cause closed at both sites — no residual.

- [ ] **A&A doc-import modeling wrinkle:** A&A's Box (and SharePoint) folder holds SEPARATE GL-COI and WC-COI PDFs, but the model has ONE COI slot per vendor (newest-per-type wins) with policies as children (no per-policy attachment). Importing the GL COI would create a 2nd COI doc that MASKS the expired WC. Decide the multi-coverage representation before bulk-importing A&A's COIs (out of U-106 scope — this is the compliance data model, not the folder plumbing).

## 🟢 U-104 — Compliance folder-import dedup (2026-07-21) — the duplicate-COI cause

Root cause: `entities/vendor_compliance_document/business/folder_service.py::import_file` created a `VendorComplianceDocument` **unconditionally**, so re-importing a byte-identical certificate minted a duplicate doc that **masked** the real one on the dashboard (dashboard surfaces the newest doc per `DocumentType` and keys status only on `expiry_date`, not `VerificationStatus` — the exact A&A Masonry incident). Fixed: same `(vendor, DocumentType, attachment file_hash)` → skip upload+create, return the existing doc + `already_on_file=True`. Pure helper `select_duplicate_compliance_doc` + `tests/test_vendor_compliance_dedup.py`. A genuine renewal (different PDF/hash, same type) still imports and the newest-per-type logic surfaces it.
- [ ] **DEFERRED (own future unit) — generic attachment dedup + reference-counted deletion.** `POST /upload/attachment` already computes SHA-256 and calls `read_by_hash` but the reuse is COMMENTED OUT (`entities/attachment/api/router.py:296-302`). Turning it on makes multiple parents share ONE `Attachment` row+blob, but the `Bill`/`Expense`/etc. delete cascades delete the `Attachment` **+ blob** with **no refcount** → deleting one parent nukes a blob another still references (data-loss). Enabling generic dedup REQUIRES building reference-counted attachment deletion across the Bill/Expense/Invoice/compliance cascades first. Owner deferred it (chose "hash-gate dedup, keep UUID names" over content-addressed storage). Un-defer as its own unit; this is also the seam for a future content-addressed-blob scheme.
- [ ] (optional) The compliance **manual-create** path (`VendorComplianceDocumentService.create`) has no same-hash guard — a human uploading the identical cert twice via the manual API could still duplicate (visible/rare vs the silent automated import). Lift the dedup into `create()` if it recurs.

## 🟢 U-075 follow-ups — Update*/read column-parity audit (2026-07-20) — guard test SHIPPED (`tests/test_update_read_column_parity.py`), report-only findings below

The Gate-1 audit established the two-layer contract: UPDATE sprocs are **full-row writers** (pass-through NULL is load-bearing — e.g. CL unlink clears `BillLineItemId` through unguarded `UpdateContractLaborById`; guarding it would break bill deletes with FK 547) and the **service layer is the merge point** (re-read + overlay non-None). Blanket CASE-WHEN guards would therefore BREAK intentional clears (`project.notes` `""→None`, `budget_line_item` grid clearability, `budget_revision` full-row). Live-prod parity swept 2026-07-20: **0 U-052-class gaps live**. Residuals:

- [ ] **Tolerant-`getattr` strictification campaign (masker class, U-052).** 26 repos map updatable columns via `getattr(row, 'X', None)` in `_from_db` — if a read sproc ever drops a column (stale-base re-apply), the merge wipes it SILENTLY instead of erroring. Ranked by blast radius: contract_labor (26 cols), invoice_line_item (14), bill_line_item (12), expense_line_item (12), bill_credit_line_item (11), attachment (10), vendor_compliance_document (9), bill (8, incl. VendorId/TotalAmount), invoice (8), vendor_insurance_policy (8), expense (7), bill_credit (6), +14 smaller. Strictify per-entity (row.Col direct → loud AttributeError) — behavior-changing, own unit(s), pair naturally with each single-source conversion.
- [ ] **Base-vs-LIVE read-sproc column drift — 16 sprocs (stale-base landmine, U-037/U-089 class).** Live is AHEAD of base on: ReadVendorById/ByPublicId (`HourlyRate`,`Markup`,`TrackCompliance`), ReadUserById/ByPublicId (7 cols incl. `IsSystemAdmin`,`IsAgent`), ReadCompanyById/ByPublicId (`OrganizationId`,+audit), ReadOrganizationById/ByPublicId, ReadUserCompany/UserModule/UserProject/UserRole ById+ByPublicId (audit + `RoleId`/`RoleName`/`CompanyId`). Re-applying any of these base files would regress prod reads and CREATE the U-052 wipe condition. Fold into the single-source campaign — reconcile base to live before any re-apply.
- [ ] **Dormant CL status sprocs foot-gun.** `UpdateContractLaborStatusByIds` + `UpdateContractLaborStatusAndLink` have zero callers; their repo methods default `bill_line_item_id=None` while the sprocs SET `[BillLineItemId]` unguarded — a future status-only caller silently wipes bill links. Guard-or-remove when a caller appears (the parity test does not cover non-`*ById` sprocs).
- [ ] **`Auth.UserId` defense-in-depth note.** `UpdateAuthById` SETs `UserId` unguarded; today defended only by `AuthUpdate.user_id: int` being schema-required. If that field ever goes Optional, a partial PUT unlinks the login from its User. Consider a COALESCE guard (no legit clear path exists — relink uses `update_user_id_by_public_id`) next time `dbo.auth.sql` is touched for another reason.

## 🟢 U-063 follow-ups — Auth rate-limiting (2026-07-17) — SHIPPED (username-keyed, in-proc, `shared/rate_limit.py`), residuals below

The rate limiter (`/auth/login`, `/mobile/auth/login`, `/admin/auth/set-credentials`) is username-keyed, per-worker in-proc token bucket, count-failures-only on login, TTL+LRU bounded at 10k keys. Codex `gpt-5.5` (xhigh) verified atomicity, the O(1) front-scan eviction invariant, and no auth regression. Accepted, documented residuals (all deliberately NOT fixed in U-063):
- [ ] **P3 refund-vs-refill boundary race (Codex R2).** A legit login that reserves a token just before a 180s refill boundary, interleaved with an attacker hitting the same username *at* the boundary, can yield ONE extra failed attempt (6 where 5 intended). Reproduced (`5 1 6`). Long-run rate unchanged; fix needs a reservation-handle protocol threaded through the auth hot path — worse trade than the slop. Documented, not fixed.
- [ ] **P2 LRU-reset of a throttled bucket.** Inherent to the bounded store: ~10k distinct sprayed usernames per worker evicts a target's bucket, resetting it to full. Cost/benefit for the attacker is terrible (~2000:1); the alternative is an unbounded store (OOM) or DB-backed (rejected). Mitigated by the per-IP follow-up below.
- [ ] **`/signup/auth` is NOT rate-limited (out of U-063 scope).** It brute-forces a shared `signup_registration_code` via `hmac.compare_digest` (`entities/auth/business/service.py:695`), unauthenticated, and a correct guess MINTS an account. Stronger target than set-credentials — promote to its own unit (rate-limit keyed on IP-or-registration-code, or a lockout after N global failures).
- [ ] **U-070 — per-IP rate-limiting (deferred).** v1 is username-only because `X-Forwarded-For` is client-spoofable and `request.client.host` is the Azure LB IP (would bucket the whole company). First step: empirically verify what Azure App Service actually sends (the `ProxyHeadersMiddleware` at `app.py:134` only handles `X-Forwarded-Proto` today), THEN key on the verified client IP. Would mitigate both the targeted-throttle residual and the LRU-reset residual.
- [ ] **iOS 429 handling unverified.** The app has never received a 429 from login. Confirm it surfaces "try again shortly" rather than a credential-failure or a retry loop (cf. `project_ios_onboarding_lockout`).
