## U-069 — QBO/MS `force_refresh` redundant-refresh dampener (2026-07-17, LOW — found during U-063)

Not a bug (no token loss — the lock ordering is already correct). `ensure_valid_token(force_refresh=True)` skips the already-refreshed check, so N concurrent 401-recoveries each do a real serialized refresh instead of one. Waste + Intuit/MS rate-limit exposure, not correctness. `integrations/intuit/qbo/auth/business/service.py` + `integrations/ms/auth/business/service.py` (keep symmetric).

## U-080 — Dead completion triggers on create/update paths (2026-07-17, found during U-065) — LATENT, pre-existing

`create` (non-draft) at `entities/bill/api/router.py:104` and `update` (draft→complete) at `:375` both `add_task(_run_complete_bill, ...)` — but the row's `IsDraft` is already `False` by the time the background task reads it, so `_run_complete_bill`'s `is_draft` guard (`:419`) self-skips and the SharePoint/Excel/QBO side-effects **never run**. Only `POST /complete/{id}` keeps the row a draft through `complete_bill()`, so it is the one live completion path. Deliberately split out of U-065 (bracketing them with a CompletionJob = phantom-success rows, or removing the skip = silently activating a dormant side-effect path — a real behavior change out of that unit's scope).
- [ ] **Confirm no client completes via those paths first** — web "Complete" uses `/complete`; check iOS / agents / scripts never `POST create is_draft=false` or `PUT is_draft=false`. If any do, this is a live data-integrity bug (entity marked complete, zero external sync).
- [ ] Then **either delete the triggers as vestigial, or activate + CompletionJob-cover them.** **Audit all four** — bill / expense / invoice / bill_credit routers share the shape. (U-065 left one-line `# vestigial, see U-080` markers at the sites.)

## U-081 — MS Excel drain worker re-checks column-Z at drain time (2026-07-17, found during U-065 Gate-2) — closes the reclaim-overlap residual

The one side-effect NOT idempotent under overlapping completion runs. Column-Z (line-item public_id) is checked only at *enqueue*; Excel outbox rows never coalesce and the drain worker doesn't re-check, so two overlapping runs of the same completion — a reclaim overtaking a still-alive >30-min-hung task (U-065's N=1800s residual, practically unreachable but real) — can enqueue duplicate DETAILS rows. SharePoint (coalesce+replace) and QBO (mapping-check) are already overlap-idempotent; Excel is the only gap.
- [ ] Make the MS Excel drain worker re-check column-Z at drain time → true idempotency under overlap. **Shared sensitive path** — touches every Excel sync (Box + SharePoint workbooks) and the insert row-index is position-dependent, so it needs its own verify + blast-radius pass (which is why it was kept out of U-065).

## U-084 — Diagnose the real `/time-entries` 13.5s (2026-07-17, from U-064 Gate-2) — the iOS-timeout blocker is NOT the DB query

**Correction to U-064's premise.** U-064 (`e69e93a`) shipped a covering index `IX_TimeEntryStatus_TimeEntryId_CreatedDatetime_Id INCLUDE(Status)` — a *real, correct* optimization (~5–9× on the warm status query; applied to prod 2026-07-17). But the Gate-2 perf measurement disproved the triage's premise: at prod volumes (**945 TimeEntry / 2,233 TimeEntryStatus rows**) the DB was never the bottleneck — `ReadTimeEntriesPaginated` was 242–482ms *without* the index and 52ms with it; the `@Status`-filter APPLY path 48ms; count 106ms; batch status read 96ms. **Nothing DB-side approaches 13.5s.** The U-064 commit message's claim ("the real 13.5s cost is the missing covering index") is wrong — keep the index (it's a genuine win) but it does not resolve the 13.5s.

- [ ] **Measure `/time-entries` END-TO-END from the client** (not the sproc in isolation): container cold-start (Always On healthy? the documented 10–15s cold-start window matches 13.5s almost exactly), per-request RBAC permission resolution + the `UserCanAccessTimeEntry` UDF on a cold cache, response serialization, network/TLS from the iOS app.
- [ ] Identify the dominant cost, fix it, THEN raise the iOS request timeout (U-064 was mis-scoped to unblock that — it doesn't).

Measured baseline as of 2026-07-21: **120** sproc names defined in 2+ `.sql` files (94×2, 22×3, 4×4), **39** distinct non-base files carrying a duplicate body, **14** names with no single canonical home. After it lands, each conversion becomes a *deletion* from the allow-list — the ratchet only turns one way.

## 🟡 U-057 follow-ups — TimeEntry.ProjectId retirement (2026-07-16) — code LANDED, prod DDL DEFERRED by request

**Context.** The project link lives on `dbo.TimeLog.ProjectId` (one per clock-in segment — a worker can move sites inside one `WorkDate`; Weston Parker 2026-05-06 is the worked example: 8.00h at 925 Overton Lea + 0.57h at 424 Westview Avenue). `dbo.TimeEntry.ProjectId` was left behind by the original move: nullable, FK already dropped, **NULL on all 939 rows**, read by nothing. U-057 repointed the 14 `ActiveProjectIds` CTEs to TimeLog, granted the 5 missing GEN rows, and staged a guarded drop in the base file.

- [ ] **Apply the prod DDL** (deferred by Chris — code landed first, on purpose). `.venv/bin/python scripts/run_sql.py entities/time_entry/sql/dbo.time_entry.sql` drops `IX_TimeEntry_ProjectId` + `FK_TimeEntry_Project` + the `ProjectId` column. The block is **guarded**: it aborts with a RAISERROR if any non-NULL value survives, so it cannot silently eat data in an environment that never completed the TimeLog backfill. Until this runs, the file and prod disagree on `dbo.TimeEntry`'s shape — the exact drift that started this thread. Verified safe to defer: `CreateTimeEntry`'s params + INSERT column list contain no `ProjectId`, so nothing can write a value into the window. Base file re-verified with `SET PARSEONLY ON` (42/42 batches).
- [ ] **ALTITUDE — collapse the 14 copy-pasted `ActiveProjectIds` CTEs into one `dbo.ActiveProjectIds(@Cutoff)` inline TVF.** This duplication *is* the root cause U-057 fixed: the same defect existed in 14 files at once (12 `onboard.*.sql` + `patch.austin_user_projects.sql` + `seed.personas.sql`) and had to be regex-fixed 14 times. Next time an FK moves tables, all 14 must be found again — miss one and that persona silently under-grants project access with **no error**. One TVF makes it a one-line change. (Deferred out of U-057 to keep the unit scoped; it's a mechanical but broad refactor of one-off onboarding scripts.)
- [ ] **LATENT — the two RoleId-granting scripts now expand review-notification recipients.** `onboard.tanner.sql` (`@PMRoleId`) and `patch.austin_user_projects.sql` (`@OwnerRoleId`) are the only two whose `UserProject` INSERT carries a `RoleId`. The TimeLog branch adds exactly one project the financial branches don't cover — **152 `GEN - General Project`** — so **re-running either script** inserts `UserProject(user, 152, RoleId=Owner|Project Manager)`. `dbo.ResolveReviewRecipientsByBillId` filters `UserProject` to `Role.Name IN ('Project Manager','Owner')`, so Austin/Tanner would begin receiving forward-of-original review emails for every bill coded to GEN. **Not live** — U-057's own grant inserted `RoleId=NULL` (pure access, zero notification effect) and GEN currently has **no** PM/Owner rows at all. Decide whether that's wanted *before* those scripts are next re-run.

## 🟠 Box draw-requests mapping drifted to a per-invoice subfolder → new draws nest one level too deep (HP2-11, 2026-07-16) — DATA FIXED, code root-cause OPEN

**Symptom (HP2-11 InvoiceAgent run):** operator couldn't see the HP2-11 folder under HP2's `15 - Draw Requests` in Box. Files were **not lost** — the packet + 30 line PDFs uploaded fine but landed at `15 - Draw Requests / HP2-10 / HP2-11 /` (nested inside the *prior* draw's folder) instead of `15 - Draw Requests / HP2-11 /`. SharePoint was correct the whole time (HP2-11 a proper sibling of HP2-01…HP2-10).

**Root cause:** HP2's `box.ProjectFolder` **draw_requests** mapping (FolderRowId 5) pointed at Box folder **`388344464685`**, which is actually the **`HP2-10` per-invoice subfolder** — not the real `15 - Draw Requests` (`388348167653`). The `box.Folder` row even carried a **stale name** ("15 - Draw Requests") for that folder, so the Step-1c mapping pre-flight looked correct by name. `InvoiceService._enqueue_box_line_pdfs` creates a per-invoice subfolder `<INV#>` under the mapped folder, so with the mapping pointing at HP2-10 it created `HP2-11` inside HP2-10. HP2-10's own files (created when the mapping still pointed at `15 - Draw Requests`) are directly in `388344464685` — consistent with the mapping having been repointed to the HP2-10 subfolder **between** the HP2-10 and HP2-11 draws.

**The dangerous shape:** if some code path rewrites the draw_requests mapping to the newly-created per-invoice subfolder at the end of a draw, **every project nests one level deeper each draw** (HP2-12 under HP2-11 under HP2-10…), silently, forever. Only caught here because the operator eyeballed the finished draw.

- [x] **Data fixed 2026-07-16 (HP2, user-authorized).** (1) Moved Box folder `400608575452` (`HP2-11`, 31 files) from parent `388344464685` → `388348167653` (real `15 - Draw Requests`); verified new path + sibling of HP2-10; HP2-10 untouched (66 items). (2) Repointed `box.Folder` row 5 → BoxFolderId `388348167653`, Name `15 - Draw Requests`, ParentBoxFolderId `388317112134` (project root), so future HP2 draws nest correctly.
- [x] **Cross-project audit 2026-07-16 — CLEAN.** Checked all **23** `box.ProjectFolder` draw_requests mappings against the live Box folder name + parent (`scratchpad/box_drawmap_audit.py`): every one points at a real `15 - Draw Requests` folder whose parent is the project root; **0 mis-pointed, 0 stale DB-names**. HP2 was the only affected project. (Shared sub-unit folders — MR2-BARN/MAIN/SITE → one folder, OHR2/OHR2-GUEST → one folder — are the intended shared-folder behavior, not defects.)
- [ ] **CODE ROOT-CAUSE — find what repointed HP2's mapping to the subfolder.** Grep for any writer of `box.ProjectFolder` / `box.Folder.BoxFolderId` outside the explicit `POST /box/map-project` admin path (esp. anything in the draw/`_enqueue_box_line_pdfs` / packet path or the Box outbox drain that could "update the mapping to the folder it just created/uploaded into"). Until this is found, the audit above should be **re-run periodically** (cheap) to catch any project that starts drifting.
- [ ] **Guard — subfolder-name sanity at enqueue.** `_enqueue_box_line_pdfs` (and the packet enqueue) could assert the resolved draw_requests folder's **name is draw-like** (contains "Draw Request") before creating a `<INV#>` subfolder under it — refuse + surface if the mapping resolves to something named like an invoice (`<ABBREV>-<NN>`). Cheap belt against the nesting-forever failure mode.
- [ ] **Fix `box.Folder` name drift generally.** The stale "15 - Draw Requests" name on a folder actually named "HP2-10" is what made the Step-1c pre-flight pass. Consider having the mapping pre-flight (Step 1c) verify the *live* Box folder name, not the cached `box.Folder.Name`, so a drifted mapping is caught during Phase-1 audit, not on the finished packet.

Both gates held; Codex Pass-1 caught 3 vacuous/weak assertions → tightened + mutation-verified to bite. - [ ] **Backfill — DB-integration coverage (deferred out of v1; needs a test-DB strategy).** The risk-first paths that require a live DB / sproc execution: **sprocs** (NULL-overwrite guard, `SET NOCOUNT ON`, no in-sproc `ROLLBACK`, FK cleanup order) → **RBAC scoping** (UserProject / `set_authz_context`) → **QBO mappings** (`BillLineItemBillLine`, `qbo.*.Id` ≠ `dbo.*.Id`). Design the test-DB approach (fixture DB / transactional rollback / sproc harness) first.
- [ ] **Follow-up — extract the CL bill consolidation math into a pure helper so the NUMBERS can be unit-tested (own Pass-1 review).** Today the Hours×Rate cost, `is_billable is not False` grouping, non-billable-still-A/P, and markup are inline in the DB-writing `generate_bills_for_vendor` — v1 covered them **structurally only**. A small behavior-preserving extraction (e.g. `consolidate_bill_line_items(items)` / A/P-total) makes the app's highest-risk money path numerically testable; it touches the financially-critical bill-gen path, so it needs its own correctness review (not a rider on a test-harness unit).
- [ ] **Test-env note:** `xlrd==2.0.2` (declared in `requirements.txt`) must be installed in whatever env runs pytest, or `tests/test_cl_parse.py` `importorskip`s (the `_parse_row` tests skip rather than run). `build.one.scheduler` is the same greenfield story if/when it gets logic. Full playbook: `build.one.team/prompts/test-automation.md`.

## ✅ RESOLVED 2026-07-16 (U-052) — was 🔴 LIVE (found during U-050): editing a Project in the React UI silently wiped `Project.Notes`

**Fixed in prod 2026-07-16 (U-052):** the 4 corrected read sprocs (`ReadProjects` / `ReadProjectById` / `ReadProjectByPublicId` / `ReadProjectByName`) were applied to prod via a surgical `CREATE OR ALTER` (not a whole-base apply) after backing up all 135 `Project.Notes` rows. Verified: all 4 now return `[Notes]`, no `@ActorUserId IS NULL` bypass, UserProject scope preserved; data unchanged (135/1 Notes; OHR2 'Bluebird' alias intact); `EXEC ReadProjectByPublicId(OHR2, admin)` returns the Notes value. The repo base file already matches (U-050 `4375d82`). Original diagnosis retained below for lineage.

**Not latent — this is live on prod right now.** Surfaced while reconciling the project base file (U-050); deliberately left unfixed there to keep that unit repo-only/net-zero. Needs its own unit (prod sproc apply → own Gate 2).

**Root cause — prod's 4 project read sprocs don't return `[Notes]`.** Verified by `sys.sql_modules` read 2026-07-16: `ReadProjects` / `ReadProjectById` / `ReadProjectByPublicId` / `ReadProjectByName` are live **without** the `[Notes]` column, while `ReadProjectsByUserId`, `UpdateProjectById` and `FindProjectForInvoice` **do** have it. Why: `Project.Notes` was added to the base file 2026-05-07 (`a539ccb`), then migration `002_remove_legacy_actor_bypass.sql` (2026-05-12, `ee0388e`) was written by copying **migration 001's pre-Notes body** instead of the then-current base — applying it to prod silently reverted the 5-day-old column. Same incident class as U-037, mirrored: there the base was stale and the migration won; here the base was *ahead* and the migration reverted it.

**The data-loss chain (each link verified in code):**
1. `ReadProjectByPublicId` (live) omits `[Notes]` →
2. `entities/project/persistence/repo.py:48` — `notes=getattr(row, "Notes", None)` is a **tolerant** read → no crash, silently `notes=None` →
3. API returns `notes: null` → `build.one.web/src/pages/projects/ProjectEdit.tsx:36` — `notes: item.notes ?? ""` → textarea renders **empty even though the DB holds a value** →
4. user edits any other field and saves → `ProjectEdit.tsx:59` sends `notes: form.notes` = `""` →
5. `entities/project/business/service.py:202-203` — `if notes is not None: existing.notes = notes if notes != "" else None` → `""` → `None` →
6. `UpdateProjectById` (`entities/project/sql/dbo.project.sql:285`) — `[Notes] = @Notes` **unconditional, no `CASE WHEN` guard** → **NULL. Permanently.**

**Blast radius (as of 2026-07-16):** exactly **1 of 135** projects carries Notes — `OHR2 - 1539 Old Hillsboro Road`, holding the *"Also referred to as 'Bluebird'"* address alias. That alias is precisely what `FindProjectForInvoice` (which *does* return Notes) feeds the bill_specialist / project_specialist for invoice→project matching, so wiping it silently degrades agent invoice routing for OHR2. Low frequency (one rarely-edited row), unrecoverable when it fires. `Vendor.Notes` has 1 row and is **not** affected (the vendor read path returns the column) — but the vendor read path deserves the same check before closing this out.

- [x] **Fix — apply the U-050-corrected read sprocs to prod. DONE 2026-07-16 (U-052).** Applied the 4 read sprocs surgically (extracted from the base, `CREATE OR ALTER`, **not** a whole-base `run_sql.py` apply — the base is an unconverted entity and could carry other drift). Backed up all 135 `Project.Notes` rows first (`scratchpad/u052_project_notes_backup.json`). Verified after: `[Notes]` present on all 4, no bypass, UserProject scope intact; data unchanged (135/1; OHR2 'Bluebird' alias intact); `EXEC ReadProjectByPublicId(OHR2, admin)` returns Notes. Deliberately did NOT touch `UpdateProjectById`'s unconditional `[Notes]=@Notes` write — the read fix removes the trigger (UI now shows the real value); a CASE-WHEN guard there is a separate design call (null could be a legitimate clear). Original plan retained above.
- [ ] **Consider — `CASE WHEN` guard on `UpdateProjectById.[Notes]`** as defence-in-depth, per the repo's documented NULL-overwrite convention. Not strictly required once the read is fixed (the service does read-modify-write, so unconditional SET is the intended design there, as it is for `Name`/`Description`/`Abbreviation`) — but a guard would have made this chain un-exploitable. Weigh against the deliberate "user cleared the textarea → store NULL" behaviour at `service.py:203`, which a naive guard would break.
- [ ] **Audit the same shape elsewhere.** The trigger is a *tolerant* `getattr(row, "X", None)` mapper sitting over a sproc that may not return `X`, feeding an unconditional `SET [X] = @X`. That combination converts a schema/sproc drift into silent data loss with no error anywhere. `repo.py` uses `getattr` defensively in several entities — grep for the pattern and check each against its live sproc's column list.
- [ ] **Sibling (latent, repo-only) — `scripts/migrations/gap2_core_threading.sql` carries a DRIFTED `CreateProject` that drops `[Notes]`.** Found + verified during U-050's Pass-2 (2026-07-16): that file's `CreateProject` body has **no `@Notes` param, no `[Notes]` INSERT column, and no `INSERTED.[Notes]` OUTPUT**, vs the canonical base (`entities/project/sql/dbo.project.sql:60-89`) which has all three. Same Notes-regression class as the read-path bug above, but on the **write** path and **latent** rather than live: re-applying `gap2_core_threading.sql` would revert `CreateProject` to a body that **silently discards user-entered Notes on every project create** and drops Notes from the create response. Not fixed in U-050 (outside its 4 approved paths). Fix = reconcile that copy to the base or stub it. **NOT done by U-051 (2026-07-16) — re-tag to the next `project`-conversion unit.** U-051 turned out to be the access-UDF re-homing only; `CreateProject` is a *sproc on an unconverted entity*, and folding it in would have rebuilt exactly the blast radius U-045/U-051 exist to shrink. Still open, still latent. NB the U-050 guard (`tests/test_project_sproc_bypass_guard.py`) does **not** cover this: it is deliberately scoped to the 4 RBAC *read* sprocs, and `CreateProject` is out of that set.

## ✅ RESOLVED 2026-07-12 — Auth cross-site "hourly re-login" fix → Path B (custom API domain `api.bld-one.com`) — COMPLETE (API + web both live)

Fixes the forced-re-login-~hourly bug (web `TODO.md` Phase 0.5). **Path A (SameSite=Lax→None) was built then REVERTED**: Codex Pass-1 confirmed a **P1** — even with cookies transmitting cross-site, the CSRF double-submit can't be satisfied because the web (`app.bld-one.com`) can't read the host-only `token.csrf` cookie set on the API host to echo it as `X-CSRF-Token` → `_require_csrf` 403s. Path A only trades a 401 for a 403. Pivoted to **Path B** (same-site via shared eTLD+1 `bld-one.com`).

**Done + verified:**
- DNS (Squarespace): `api` CNAME → `buildone-…azurewebsites.net` + `asuid.api` TXT (ownership proof) — both resolve.
- `api.bld-one.com` custom hostname bound to the `buildone` App Service + free managed TLS cert (SniEnabled, thumbprint `0A44A6FB79C6336A58D861D382CCFB7AF3765E79`); serves 200.
- API code: **`1294d1f`** scopes ONLY the non-HttpOnly `token.csrf` cookie to `Domain=bld-one.com` (prod-only via the secure gate; dev host-only) so `app.bld-one.com` can read it for the double-submit; access/refresh stay HttpOnly host-only; SameSite stays `lax`. Codex Pass-1 **PASS**.
- **✅ API DEPLOYED 2026-07-11** — landed on `master` (via the `feat/model-cascade` merge) and shipped: `master` `b23cc8e` → `buildone:latest` digest `3a0ef43…`, `az webapp restart` done, prod healthy (604 routes on both `api.bld-one.com` + `azurewebsites.net`). The CSRF-domain code is LIVE but **inert until the web flips** (it only activates when the web calls `api.bld-one.com`).

**✅ WEB FLIP DONE 2026-07-12 — Path B COMPLETE.** Web `VITE_API_BASE_URL` flipped to `https://api.bld-one.com` (`build.one.web` `main` `9654743`), rebuilt + SWA-deployed to `app.bld-one.com`. Verified: the live bundle references only `api.bld-one.com` (old host gone from `dist`); CORS preflight from `app.bld-one.com` → `api.bld-one.com/auth/refresh` returns 200 with `Allow-Credentials: true` + `Allow-Origin: https://app.bld-one.com` + `Allow-Headers: x-csrf-token`. `app` + `api` now share eTLD+1 `bld-one.com` → same-site → SameSite=Lax cookies + CSRF double-submit both work. **Remaining human check:** confirm a live browser session on `app.bld-one.com` stays logged in past ~60 min (the actual hourly-logout fix). The old `azurewebsites.net` host stays live (iOS / scheduler / MCP unaffected).
- **No user regression while deferred** — the API fix is inert until the flip, so users are exactly as before (hourly logout) until the web ships.

## 🟠 OHR2-CHAPEL-09 follow-ups (2026-07-09) — code/guard items from the $925.54 double-bill debrief

Playbook side already shipped (KI-41..44, Step 3a pre-delete checklist, Delta-re-run removal checklist, Phase-1 duplicate-pair + page-audit screens). These are the code/guard halves, in priority order:

- [x] **P1 — CustomerProject mapping stability + safe Step-3a ordering.** (U-022, 2026-07-14) FIXED both halves. **Part 1 (heal-don't-delete):** the customer-sync connector's mapping-exists-but-Project-read-empty `else` branch ([integrations/intuit/qbo/customer/connector/project/business/service.py](integrations/intuit/qbo/customer/connector/project/business/service.py)) no longer deletes the mapping + falls through to a create. All LOCAL dbo/qbo mapping ops (no QBO external write, no `ALLOW_QBO_WRITES` path). Verify: **138 pytest green** incl. new `tests/test_qbo_customer_project_heal.py` (10 pure-logic tests, mocked repos). ## ✅ RESOLVED (2026-07-09) — `ms.outbox.upload.link_failed` was a dead DB write-back, not a SharePoint upload failure

**Root cause (corrects the original framing).** The SharePoint upload *succeeds*; the failure is the non-fatal DB write-back that links the uploaded file to its Attachment. `ms.DriveItemAttachment.MsDriveItemId` is `BIGINT` (FK → `ms.DriveItem.Id`, a local PK), but both callers — `MsOutboxWorker._handle_upload_sharepoint_file` and `BillCreditCompleteService` — passed `uploaded_item["id"]`, the **Graph DriveItem ID string**. `DriveItemAttachmentConnector.link_driveitem_to_attachment` then runs `read_by_ms_driveitem_id(<string>)` against a BIGINT column → SQL type error → `DatabaseOperationError`, thrown before the guarded `create()` and swallowed by the caller. Failed **100% of the time**; never worked (Feb code). No `ms.DriveItem` record is created on the upload path either.

**Impact was nil.** The `ms.DriveItemAttachment` mapping is **write-only — nothing reads it** (`get_driveitem_for_attachment`/`get_attachment_for_driveitem` have zero callers). Only effect was one swallowed `DatabaseOperationError` per SharePoint upload polluting `AppExceptions`. No lost documents, no retry loop, no drain failures. (The earlier "retry loop / failed drains / wasted Flex" framing was the ACR-incident backlog, unrelated; and the `DatabaseOperationError` type was *correct*, not a mislabel — it genuinely is a DB error from bad input.)

**Fix applied (Option 1):** removed the dead link-back calls in `integrations/ms/outbox/business/worker.py` (+ the now-orphaned `_link_attachment_if_requested` method) and `entities/bill_credit/business/complete_service.py`. Noise gone, zero functional loss.

- [ ] **Optional cleanup (low priority):** `DriveItemAttachmentConnector` + the `ms.DriveItemAttachment` table/repo/sprocs are now fully orphaned (no callers). Drop them in a dedicated schema-cleanup change if desired; left in place here to keep this fix migration-free. If per-attachment SharePoint links are ever needed, build it properly (upsert `ms.DriveItem` → link by PK) **with** its consumer.

## Box outbox drain — hardening (2026-07-08)

Surfaced by a `drain_box_outbox` alert during a transient Box **503 `unavailable`** window on `box.excel.upload_version`. Box recovered on its own and nothing was lost, but the incident exposed two config smells that will re-bite on the next slow-Box spell:

- [x] **Retry budget is self-defeating for Excel uploads.** A single hung `upload_version` attempt ran ~40s before Box returned 503, which exhausted the ~40s retry budget in *one* attempt (`remaining_budget_seconds=0.0`) — so a genuinely transient blip dead-letters with effectively zero retries. Either raise the Excel-upload retry budget or cap the per-request timeout so one slow response doesn't consume the whole budget. (`integrations/box/base/retry.py::execute_with_retry`.) **(FIXED U-019 `8440b1f` — `RetryPolicy.for_uploads()`, budget 400s = 3×130s tier-C ceiling + 10s headroom, applied to `upload_file`+`upload_file_version`.)**
- [x] **Retry budget is self-defeating for Box downloads too (U-019 out-of-scope sibling).** `download_file` (tier-C, 120s per-request) delegated to `_execute_raw` which hardcoded `RetryPolicy.for_reads()` (30s budget), so one slow download attempt blew the whole budget on attempt 1 and zero-retry dead-lettered — the same defect, and downloads legitimately retry (Box 202 + Retry-After "not yet available" is raised as `BoxServiceUnavailableError` for the retry layer). **(FIXED U-020 — `RetryPolicy.for_downloads()`, budget 400s = 3×130s tier-C ceiling + 10s headroom; `max_attempts=3` mirrors uploads to bound the shared `box_outbox_drain` lock hold; only `_execute_raw`'s policy changed, JSON reads/writes untouched. Differential test `tests/test_box_retry_download_budget.py`.)**
- [ ] **30s drain cadence vs ~40s lock hold → residual lock-timeout log noise.** (2026-07-14 recon: the CORE ask is ALREADY met — `outbox/business/worker.py` `drain_once`/`drain_all` treat a lock-busy tick as a clean zero-count skip and the endpoint returns 200, so it no longer inflates `buildone-sched-failed-invocations`.) **Residual only:** downgrade the leftover `box.lock.timeout` WARNING → debug at `integrations/box/base/locking.py:82-90` — a lock-busy tick means "another tick is still working," not an error worth a WARNING.

## ✅ FIXED (deployed a241b73, 2026-07-09) — QBO sync HTTP path runs without system-admin authz context → poison-batch retry loop (2026-07-08)

**Fix:** made `_require_drain_secret` an `async` dependency (`shared/api/admin.py`) so `set_authz_context(is_system_admin=True)` runs in the request coroutine's context instead of a discarded anyio threadpool worker; it now propagates through `_timed`'s `asyncio.to_thread` into the sync. Deployed image `2143bf9b` / `:latest`. **Verified:** post-deploy `access-denied=0` (was: every row), purchase sync `success=True failed_count=0`, purchase watermark advanced `02:37→11:38`. Covers all four project-scoped syncs + reconcile.

Also deployed `4b13d1f`: **`?attachments=false` query param on `/admin/sync/qbo/{entity}`** (bill/vendorcredit) — runs an attachment-free pass to help clear a slow backlog; no-op elsewhere.

### 🟠 OPEN follow-up — bound the QBO bill/purchase pull so a backlog drains under the 240s gateway cap

Verification exposed a **pre-existing, separate** problem (NOT the authz bug — post-fix `access-denied=0`): a bill backlog can't clear via HTTP. As of 2026-07-09 ~12:55 UTC the `bill` watermark is still frozen at `02:31` with **~60 bills** pending (grew from 29 over the day as new QBO modifications accumulate on the held watermark). **All 60 are already fully synced** (verified: BillLineItems + `qbo.BillLineItemBillLine` + `qbo.BillBill`), so the loop does *no useful work* — it just reprocesses complete bills every 15 min, 504s at Azure's 240s gateway before the end-of-run watermark update, and never advances. Confirmed a full pass exceeds 240s **even with `attachments=false`** (60 bills × module-upsert + per-project Excel + per-bill SharePoint + Box enqueue). Harmless (no data loss; no brownout now that access + purchase + drains are healthy) but wasteful — pins one of two workers ~240s every 15 min + re-hits QBO. **Chris's call 2026-07-09: leave the loop; build Part A as a reviewed+tested change (do NOT blind-deploy; do NOT hand-advance the watermark).**

**Part A spec — bounded pull (needs testing against real QBO before deploy):**
- **Complication found:** the `QboBill` mirror does **not** carry `Metadata.LastUpdatedTime` (it's dropped in the external→mirror mapping — see [integrations/intuit/qbo/bill/external/schemas.py:121](integrations/intuit/qbo/bill/external/schemas.py) `metadata` vs [.../business/model.py](integrations/intuit/qbo/bill/business/model.py)). The watermark filter is strictly `>` ([external/client.py:153](integrations/intuit/qbo/bill/external/client.py)) and QBO returns bills **ordered by Id, not LastUpdatedTime**. So a naïve "cap N + advance watermark to the Nth bill's timestamp" is **unsafe** — it'd silently skip any bill sharing that same second.
- **Two safe implementation options:**
  1. **QBO-native pagination:** add `ORDER BY Metadata.LastUpdatedTime` + `MAXRESULTS=<cap>` to the pull query; capture the page's max `LastUpdatedTime` from the raw response; advance the watermark to it only after processing the *complete* same-timestamp group (never split a second). Bounds ALL per-bill work, not just attachments.
  2. **Idempotent-skip convergence (no watermark-semantics change):** in the per-bill loop, skip bills already fully synced (existing `qbo.BillBill` + line-maps) so re-runs get cheap; with per-bill commits persisting partial progress, a run eventually processes only the un-synced tail + skips the rest → completes < 240s → watermark advances to now normally. Lower data-loss risk (worst case = redundant work, not skipped bills); still needs the un-synced tail to fit one run.
- Add `SYNC_BILL_MAX_PER_RUN` (env, default preserves today's behavior for small batches). Mirror into `sync_qbo_purchase`. **Cannot be verified from a dev laptop** (no `ENCRYPTION_KEY`/QBO creds) — verify in the deploy env or a QBO sandbox.
- **Interim clear (when someone's in the deploy env):** `python scripts/sync_qbo_bill.py --skip-attachments` (no gateway cap, runs as system-admin via `__main__`) drains the backlog in one pass and advances the watermark — safe, no DB surgery. **DONE 2026-07-09 13:46 UTC** — ran the CLI `--skip-attachments`, 56 bills module-synced, `failed_count=0`, watermark advanced `02:31 → 13:46`; the failing-loop / `sched-failed-invocations` noise is cleared. (Attachment sync is idempotent — reuses existing Attachment rows by QboAttachable mapping — so a `--skip` run strands nothing and no cruft is created.)
- **Dominant cost = attachable full-realm prefetch (fix this in Part A).** `QboAttachableService.sync_attachables_for_bill` pulls the **entire realm Attachable list (~11k+ rows, paginated 1000/page, several minutes)** into a per-run cache ([integrations/intuit/qbo/attachable/business/service.py:101-105](integrations/intuit/qbo/attachable/business/service.py)) even to sync a handful of bills — this one-time prefetch is the main reason a with-attachments pass blows past 240s. The client already has a filtered `query_attachables_for_entity(entity_type, entity_id)` ([external/client.py:213](integrations/intuit/qbo/attachable/external/client.py)); use it per-bill for small incremental batches instead of prefetching all 11k.

## 🟠 P0 — QBO→dbo bill onboarding minted header-only "zombie" bills in prod (2026-07-07) — CODE FIX SHIPPED (U-006), deploy + data-repair pending

**Update 2026-07-13 (U-006):** compensating rollback shipped in code (pending Gate-2 deploy). The line-failure *source* was the authz bug already fixed + deployed as `a241b73` (see the ✅ FIXED section above); U-006 adds the missing *defense* so any FUTURE permanent line failure can no longer strand a header-only bill. Two items remain open below: **deploy the code fix** and **repair the 2 remaining live zombies** (separate owner-gated prod step).

Surfaced running the BR-MAIN-26 InvoiceAgent playbook: **30 `dbo.Bill` rows were created 2026-07-07 19:16–21:32 UTC as headers only — correct `TotalAmount` + `qbo.BillBill` parent map, but ZERO `dbo.BillLineItem` rows.** Only 4 bills created that day had line items. The invoice's 22 source bills were mostly in this state, so nothing linked/reconciled until repaired.

- **Root cause / mechanism.** `BillBillConnector.sync_from_qbo_bill` ([integrations/intuit/qbo/bill/connector/bill/business/service.py](integrations/intuit/qbo/bill/connector/bill/business/service.py)) commits the header (`BillService.create`) + `qbo.BillBill` mapping in separate service-layer transactions, then calls `_sync_line_items`, which **raises** if any line fails to project. The raise leaves a committed header + parent-map with no lines and no `qbo.BillLineItemBillLine` — a zombie. **Line creation works fine from the current local checkout** (re-ran `BillLineItemConnector.sync_from_qbo_bill_line` per line → clean BLI with SCC + Price + Project every time), so the failure is **prod-side**: the deployed API code (or its container tag — see `docs/runbooks/deploy-tag-pinned.md`) is behind, throwing on the line-create path. **The prod scheduler's periodic bill sync will keep producing zombies until the prod image is redeployed.** First action: diff deployed image vs `main`, redeploy `:latest`, confirm a fresh QBO bill onboards WITH lines.
- [x] **Compensating rollback (U-006, 2026-07-13).** `sync_from_qbo_bill` NEW-bill path now wraps `_sync_line_items` — on any raise it deletes the just-created header (`bill_service.delete_by_public_id`) + `qbo.BillBill` mapping, then re-raises (watermark holds; next re-pull rebuilds cleanly, idempotent). Shared helper `integrations/intuit/qbo/base/compensation.py::rollback_orphan_header`. Same fix mirrored into the two same-root-cause siblings: Purchase→Expense + VendorCredit→BillCredit. Existing-mapping re-sync branches deliberately NOT rolled back. Tests: `tests/test_qbo_zombie_rollback.py` (4). Codex xhigh review PASS. **⛔ Pending Gate-2 deploy** (`az acr build … buildone:latest` → restart).
- [ ] **[OPS]** **Repair the 2 remaining live zombie bills** — separate owner-gated Gate-2 prod-data step, AFTER the code fix deploys (down from 14; most already repaired). Detection query:
  ```sql
  SELECT b.Id, b.BillNumber, b.TotalAmount, v.Name
  FROM dbo.Bill b LEFT JOIN dbo.Vendor v ON v.Id = b.VendorId
  WHERE NOT EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM dbo.BillLineItem WHERE BillId = b.Id)
    AND EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM qbo.BillBill bb WHERE bb.BillId = b.Id);
  ```
  Still-broken as of 2026-07-07 (all `qboMap=1`, 0 lines): `19271` Ferguson #SC80489 $120.15 · `19290` Integrated Power #14714 $29,632.50 · `19291` Liaison Home Automation #9953 $24,358.81 · `19292` The Clean Living Co #794 $9,413.56 · `19293` The Iron Grove #2480 $9,359.00 · `19294` SledgeCraft #107836 $1,293.82 · `19295` SledgeCraft #107840 $493.26 · `19296` Siteworks #26-0161 $53,577.25 · `19297` Green Acres #21028 $28,000.00 · `19299` SavATree #001927012 $406.00 · `19300` Southern Staircase #ISA000010726 $906.95 · `19301` Cobra #C10659 $2,182.50 · `19302` Alley-Cassetty #000655919 $28.32 · `19303` Walker Lumber #226574 $615.31.
  **Repair recipe** (per zombie bill, from the current checkout, under `set_authz_context(is_system_admin=True)`): resolve `dbo.Bill.Id` via `qbo.BillBill.BillId`, then for each `qbo.BillLine` on the mapped `qbo.Bill` call `BillLineItemConnector().sync_from_qbo_bill_line(dbo_bill_id, qbo_line)` — it creates the BLI (SCC from ItemRef, Price=Amount, Project from CustomerRef) **and** the `qbo.BillLineItemBillLine` mapping. Verify each bill's `SUM(BLI.Amount) == Bill.TotalAmount` afterward. These are QBO-onboarded bills → lines project but **attachments do not**; only pull QBO attachables if/when a bill is invoiced (CRITICAL #5). Draft-collision variant (a promoted email-intake draft already holds the number) → Step 3b.i of the invoice playbook.

## Structured Alias entities — ProjectAlias, VendorAlias, SubCostCodeAlias, CostCodeAlias (2026-06-26)

Live runs kept tripping on entity resolution across all four parent types:
- **Project**: invoices reference projects by ship-to address, homeowner name, marketing nickname ("Bluebird"), or street-number-with-letter-suffix (`7550C Buffalo` vs `7550 Buffalo Rd`) — none of which line up cleanly with `Project.Name`. Current workaround is the Bluebird→OHR2 line we hand-appended to `Project.Notes` yesterday.
- **Vendor**: senders use DBA names, abbreviations, parent-company names, and divisional brands — e.g., emails from `accounting@leebp.com` say "Lee Masonry Products" but the vendor row is "Lee Building Products"; Cobra invoices come from `stacey@cobrallc.net` (FromName "Stacey Downs" — the AR clerk, not the vendor name).
- **SubCostCode**: reviewers and vendors reference SCCs by old numbering (`62.0` vs canonical `62.00`), name shorthand ("trim labor" → 44.00 Trim Labor), or vendor-internal codes ("HVAC rough" → 25.00 HVAC). `SubCostCode.Aliases` is a single comma-separated string today — brittle and lacks provenance.
- **CostCode**: same story one level up — division shorthand ("trade labor", "site work") needs structured lookup.

All four currently rely on either free-text Notes / Aliases columns or substring-fuzz against the canonical Name. That's brittle: no provenance, no confidence, no way to record what an alias originated from. **One unified entity pattern** across all four parents — same shape, same agent tool family, same UI affordance.

**Proposed shape (template — applied to all four entities):**
```
entities/{project|vendor|sub_cost_code|cost_code}_alias/
  api/router.py
  business/service.py
  persistence/repo.py
  sql/dbo.{project|vendor|sub_cost_code|cost_code}_alias.sql
  intelligence/tools.py  -> record_{parent}_alias(parent, type, value, source, confidence)
```

`dbo.{Parent}Alias` table (one per parent):
- `Id BIGINT PK`, `PublicId UUID`, `RowVersion`, `CreatedDatetime`, `ModifiedDatetime`, `CompanyId`, `CreatedByUserId`
- `{Parent}Id BIGINT FK NOT NULL`
- `Type NVARCHAR(40) NOT NULL` — per-parent enum (see "Type vocabularies" below)
- `Value NVARCHAR(200) NOT NULL` — the actual hint string
- `NormalizedValue NVARCHAR(200) NOT NULL` — case-folded, punctuation-stripped, persisted for lookup
- `Source NVARCHAR(40) NOT NULL` — `manual` | `agent_inferred` | `vendor_supplied`
- `Confidence DECIMAL(5,4) NULL` — only populated when `Source = 'agent_inferred'`
- `IsActive BIT NOT NULL DEFAULT 1` — soft-delete for retired aliases
- Filtered unique index `UQ_{Parent}Alias_Value_Type_Parent_Active` on `(NormalizedValue, Type, {Parent}Id)` where `IsActive = 1`

**Type vocabularies (per parent):**
- **ProjectAlias.Type**: `address_alias` | `nickname` | `vendor_referent` | `homeowner_name` | `mailing_label` | `lot_number`
- **VendorAlias.Type**: `dba_name` | `abbreviation` | `division_brand` | `parent_company` | `vendor_internal_code` | `domain_alias`
- **SubCostCodeAlias.Type**: `legacy_number` | `name_variant` | `vendor_referent` | `reviewer_shorthand`
- **CostCodeAlias.Type**: `legacy_number` | `name_variant` | `division_shorthand`

**Upgrade the `Find*ForInvoice` sproc family** to JOIN against the new alias tables and score structured matches HIGHER than the existing free-text fuzz:
- `1.00` `alias_exact` — `{Parent}Alias.NormalizedValue` exactly matches lookup chunk
- `0.95` `alias_substring` — alias is a substring of the lookup hint
- `0.90` existing `name`/`abbreviation` exact (current top-of-rankings)
- lower tiers — existing prefix/substring fuzz (kept as fallback)

For SubCostCode + CostCode lookup, upgrade `FindSubCostCodeForReply` similarly: alias_exact > number > name > current alias-string LIKE fuzz.

**Agent tools** (one per parent — same handler shape):
- `record_project_alias(project_public_id, type, value, source, confidence)`
- `record_vendor_alias(vendor_public_id, type, value, source, confidence)`
- `record_sub_cost_code_alias(sub_cost_code_public_id, type, value, source, confidence)`
- `record_cost_code_alias(cost_code_public_id, type, value, source, confidence)`

When an agent resolves a parent via fuzzy match + the user later confirms (via [Review] reply approving the draft Bill / line coding), the agent auto-persists the fragment as `agent_inferred` with the resolved confidence. Next invoice referencing the same fragment hits `alias_exact` instead of fuzz — the agent gets compounding-smarter every time AP confirms a match. Manual UI entries → `Source='manual'`, `Confidence=NULL`.

**Why a child entity per parent, not just more columns:**
- A parent commonly has 3-10+ aliases (e.g., Cobra LLC: dba "Cobra", domain "cobrallc.net", AR name "Stacey Downs", etc.).
- Per-row provenance (`Source`, `Confidence`) doesn't fit into a single column.
- Soft-deletable so retired aliases (e.g., DBA renamed) don't bleed into matches.

**Backfill plan:**
- Migrate the Bluebird→OHR2 alias we hand-added to `Project.Notes` yesterday into a real `ProjectAlias` row (`Type='nickname'`, `Source='manual'`).
- Migrate the existing `SubCostCode.Aliases` comma-separated string into `SubCostCodeAlias` rows (one per token, `Type='name_variant'`, `Source='manual'`). Then drop the column on a follow-up migration.
- Future cleanup pass: parse `Vendor.Notes` + `Project.Notes` free-text on every row, surface alias-shaped lines for human review-then-promote.

**Mechanical pieces (per parent — 4× the same work):**
- New entity package (mirror Contact's child-of-parent shape, but single-parent FK).
- New module row per alias entity + RBAC grants on parent's module roles.
- React surface: inline section on parent edit page, similar to `<InlineContacts />` component → `<InlineAliases parentEntity="project" parentId={...} />`.
- Migration: `scripts/migrations/alias_entities_create.sql` covering all four tables + indexes + the `Find*ForInvoice` sproc upgrades + `SubCostCode.Aliases` backfill.

Driven by: 2026-06-26 live-run friction where parser missed project on 4 of 7 vendor invoices (project lookup) + missed vendor on 3 of 3 Cobra invoices (vendor lookup keyed on sender name not company name). Today's lookups are binary (match canonical Name substring or fail); a structured hint layer makes the agent compounding-smart across all four parent types (every successful match teaches next time's lookup). Apply the pattern uniformly so the alias-handling shape is one mental model, not four.

## BillView page load is slow (2026-06-25)

User reported: clicking the "View Bill in build.one →" button in an agent confirmation forward (Bill 25801, public_id `46F24E84-A149-421E-A232-BFE037C4E3EC`) landed on the right URL after the PWA service-worker cache was cleared, but the page took noticeably long to render the data details (Bill header, attachment image, line items, etc.). UX friction; not a correctness bug.

Likely culprits (need diagnostic data before optimizing):
- BillView fires multiple serialized API calls (header → line items → attachments → reviews → enrichment lookups) — classic React-page waterfall.
- Each individual endpoint may also be slow if heavy joins (e.g., `/api/v1/get/bill/{public_id}` JOIN to vendor + payment_term + project lookups; `/api/v1/get/bill-line-items/...` JOIN to sub_cost_code + attachment thumbnails; the attachment image fetch from blob storage is a separate round-trip).
- Initial React render may be heavy if BillView renders synchronously instead of streaming sections.

Next action: ask user to open DevTools → Network tab on the next BillView load + share the top 2–3 slow endpoints (or screenshot of the waterfall). 10 seconds of inspection saves hours of optimizing the wrong thing. Once we know which endpoint or which serial round-trip is the long pole, options are:
- **(a) One slow endpoint** → server-side fix (denormalized join, missing index, or N+1 inside the sproc).
- **(b) Too many serialized round-trips** → parallelize with `Promise.all` (web side) OR add a `/api/v1/get/bill/{public_id}/full` endpoint that returns Bill + lines + attachments + reviews in one envelope.
- **(c) Heavy initial render** → split BillView into independently-suspending sections (`<Suspense>` per section) so the page chrome renders immediately and each detail card streams as its data arrives.

Lives in build.one.web (BillView page) + potentially build.one.api (denormalized read endpoint or sproc tightening). Cross-repo coordination needed once the diagnostic narrows the fix surface.

## TimeEntry auto-submit (deterministic prior-day sweep) — follow-ups (2026-06-23)

Shipped: `TimeEntryAutoSubmitService` + `POST /api/v1/admin/time-tracking/auto-submit-prior-day` (DRAIN_SECRET-gated, `TIME_AUTOSUBMIT_MODE` off|dry_run|on) + scheduler timer `auto_submit_prior_day` (18:00 UTC = noon CST). Deterministic submit-if-clean / flag-else for the prior day; excludes test/agent + duplicates. Adversarial review applied the Id-tie-break, business-tz `today`, NULL-status guard, actions cap, sargable WorkDate predicate. Deferred:

- [x] **(medium) Root-cause `Id` tie-break on the shared status-read sprocs. DONE 2026-07-14 (U-015).** `TimeEntryStatus.CreatedDatetime` is DATETIME2(3); `reject()` writes `rejected` then `draft` in two `SYSUTCDATETIME()` calls that can collide at ms → non-deterministic "current status" (a reject→draft entry could read as `rejected`, so `submit()._validate_transition` threw a spurious transition error — caught + counted as `errors`, no bad write). Fixed in canonical `dbo.time_entry.sql` + new migration `015_status_read_id_tiebreak.sql` (byte-consistent): appended `, [Id] DESC` to the three current-status `TOP 1` picks — the `ReadTimeEntriesPaginated` + `CountTimeEntries` correlated `OUTER APPLY` subqueries and `ReadCurrentTimeEntryStatus` (higher Id = later-inserted `draft` = true current status) — and `, [Id] ASC` to the `ReadTimeEntryStatusesByTimeEntryId` ascending history list. ORDER-BY-only; zero logic/row-set/result-shape change. Historical migration copies (001/002/011/013/014) left untouched (applied + superseded by the base `CREATE OR ALTER`). Pass-1 Codex gpt-5.5/xhigh PASS 0 findings; 113 pytest green; Pass-2 skipped (trivial ORDER-BY diff). **Deploy = SQL-only:** apply `015_status_read_id_tiebreak.sql` via `scripts/run_sql.py` (no container rebuild — no Python changed).
- [x] **(medium, follow-up from U-037) Reconcile drifted TimeEntry migration files 014 + 001 + 002 so re-running any is a safe no-op. DONE 2026-07-16 (U-039).** *(Original U-037 diagnosis retained below for context.)* Migration `014_2026_07_01_read_time_entries_sort_by_worker.sql` `CREATE OR ALTER`d the WRONG object.** Its header/PRINT say "ReadTimeEntries" but its body is the *paginated* worker-sort list; the live web list calls `ReadTimeEntriesPaginated` (via `repo.read_paginated`), while the `ReadTimeEntries` name it targets is only reachable from the **dead** `repo.read_all()` (no router/tool/script caller). Two consequences: (a) the `@SortBy='Worker'` feature never reached the live list sproc — it only "works" via the trailing `u.Lastname, u.Firstname` tie-break; (b) migration 014's `ReadTimeEntries` body is UNSCOPED (no `@ActorUserId/@ActorIsSystemAdmin/@ActorCanViewTeam`) — re-running 014 standalone after U-037 would re-drift `ReadTimeEntries` back to unscoped + wrong shape (the exact U-037 landmine class; `read_all()` would then 500 SQL 8144 if ever wired up). U-037 reconciled the base file's `ReadTimeEntries` to the scoped/authoritative version, so this is **latent** (dead sproc + base-wins-if-run-last), NOT a live regression. Fix: either delete 014 (worker-sort already effectively holds via the tail) or re-point it to `ReadTimeEntriesPaginated` with the actor-scope params + `[Id]` tie-break. Discovered during U-037 Pass-1 (Codex). **RESOLVED — U-039 (2026-07-16), repo-only (NOT applied to prod — base already carries the canonical scoped state):** 014 now defines ONLY the scoped 3-param `dbo.ReadTimeEntries` (byte-identical to base). Extended the same unit to the two OTHER re-drift hazards — `001_phase3_scope_by_user.sql` + `002_remove_legacy_actor_bypass.sql` each redefined the 16 TimeEntry/TimeLog/TimeEntryStatus read+mutation sprocs in the STALE 2-param form (pre-`@ActorCanViewTeam`), so re-running either reverts the live 3-param signature → SQL 8144 on list/count; both now carry a prominent SUPERSEDED banner + all 16 sprocs replaced with the canonical 3-param scoped bodies copied verbatim from `dbo.time_entry.sql`, making a naive re-run idempotent, not a revert. Audit: only 001/002/014 were scoping-revert hazards — 013/012/010 create NEW-name batch/aux sprocs (`ReadCurrentTimeEntryStatusesByTimeEntryIds` etc.) that never carried actor params, so re-running them neither reverts scoping nor is dropped by a base re-run (safe; they're migration-only base-file gaps, tracked separately). Evidence = per-sproc byte-match table (all 16 + `ReadTimeEntries` == base 1:1). Write=Composer `composer-2.5-fast`; Pass-1 review=Codex `gpt-5.5` xhigh INDEPENDENT → PASS, 0 findings; Pass-2 = no simplification surface (verbatim base copies are byte-locked; GO separators match base convention). 209 pytest green. Worker `@SortBy='Worker'` sort intentionally DEFERRED (see sub-bullet).
  - [x] **Follow-up (low, deferred from U-039): complete the `@SortBy='Worker'` list sort. DONE 2026-07-16 (U-042).** *(Original diagnosis retained:)* The web `TimeEntryList` "Worker" column header is a live sortable control with a ▲/▼ direction toggle, but the scoped `ReadTimeEntriesPaginated` (the sproc `/time-entries` actually calls) has no `@SortBy='Worker'` ORDER-BY branch — it falls through to `u.Lastname ASC, u.Firstname ASC`, so the toggle is **direction-inert** (Worker+DESC still sorts ascending). **RESOLVED — U-042 (2026-07-16), SQL-only (needs a prod apply of migration 016; no code deploy — `entities/time_entry/api/router.py:141-142` already passes `sort_by`/`sort_direction` through).** Added the 4 signature-preserving `CASE WHEN @SortDirection=… AND @SortBy='Worker' THEN u.[Lastname]/[Firstname] END` branches, inserted before the trailing fallback. **Scope widened at Gate-1 (Chris) from the planned 3 definitions to 5**: U-039's bodies-match reconciliation had left `001_phase3_scope_by_user.sql` + `002_remove_legacy_actor_bypass.sql` carrying byte-identical `ReadTimeEntriesPaginated` copies, so a base-only edit would have re-drifted them (the CLAUDE.md "future base change must re-port them" residual). All five bodies are byte-identical again (3219 chars / 83 lines, verified programmatically) → re-running ANY of the four migrations is a safe no-op. **Pass-1 (Codex `gpt-5.5` xhigh, INDEPENDENT of the writer) returned CHANGES-REQUESTED on one P2, confirmed by the EM and fixed in-unit:** the Worker sort ties **every** row of a given worker on all ORDER-BY keys, so `OFFSET/FETCH` pagination was non-deterministic (a row could duplicate or vanish across pages). Pre-existing in kind — but sharply amplified: a `WorkDate` tie-class is one worker-day (usually 1 row) whereas a `Worker` tie-class is *all* of one worker's entries, and at `page_size=50` any worker with 50+ entries has a tie-class guaranteed to straddle a page boundary. Fix = appended `te.[Id] ASC` (TimeEntry PK) as a final total-order key in all 5 bodies — same shape as migration 015's own `s.[Id] DESC` precedent. ## QBO pull-race guard — follow-ups (2026-06-23)

Shipped this unit: `integrations/intuit/qbo/base/pull_race.py` (`guard_lines_present` + `read_lines_riding_out_race` + `header_has_amount`) wired into all 3 QBO connectors (Bill/Purchase/VendorCredit), the 3 production pull scripts, and the backfill — fixes the cross-process pull-race that minted half-built (header-only / missing-line) entities under load. Connector raises a last-resort guard; pull scripts re-read past the race then DEFER (skip → watermark advances, no stall). Deferred:

- [x] ~~**No QBO reconciler for Purchase/VendorCredit.**~~ Shipped 2026-07-13 (U-007). `ReconciliationService` now has `reconcile_purchases` (Purchase→Expense) and `reconcile_vendor_credits` (VendorCredit→BillCredit), each mirroring `reconcile_bills`' two detectors: a `qbo_missing_locally` auto-fix (gated on `QBO_RECONCILE_PURCHASE_AUTOFIX` / `QBO_RECONCILE_VENDORCREDIT_AUTOFIX`, default off — counts + one low-severity summary when off) and a `qbo_voided` FLAG-only detector. Added `QboVendorCreditService.upsert_from_external` (VC lacked the persist→project seam Bill/Purchase already had). Daily `/reconcile/qbo` (`shared/api/admin.py`) + the in-proc scheduler fallback (`shared/scheduler.py`) now call all three, each failure-isolated so one entity's failure can't abort the others. Tests: `tests/test_qbo_reconcile_purchase_vendorcredit.py` (autofix on/off, voided-flag, detector isolation). Codex PASS; 73 pytest green.
- [x] **Half-built cleanup DECIDED + bill-dups DONE (2026-06-23).** Sweep: 141 half-built Bills + 286 Expenses. **0 customers over-billed** (every invoiced dup referenced only the keeper line) → INTERNAL cleanup, no customer-facing corrections. **Bill DUP cleanup done**: `scripts/cleanup_halfbuilt_dups.py` (95 bills / 101 dup lines, balanced, reversible backup `/tmp/halfbuilt_dup_backup.jsonl`, adversarial review wf_48d75cc2 caught+fixed 4 defects incl. the ContractLabor-FK budget-double-count). Keeper=enriched/invoiced, orphan=raw; sub-cases unmapped-orphan(122)/both-mapped-staging-dup(38).

## QBO invoice phantom fix — follow-ups (2026-06-23)

Shipped: gap-detect/adopt in `InvoiceInvoiceConnector.sync_from_qbo_invoice` (stops minting phantom `-N` duplicate invoices) + don't-swallow `create_mapping` failure (rollback the orphan) + a one-time cleanup of 46 empty phantom headers. Adversarial review (wf_518ad3e3 → wf_4736eef0) hardened it: adopt only on a header **fingerprint** (total within $0.01 + non-blank matching date), never re-sync lines onto an already-populated invoice, loud rollback-failure. Deferred:

- [ ] **(P2, pre-existing) UPDATE-path `_sync_line_items` double-adds when line mappings are lost.** `sync_from_qbo_invoice`'s mapped-UPDATE branch re-projects QBO lines unconditionally; if an invoice's `InvoiceLineItemInvoiceLine` line-mappings are gone but the lines are source-backed (Bill/Expense, not Manual), the Manual-only fingerprinter can't match them → every QBO line is re-created as a phantom Manual duplicate. Pre-existing (not introduced by the phantom fix; the fingerprint guard makes a *wrong*-invoice adopt that triggers this ≈impossible). Fix: in the line connector, when a QBO line has no line-mapping AND no Manual fingerprint match, don't create a Manual dup on an invoice that already carries source-backed lines for that QBO line — reconcile against the source FKs or skip + warn.
## QBO cross-entity attachables P1 (2026-06-23)

- [x] **Inline same-entity fix DONE + deployed (commit a35c033, build ca6d).** `_query_attachables_with_fallback` now pulls the full realm list once (cached per sync run) + exact (type,id) filter — fixes the unsupported-WHERE 200-trust + the single-page (>1000) miss that produced false "no attachments" on pull. Unit-tested.
- [ ] **Historical backfill of the 1,448 Invoice-keyed attachables → source lines is NOT viable by filename fingerprint (proven via dry-run: 2/1448 resolve).** 1,013 have opaque filenames (UUIDs / `invoices/rogers-build-inc/<uuid>.pdf` email-intake uploads) with no metadata; 384 have an amount that matches no source line (markup/partial/different total); 36 already have a doc. Forcing it risks mis-linking a receipt onto the wrong customer-packet line. Options if revisited: (a) accept as not-auto-recoverable (docs remain in QBO; inline fix prevents future same-entity loss); (b) link them to the local INVOICE (InvoiceAttachment) — faithful to QBO, no ambiguity, but doesn't flow to the per-line packet without extending the packet generator; (c) OCR the blobs to extract amount/vendor (heavy, still ambiguous). Going-forward cross-entity coverage = the deferred daily all-realm reconcile timer (Component 3, build.one.scheduler).
- [ ] **ROUND BACK: attachable file-integrity + data-integrity initiative (the 1,448 + beyond).** Filename-fingerprint can't recover the historical 1,448, but they shouldn't just be written off — treat as a dedicated integrity pass when we revisit:
  - **File integrity:** verify each of the 1,448 (and ideally all 3,301 staged attachables) has a healthy, downloadable blob — exists in QBO (FileAccessUri/TempDownloadUri live), download succeeds, sha/size matches, and where already linked locally the blob in our storage is present + uncorrupted (reuse `AzureBlobStorage.exists()` / the Step-3 health-filter pattern). Surface dead/missing blobs.
  - **Data integrity (linkage):** build a proper attachable↔source-line reconciliation beyond filename amount-match — candidate signals: QBO `LinkedTxn` traversal (Invoice→source Bill/Purchase), OCR-extracted vendor+amount+doc# from the blob, and a human-review queue for the residual ambiguous set. Produce a report of **doc-less source lines** (the inverse view: which BillLineItem/ExpenseLineItem/BillCreditLineItem feeding completed invoices have zero attachment join rows) so packets' missing-doc exposure is visible even before recovery.
  - **Going-forward:** the deferred daily all-realm reconcile timer (Component 3, build.one.scheduler) to catch new cross-entity docs.
  - Decision framing for that pass: accept-as-QBO-only vs link-to-Invoice (faithful, needs packet-generator extension) vs OCR-resolve-to-line. Internal-only (no customer over-billing); prioritize the doc-less-source-line report first (cheap, high signal).
- [ ] **Component 3 — cross-entity attachable reconcile (daily timer; cross-repo).** Concept: every QBO doc is attached to ONE entity via `EntityRef (Type, Value)`. *Same-entity* docs (receipt on its own Bill) are now captured inline (the deployed fix). *Cross-entity* docs are attached to a DIFFERENT entity than they document — the 1,448 receipts attached to the customer **Invoice** that actually belong to an underlying **Bill/Expense/BillCredit** line. Per-entity sync structurally can't find these (it asks "what's on this Bill?" and the receipt is on the Invoice), so those source lines look doc-less and packets ship without them. Fix = a **whole-realm reconcile**: pull `query_all_attachables()` once (~3.3k rows), and for each Invoice-keyed attachable resolve the underlying source line + link the blob via the existing linkers. **Why a daily timer, not the per-entity loop:** it's a whole-realm question (can't be derived from one entity's id); per-entity would be N attachable-queries × the QBO 500-req/min ceiling, whereas one paginated all-list pull amortizes cheaply and is idempotent (`_upsert_attachable`/`sync_from_qbo_attachable` are hash/qbo_id-keyed). Shape: new `build.one.api` admin endpoint (e.g. `/admin/reconcile/qbo/attachables`, DRAIN_SECRET-gated) + a low-frequency timer in `build.one.scheduler`. **Blocked on the resolution problem** (above): filename fingerprint only resolved 2/1448, so this needs the better resolver (LinkedTxn / OCR / review-queue) from the file/data-integrity round-back first — the timer is just the delivery vehicle.
- [x] **(P1, from audit) SCC back-fill DONE (2026-06-23).** Data: 20 NULL-scc BillLineItems with a resolvable QBO `ItemRef → ItemSubCostCode` mapping back-filled (incl. the 8 on Invoice 1057 → 13.01/21.00/8.01/19.00, matching the audit's expected SCCs); 0 remaining; 0 on Expense/BillCredit (parity confirmed clean). Targeted scc-only UPDATE, reversible backup `/tmp/scc_backfill_applied.jsonl`. Code: `reconcile_project.py`'s direct mapping-create now back-fills scc from the QBO line's ItemRef (mirrors the connector resolution; targeted, scc-only) so the out-of-band path can't reintroduce the gap. (Manual tool — no deploy.)
  - [x] **(audit Finding 1 upstream) bill_specialist SCC-at-draft — WON'T FIX (2026-06-23, data-driven decision).** Analysis: SCC is genuinely a per-bill cost *classification*, not a vendor constant — of 312 vendors with history, 59% have NO dominant SCC (<70%), only 18% are ≥90%-confident. So an agent that "always resolves SCC at draft" would be WRONG for the majority, and a wrong SCC silently corrupts budget tracking (worse than NULL, which gets correctly assigned in human review). Live exposure is tiny (10 draft bills with a NULL-scc line), and the historical/QBO path is already fixed (connector translates ItemRef→scc + reconcile_project back-fills + the 20-line data back-fill). The only safe form would be a high-confidence-only vendor-dominant-SCC default (helps ~18%), judged not worth the agent-change risk vs. the existing QBO-backfill + review coverage. Revisit only if NULL-scc email-only (never-pushed-to-QBO) bills become a real pain point.

A `CREATE PROCEDURE` body runs to the end of the **batch**, not to its matching `END`, so the `FK_RoleModule_Role` block was being swallowed **into the Delete sproc's body** — it could never execute as a constraint, and re-applying the base would have silently mutated a live sproc. U-048 inserted the missing `GO` (this is also why `DeleteRoleModuleById` is byte-identical to prod *today* — the FK block sat outside its stored definition).
  **ADDRESSED by U-053 (apply pending):** all three constraints — plus the bundled `UQ_Module_Name` — now have an explicit, data-guarded, self-verifying apply vehicle at `entities/role_module/sql/migrations/001_rbac_join_integrity_constraints.sql`, so the schema write is auditable rather than an untracked side effect of a base re-apply. **Running it against prod is its own gated action and may not have happened yet.** Once it has, base == live for the constraints too and re-applying `dbo.rolemodule.sql` becomes a genuine no-op — until then it is still NOT safe to blind-apply.
  **U-053 read-back query** (run after the apply; all four must verify, and the row counts must be unchanged):
  ```sql
  SELECT fk.[name], OBJECT_NAME(fk.parent_object_id) AS ParentTable,
         COL_NAME(fk.parent_object_id, fkc.parent_column_id) AS ParentCol,
         OBJECT_NAME(fk.referenced_object_id) AS RefTable,
         COL_NAME(fk.referenced_object_id, fkc.referenced_column_id) AS RefCol,
         fk.is_disabled, fk.is_not_trusted, fk.delete_referential_action, fk.update_referential_action
  FROM sys.foreign_keys fk
  JOIN sys.foreign_key_columns fkc ON fkc.constraint_object_id = fk.object_id
  WHERE fk.[name] IN ('FK_RoleModule_Role','FK_RoleModule_Module');
  -- expect 2 rows; is_disabled=0, is_not_trusted=0, both referential actions = 0

  SELECT i.[name], OBJECT_NAME(i.object_id) AS TableName, i.is_unique, i.is_unique_constraint,
         i.is_disabled, i.has_filter, i.ignore_dup_key,
         STRING_AGG(COL_NAME(ic.object_id, ic.column_id), ',') WITHIN GROUP (ORDER BY ic.key_ordinal) AS KeyCols
  FROM sys.indexes i
  JOIN sys.index_columns ic ON ic.object_id = i.object_id AND ic.index_id = i.index_id AND ic.key_ordinal > 0
  WHERE i.[name] IN ('UQ_RoleModule_RoleId_ModuleId','UQ_Module_Name')
  GROUP BY i.[name], i.object_id, i.is_unique, i.is_unique_constraint, i.is_disabled, i.has_filter, i.ignore_dup_key;
  -- expect 2 rows: UQ_RoleModule_RoleId_ModuleId='RoleId,ModuleId', UQ_Module_Name='Name'; is_unique=1, is_disabled/has_filter/ignore_dup_key=0

  SELECT (SELECT COUNT(*) FROM dbo.[RoleModule]) AS RoleModuleRows,
         (SELECT COUNT(*) FROM dbo.[Module])     AS ModuleRows;
  -- expect RoleModule 140 (as of 2026-07-26) and the Module count unchanged across the apply
  ```
  **Audit the class — THREE live instances found (U-053 corrected the count).** U-048's Pass-2 scanned all 372 repo `.sql` files for the shape. Three exist: `dbo.rolemodule.sql` (fixed in U-048), **`integrations/sync/sql/dbo.sync.sql:214`** — `DeleteSyncById` has no trailing `GO`, so the hardcoded `UPDATE dbo.[Sync] SET [LastSyncDatetime]='2026-01-01…' WHERE [Id]=14 AND [RowVersion]=0x…` that follows is **swallowed into the sproc body**, and the standalone UPDATE never ran — and **`entities/module/sql/dbo.module.sql`** — `DeleteModuleById` had no trailing `GO`, so `IX_Module_PublicId` was swallowed into the sproc body (fixed in U-053 repo; prod apply is a separate unit). The sweep that produced "exactly two" therefore has a false-negative and is worth re-running. Blast radius on the sync instance self-limits via the `RowVersion` predicate (it matches nothing once the row changes), but the sproc body is wrong. Needs a prod `sys.sql_modules` read + apply ⇒ own unit, own Gate 2. Also still worth cross-checking each entity's *declared* FK/UQ set against `sys.foreign_keys` / `sys.objects` in prod — `dbo.RoleModule` had three constraints that never ran, and nothing would have told us.
  **Why prose won't hold this:** only the *trailing* case is silent — a mid-file missing `GO` fails loudly ("CREATE PROCEDURE must be the first statement in a batch"). `tests/test_sproc_single_source.py` already parses every `CREATE OR ALTER PROCEDURE` in all 372 files, so a mechanical assertion (last sproc batch must not contain `ALTER TABLE` / `ADD CONSTRAINT`) is one step away and would cover conversion #36 without anyone remembering the rule. CLAUDE.md currently carries it as prose only.
  **U-054 handoff from U-051 (2026-07-16) — a working detector already exists, unlanded.** During U-051 the Composer run spontaneously wrote exactly the mechanical guard described above (batch-splitter + swallowed-statement detector + `test_no_statements_swallowed_into_procedure_body`) and it **correctly flagged `dbo.sync.sql`'s trailing UPDATE** on the real tree. It was removed from U-051 as out-of-scope and preserved verbatim at `<session scratchpad>/u054_batch_trap_test_HANDOFF.py`; re-derive it from this note if the scratchpad is gone. **Do NOT land it before the fix, or the suite goes red on a known-open defect.** Two warnings carried over: (1) **the correct fix is to insert `GO` BEFORE the trailing UPDATE** so it becomes its own batch — Composer's instinct was to *delete* the UPDATE outright, which is data-loss dressed as a fix, and it silently edited `integrations/sync/sql/dbo.sync.sql` to do so before being reverted; (2) the detector anchors on the **last** `END`-only line in the batch, so a proc with nested `BEGIN/END` could mis-anchor — validate against the full tree before trusting it as a gate. When U-051's detector was made green by hand it grew a **15-file `KNOWN_OFFENDERS` allowlist** (`dbo.project.sql`, `dbo.user.sql`, `dbo.userproject.sql`, …, mostly trailing `CREATE INDEX`/FK blocks) — those are **unverified leads, not confirmed findings**; U-054 should re-derive them itself rather than trust that list.

The `(?:PROCEDURE|FUNCTION)` merge was deliberately **not** taken — the two guards have different topologies (N entities → one base file each; one family → one shared file) and merging needs a discriminator, i.e. more complexity, not less. U-048 dropped it to hold net-zero. That apply is its own unit + its own Gate 2 — and `module` is not single-source-converted, so its base may be stale vs live; verify `sys.sql_modules` before applying.
- [ ] **(U-053 follow-up, 2026-07-27) Give the RBAC admin paths an honest integrity-violation response.** U-053's constraints make `DELETE /role/{id}` and `DELETE /module/{id}` fail with SQL 547 when `RoleModule` grants still reference the row, and a duplicate `(RoleId, ModuleId)` grant POST fail with SQL 2627. **Traced end-to-end during U-053 — neither is an uncaught 500** (the Gate-1 estimate that said so was wrong): both run repo → `map_database_error` → `InstantWorkflowHandler.execute`'s blanket `except` → `success=False, error=str(e)` → `raise_workflow_error`. Net today: **547 → HTTP 409, but labelled `"Concurrency violation: The DELETE statement conflicted with the REFERENCE constraint …"`** — right status, wrong story, and only by accident: `map_database_error` keys on the substring `conflict` and the 547 text happens to contain "conflicted". **2627 → HTTP 400** with the raw SQL text (constraint name *and* the duplicate key values) echoed straight into `detail`. Worth fixing: (a) pre-clean `RoleModule` children in `RoleService.delete_by_public_id` / `ModuleService.delete_by_public_id` so an intentional delete succeeds instead of erroring; (b) classify referential-integrity and unique violations explicitly in `map_database_error` rather than letting them fall through a keyword heuristic that catches one by luck and misses the other; (c) stop echoing raw SQL text to the client. **NB do not just map the duplicate case to 409** — `shared/api/responses.py::raise_database_error` documents that 409 is reserved for the iOS optimistic-concurrency flow and deliberately returns 422 for unique-key violations; any new mapping has to stay consistent with that contract. Accepted as a residual at U-053's Gate 1 (option B1) — fail-loud is strictly better than the silent orphaning/duplication it replaces.

U-051's catch-all (`tests/test_sproc_single_source.py`) makes the **SQL** side default-ON — a new `UserCanAccess*` UDF must be added to `ACCESS_UDFS` and homed in `shared/sql/dbo.access_udfs.sql` or the suite fails — but **nothing forces the corresponding `_ALLOWED_UDFS` entry**, so the Python gate would silently not know about it. Deliberately not fixed in U-051: importing a private constant would couple a no-DB pure-logic guard to a `pyodbc`-importing runtime module, which costs more than the duplication. Better fix is probably a guard test asserting `_ALLOWED_UDFS ∪ {UserCanAccessTimeEntry} == ACCESS_UDFS`. Low: adding an access UDF is rare and the SQL-side guard makes it a conscious act.

This unit needs a prod-read budget. Fix once the live body is known: absorb it into the base file, stub the other three. Same class as the 2026-07-15 outage, one entity over.
- [x] **(U-050 → split into U-051, medium — RBAC/security-flavored) The `UserCanAccess*` UDF family has no base-file home and the `project` base file carries a LIVE legacy actor bypass. BOTH SUB-ITEMS RESOLVED.** Sub-item 1 → **U-051 (2026-07-16)**; sub-item 2 → **U-050 (`4375d82`) + prod apply U-052**. Details inline below.
  1. **RESOLVED — U-051 (2026-07-16), repo-only, net-zero.** The fix was **not** the "fold each into its entity base file" treatment this item predicted — that is structurally impossible. Each UDF is `WITH SCHEMABINDING` across more than one entity package, so an entity-local home creates a from-scratch build **cycle** (`UserCanAccessProject` binds `dbo.UserProject`, but `dbo.userproject.sql` FKs back via `FK_UserProject_Project`; `UserCanAccessBill` binds `dbo.BillLineItem`, but `dbo.bill_line_item.sql` FKs back via `FK_BillLineItem_Bill`; same for BillCredit/Expense). U-045's `UserCanAccessTimeEntry` placement only worked because `dbo.userproject.sql` has no FK back to TimeEntry. **All five UDFs now live in `shared/sql/dbo.access_udfs.sql`** (a cross-entity family gets a cross-entity home, mirroring `shared/access.py`, its Python consumer); `UserCanAccessTimeEntry` was **relocated out of** the time_entry base file, byte-identical. Bodies for the four sourced verbatim from `gap3` (verified `== LIVE` prod by an /em prod read, 2026-07-16) → net-zero. gap1/gap2/gap3 are now pure SUPERSEDED stubs — each was **100% UDF definitions**, so nothing live was dropped (the user_project backfill + scoped list sprocs live in *sibling* gap1 files, untouched). `CREATE OR ALTER FUNCTION` now appears in exactly ONE file repo-wide. Build order: `shared/sql/README.md` — note it needs `gap2_created_by_user_id.sql` first, because SCHEMABINDING binds **columns** and `CreatedByUserId` is not in the four base `CREATE TABLE` blocks (Codex Pass-1 P1 catch). Exception recorded in CLAUDE.md's single-source rule.
  2. **RESOLVED — U-050 (`4375d82`, repo-only) + U-052 (prod apply, 2026-07-16).** `entities/project/sql/dbo.project.sql` no longer contains the bypass in any executable form (only historical prose in `002_remove_legacy_actor_bypass.sql`'s SUPERSEDED banner), and U-050's `tests/test_project_sproc_bypass_guard.py` fails if it returns. U-050 also surfaced a 🔴 LIVE `Project.Notes` data-loss bug (the 4 read sprocs omitted `[Notes]`) → fixed and applied to prod as U-052. *(Original diagnosis retained below for context.)* **`entities/project/sql/dbo.project.sql` carries `OR @ActorUserId IS NULL` LIVE in 4 sprocs** (`ReadProjects`, `ReadProjectById`, `ReadProjectByPublicId`, `ReadProjectByName`), while `entities/project/sql/migrations/002_remove_legacy_actor_bypass.sql` exists specifically to remove it (its own header notes the bypass became *"a silent 'no actor → show everything' path"* on the TimeEntry side). So re-applying the project base file **reintroduces an RBAC bypass** — a NULL actor sees every project. This is the U-037 pattern with a *fail-open security* payload instead of a *fail-loud crash* payload, which makes it strictly worse: it would not 500, it would silently over-share. **Latent, not live** (no bulk runner; `run_sql.py` takes one file at a time), same as the rest of this class. Fix = reconcile the project base file to the deployed post-gap3 state (a U-037-shaped unit; needs a prod `sys.sql_modules` read to confirm the live body before copying).

## ExpenseAgent (BillAgent parity) — follow-ups (2026-06-18)

Surfaced during the Unit 1 adversarial correctness hunt. Deferred (out of the immediate unit scope) but tracked:

- [ ] **`BillService.create` has the same partial-attach rollback bug just fixed in `ExpenseService.create`.** On attach-link failure the rollback does a bare `self.repo.delete_by_id(bill.id)` while the committed placeholder `BillLineItem` still references it — `FK_BillLineItem_Bill` has no `ON DELETE CASCADE`, so the parent delete raises 547, is swallowed, and both rows orphan (then wedge retries on the (vendor, bill_number) unique constraint). Mirror the Expense fix: delete the placeholder line item (cascade-aware `delete_by_public_id`) before deleting the bill. Latent today because bill creates rarely hit the partial-failure window.
- [ ] **Canonical `entities/expense/sql/dbo.expense.sql` is drifted** (warning header added 2026-06-18). `CREATE TABLE dbo.Expense` omits `IsCredit`/`CreatedByUserId`; `ReadExpenses`/`ReadExpensesPaginated`/`CountExpenses` lack the live `@ActorUserId`/`@ActorIsSystemAdmin` Gap-1 RBAC params. Re-running the file from scratch would build a broken table and regress per-user scoping. Reconcile the canonical file to match the live sprocs (pull live defs, fold the migration history in). Same drift class likely on `bill`/`bill_credit`/`invoice` canonical SQL.
- [ ] **Inline line-item create is not Project-access-gated.** `ExpenseLineItemService.create` (and `BillLineItemService.create`) resolve `project_public_id → project_id` without `assert_can_access_project`, so a non-admin agent can attach a receipt line to a Project they have no `UserProject` membership on. Pre-existing cross-cutting pattern — fold into a dedicated line-item access-gating pass (decide QBO-connector + manual-UI exemptions first, since both legitimately stamp projects).

Check whether the SWA deploy is live before shipping a new tab.
- [ ] **Lifecycle unit tests** — plan called for them; repo has no test infra so none shipped (deviation recorded). The rules are pure-Python branches in the three services + SQL guards (smoke-tested live 2026-06-11). If/when a tests/ convention lands, this is the first candidate.
- [ ] **QBO invoice re-pull clobbers ILI source links** (pre-existing, hurts Phase 2 drawn resolution): `connector/invoice_line_item/business/service.py` update path passes `source_type='Manual'` unconditionally — InvoiceAgent-linked lines degrade back to Manual on re-pull, migrating drawn dollars to Uncategorized over time. Guard the update path to preserve existing source FKs.
- [ ] **Voided QBO invoices** — Invoice has only IsDraft; a voided invoice pulled from QBO stays IsDraft=0 with its lines. Check how voids appear in qbo staging before trusting drawn totals (Phase 2 acceptance item).
- [ ] **Budget 'archived' flow** — status value + filtered unique index support re-baselining, but no archive endpoint/UI exists yet. Unscoped until a real re-baseline need arrives.

## TimeEntry list N+1 — fix before raising iOS timeouts (2026-06-12)

- [ ] **(P1) `GET /api/v1/time-entries` took 13.5s for 35 rows** (observed in prod 2026-06-11, App Insights). Root cause: per-row `read_current` status lookup in `_entry_dict_with_current_status` (the documented N+1) + count + project-ids batch. iOS now runs a 15s inter-byte request timeout — 1.5s headroom; the pull will intermittently fail on cellular until this is fixed. Batch the status lookup (one sproc returning latest status per entry id set, mirroring `ReadDistinctProjectIdsByTimeEntryIds`). Target <5s. Do NOT solve this by raising the iOS timeout (it bounds the worst-case UI freeze window).
