## Audit 2026-05-20 — API findings

Full multi-repo audit memory: `~/.claude/projects/-Users-chris-Applications-build-one/memory/project_audit_2026_05_20.md`. The items below are the API-side findings extracted from it. Order is rough severity; pair with that memory for context, cross-repo patterns, and rationale on what NOT to change yet.

### Critical
- [x] ~~**VendorCredit float() corrupts currency on every QBO round-trip.**~~ [integrations/intuit/qbo/vendorcredit/persistence/repo.py:92,154,177,184](integrations/intuit/qbo/vendorcredit/persistence/repo.py) — `float(vendor_credit.total_amt)` and similar for Amount/UnitPrice/Qty. Bill/Purchase repos correctly use `Decimal(str(...))`; VendorCredit regressed. Silent off-by-cents on every credit memo sync. Fix: replace with `Decimal(str(...))` (or pass Decimal directly; SQL Server DECIMAL accepts it). Add unit test round-tripping a 3-decimal value.
- [x] ~~**BillCredit complete uses float() for amounts + total + Excel filename string.**~~ [entities/bill_credit/business/complete_service.py:124,175,422](entities/bill_credit/business/complete_service.py). Writes precision-lost values to SharePoint filenames + Excel cells. Same fix shape as above.
- [x] ~~**ProcessEngine references non-existent `context.metadata`**~~ at [core/workflow/api/process_engine.py:433](core/workflow/api/process_engine.py). `if context.metadata.get("is_reply")` — TriggerContext has only `payload`. Latent: no live producer per memory, but any future caller passing `is_reply` would AttributeError. Fix: `context.metadata.get("is_reply")` → `context.payload.get("is_reply")`.
- [x] ~~**Latent SQL injection in `shared/access.py::_check()`**~~ — `f"SELECT dbo.{udf_name}(?, 0, ?)"` interpolates UDF name into SQL. All call sites hardcoded today; one refactor away from exploitable. Fix: whitelist dispatch dict `{entity_type → udf_name}`.

### High
- [x] ~~**`current_user_id` ContextVar not set in `InstantWorkflowHandler.execute()`.**~~ [core/workflow/business/instant.py:334-339](core/workflow/business/instant.py). Tenant_id is threaded; user_id is not. Scheduler-triggered instant workflows write NULL for `CreatedByUserId` on every downstream sproc that depends on the ContextVar fallback. Fix: `token = current_user_id.set(context.user_id); try: method(**kwargs); finally: current_user_id.reset(token)`.
- [x] ~~**Workflow row + WorkflowEvent write are NOT atomic.**~~ Fixed 2026-05-27. Repos now accept an optional `conn` parameter; `create_workflow()` and `transition_to()` open one connection and pass it to both the Workflow write and the WorkflowEvent write. Also removed a redundant DB persist from the `state` setter (transitions library sets state in-memory; `transition_to` persists atomically with the event). Bonus: eliminated one extra DB round-trip per transition.
- [x] ~~**Workflow event `_json_serial` missing UUID + bytes handlers.**~~ [core/workflow_event/persistence/repo.py:24-30](core/workflow_event/persistence/repo.py) and [core/workflow/persistence/repo.py:24-30](core/workflow/persistence/repo.py). Any payload carrying `public_id` (UUID) or `row_version` (bytes) silently fails the audit write with `TypeError`. Fix: add `uuid.UUID → str(obj)` and `bytes → base64.b64encode(obj).decode('ascii')` handlers.
- [ ] **OAuth refresh race — QBO + MS both vulnerable.** [integrations/intuit/qbo/auth/business/service.py:134-248](integrations/intuit/qbo/auth/business/service.py) and [integrations/ms/auth/business/service.py:232-268](integrations/ms/auth/business/service.py). `sp_getapplock` acquired AFTER reading the auth row → two callers can each POST the pre-lock refresh_token to Intuit/Graph; whichever writes the DB row last wins, and the fresher token is silently overwritten. Fix: acquire the lock BEFORE the read so the pattern is "lock → read → check freshness → refresh if still stale → write".
- [ ] **`_run_complete_bill()` (and Expense/Invoice/BillCredit peers) is fire-and-forget BackgroundTask with no watchdog.** [entities/bill/api/router.py:365-378,381-403](entities/bill/api/router.py). Crash post-finalize / pre-outbox = bill stuck `IsDraft=False`, no QBO/SharePoint/Excel, no recovery sweep. Per `project_one_file_per_tick.md`, this should be outbox-driven. Short-term: add a scheduler watchdog that detects `IsDraft=False AND no completion_result AND age > 5min` and surfaces them.
- [x] ~~**Double-click on Complete bypasses idempotency.**~~ [entities/bill/api/router.py:381-403](entities/bill/api/router.py) — guard check + BackgroundTask enqueue are not atomic; rapid second click enqueues a second task that re-enqueues all QBO/SharePoint/Excel outbox rows. Fix: re-check `is_draft` inside `_run_complete_bill()` itself before enqueueing side effects (return early if already complete).
- [x] ~~**Completion-result endpoints leak across tenants.**~~ [entities/bill/api/router.py:186-194](entities/bill/api/router.py) — gates on `Modules.BILLS` only, no `assert_can_access_bill()`. Same shape almost certainly on `/get/expense/{id}/completion-result`, `/get/invoice/...`, `/get/bill_credit/...`. Fix: fetch via service `read_by_public_id` (which goes through `assert_can_access_*`) before returning the result.
- [x] ~~**JWT validation missing `aud` + `iss` checks.**~~ [entities/auth/business/service.py:92-115](entities/auth/business/service.py). `jwt.decode` validates signature + exp + `token_type` only. Add `audience="build.one.api"` + `issuer="build.one"` to settings and to `jwt.encode`/`jwt.decode`.
- [x] ~~**`POST /admin/auth/set-credentials/{user_public_id}` missing `_require_csrf`.**~~ [entities/auth/api/router.py:188-214](entities/auth/api/router.py). Compare with `/auth/refresh` which does call `_require_csrf` when cookie auth is present. Cookie-authed admin visiting attacker site = CSRF'd password reset on any user.
- [x] ~~**No web logout endpoint**~~ — only `/mobile/auth/logout` exists. Web clients clear localStorage but can't revoke the refresh token server-side. Add `POST /api/v1/auth/logout` that revokes the refresh cookie + clears cookies, requires CSRF.
- [x] ~~**`shared/access.py::_check()` doesn't explicitly close the cursor** — under list-page scans (cursor-per-row), pyodbc cursor handles leak. Wrap in try/finally + `cursor.close()`.~~
- [x] ~~**MS mail draft 2-step PATCH has no rollback on PATCH failure.**~~ Fixed 2026-05-27. On PATCH failure, the orphan forward draft is now DELETEd before re-raising. Failure to delete is logged but doesn't mask the original error.
- [x] ~~**Excel 423 (Locked) treated as non-retryable in MS base client.**~~ [integrations/ms/base/client.py:853-869](integrations/ms/base/client.py). Concurrent Excel writes dead-letter immediately instead of waiting. Add `MsLockedError(MsGraphError, is_retryable=True)` and map 423 to it.
- [x] ~~**`decrypt_if_encrypted` masquerades ciphertext as plaintext on EncryptionError.**~~ Fixed 2026-05-27. New `_looks_like_fernet()` structural check (base64 + version byte `0x80` + min length). If decryption fails on something that looks like Fernet, raises `EncryptionError` with a logged `field_name` instead of silently passing ciphertext through. All 6 callers (MS + QBO auth repos + QBO client repo) now pass `field_name` for diagnostics. Plaintext passthrough preserved for non-Fernet values.

### Medium
- [ ] **SP NULL-overwrite anti-pattern.** Only `IsDraft` uses `CASE WHEN` guard. Every other UPDATE sproc unconditionally `SET`s — any optional column passed as `None` is silently NULLed in the DB. Sweep `entities/*/sql/` for `Update*.sql` files and add `CASE WHEN @Field IS NOT NULL THEN @Field ELSE [Field] END` per optional column.
- [x] ~~**`enrich_line_items()` silently drops unknown `source_type`.**~~ Fixed 2026-05-27. Added `_KNOWN_SOURCE_TYPES` set and `logger.warning()` when an unknown source_type is encountered.
- [x] ~~**`ExpenseLineItemAttachment` create silently returns existing on duplicate.**~~ Fixed 2026-05-27. Added logging + warning when duplicate create has mismatched `attachment_id` vs existing record.
- [ ] **AttachmentService blob delete failure doesn't block DB record delete** → orphan blobs in Azure (storage cost grows). Add an `OrphanedBlob` audit table + a cleanup job.
- [ ] **`useCompletionPolling` in React swallows all errors.** Move to API side: ensure completion-result endpoint returns clear 401/403/500 distinct from 404 ("not ready"). Web fix already in `build.one.web/TODO.md`.
- [ ] **QBO `BillLineItemBillLine` mapping skipped silently when `qbo_line.line_num` is NULL or unmapped.** [integrations/intuit/qbo/bill/connector/bill/business/service.py:406-417](integrations/intuit/qbo/bill/connector/bill/business/service.py). Orphan-row risk on QBO tax/total/shipping lines. Log warning + persist orphan reference to a reconciliation table.
- [ ] **Outbox `MAX_ATTEMPTS` enforced in Python only.** Add DB CHECK constraint on `[qbo].[Outbox]` + `[ms].[Outbox]` to fail closed if a future drainer regresses.
- [ ] **No rate limiting on `/auth/login` or `/admin/auth/set-credentials`.** Brute-force / credential-spam unthrottled. Add `slowapi` or similar.
- [x] ~~**Refresh token `REVOKED_GRACE_SECONDS = 60` is too generous.**~~ Fixed 2026-05-27. Lowered to 15s.
- [ ] **Permission cache 5-min TTL with best-effort invalidation = TOCTOU window between role revoke and next-tick flush.** Consider versioning the cache key (`permission_schema_version`) so invalidation is immediate.
- [x] ~~**Switch-company cache invalidation runs AFTER new tokens returned.**~~ Fixed 2026-05-27. Swapped order: invalidate first, then mint and return new tokens.
- [x] ~~**Scheduler `process_bill_folder` timer has no error handling around `_post()`.**~~ Fixed 2026-05-27 (commit `eadc741` in build.one.scheduler). Wrapped `_post()` in try/except inside the drain loop; transient failures break the loop cleanly instead of crashing the timer.
- [x] ~~**DI `analyze_document_bytes()` has no max file-size check.**~~ Fixed 2026-05-27. Added 50 MB cap (`_MAX_DOCUMENT_BYTES`) with clear `DocumentIntelligenceError` on violation.
- [ ] **DI confidence threshold not enforced on hoisted KV pairs.** Agent prompt should be explicit; OR `_hoist_and_validate` should filter < 0.70 confidence.
- [x] ~~**Anthropic timeout is global request timeout, not per-chunk.**~~ Fixed 2026-05-27. Split into per-phase `httpx.Timeout(connect=10, read=self._timeout, write=30, pool=10)`.
- [ ] **AgentSession can be left in `Status='running'` forever** if the background task is cancelled mid-stream. [intelligence/loop/session_runner.py:71-82](intelligence/loop/session_runner.py). Add a scheduler tick that resets running sessions older than 30 min.
- [x] ~~**Email body content flows into LLM tool results unescaped.**~~ Fixed 2026-05-27. Added `[EXTERNAL EMAIL CONTENT START/END]` injection markers around `body_content` in `_read_email_message` tool results.
- [ ] **Hardcoded model IDs across every agent definition** (`claude-sonnet-4-6`, `claude-haiku-4-5-20251001`). Anthropic deprecation = simultaneous fleet outage. Move to config (env var `{agent}_MODEL_OVERRIDE`).

### Low
- [ ] Workflow event has no retention/TTL policy → unbounded growth. Define retention + add a cleanup job.
- [ ] PII redaction in workflow event payloads — workflow rows persist user input verbatim in JSON.
- [x] ~~`print()` debug statements in [core/workflow/api/process_engine.py:389,401](core/workflow/api/process_engine.py) — replace with `logger.debug`.~~
- [x] ~~Signup endpoint leaks username existence ("Username already exists" vs "Invalid registration code"). Use generic error.~~
- [x] ~~TimeEntry approve/reject route gates on `can_update`, should gate on `can_approve`.~~ Fixed 2026-05-27. Changed to `require_module_api(Modules.TIME_TRACKING, "can_approve")`. Verified existing RoleModule grants already have `can_approve` correctly set.
- [x] ~~`orchestrator.create_workflow()` returns a tuple but type hint says `Workflow`.~~ Fixed 2026-05-27. Return type hint corrected to `tuple[Workflow, bool]`.
- [x] ~~Missing entries in `SYNCHRONOUS_TASKS` registry fail at request time, not startup.~~ Fixed 2026-05-27. Added import-time `RuntimeError` check validating SYNCHRONOUS_TASKS entries exist in PROCESS_REGISTRY.
- [x] ~~`DRAIN_SECRET` timing-leak (missing vs wrong-secret takes different code paths). Always perform HMAC compare.~~

## iOS v0.1.1 (target: distribute to Field SMEs by Friday May 1, 2026)

- [ ] **(P0, blocks Austin) Replace closure-of-services with `Resettable` protocol.** Three closures in [AppEnvironment.swift](../build.one.ios/BuildOne/App/AppEnvironment.swift) (`companyService.onCompanySwitch` lines 174-198, `authService.onLogout` lines 210-233, `authService.onUserLogout` lines 234-252) each list ~16 weak service references and call `resetForLogout()` / `prepareForLogout()` on each one. Adding a new service requires updating all three closures — and if you miss one, stale data silently leaks across logout / company switch (this exact bug class would have caught the v0.1.0 CoreData state-bleed bug if it had existed). **Fix**: define `protocol Resettable: AnyObject { func resetForLogout(); func prepareForLogout() async }`, adopt on every service (they already have these methods), expose `private var resettableServices: [Resettable]` on `AppEnvironment`, replace the three closures with `services.forEach { $0.resetForLogout() }` (or async-for-loop with prepareForLogout). New service → adopt protocol (compiler enforces), add to array once. ~1 hr work. Eliminates a whole class of bug.
- [ ] **Crash reporter (Sentry / Firebase Crashlytics / roll-your-own `POST /api/v1/admin/crash-log`).** Today if a tester's app crashes at any point during AppEnvironment.init (CoreData corruption, Keychain failure, schema migration error) we have zero visibility. Every "the app didn't work" report from a field SME is a black box. Even a 100-line roll-your-own (NSSetUncaughtExceptionHandler + a POST endpoint) catches Swift fatalErrors and gives us a way to find out what's happening. Misses signals (SIGSEGV etc.) but good enough for v0.1.x beta — upgrade to Sentry/Crashlytics in v0.2+. Cost: low. Value: huge.
- [ ] **(P0) Carousel must render before `permissionsReady` gate.** Currently `RootView` in [BuildOneApp.swift](../build.one.ios/BuildOne/App/BuildOneApp.swift) blocks the onboarding carousel on `permissionsReady` becoming true. Any 403 or network failure during `loadCorePermissions()` leaves first-time users stuck on a black `PermissionsLoadingView` with a red toast — Austin Rogers hit this on 2026-04-30 launch when his Owner role lacked some startup module grants. Carousel itself needs no permissions to render (it's 4 slides about Location/Notifications/Face ID/Loading) and the LoadingSlide already calls `bootstrapForLoggedInUser()` which fires both `loadCorePermissions()` and `loadAccountData()` in parallel — by the time the user finishes the carousel, perms are loaded. **Fix**: reorder the conditional so `!hasCompletedOnboarding → OnboardingView` is checked BEFORE `!permissionsReady → PermissionsLoadingView`. Returning users (post-onboarding) still get the brief loading screen — that path is unchanged. Consider time-boxing the gate (after ~5s, render TabView anyway and let `hasEntityAccess`/`NoModulesView` handle the failure state) — defer if v0.1.1 scope is tight.
- [ ] **TimeEntry CoreData scoping fix.** `TimeEntryService.loadFromCache()` calls `CDTimeEntry.fetchAll()` with no `userId` filter, so on user-switch the prior user's logs flash on the Time tab before the API response replaces in-memory state. CDTimeEntry already has a `userId` Int64 attribute. Fix: add `NSPredicate(format: "userId == %@", currentUserId)` to all `fetchAll()` reads in TimeEntryService; tag every `upsert(...)` with `cd.userId = tokenStore.userId` if not already set. CDTimeLog filters via parent TimeEntry (no separate userId needed). CDTimeEntryStatus already has userId — same filter pattern. **Do NOT delete CoreData rows on logout** — would destroy field workers' offline-queued edits on session expiry. Leave `resetForLogout()` clearing in-memory `@Observable` state only. After fix, bump `MARKETING_VERSION` 0.1.0 → 0.1.1 + `CURRENT_PROJECT_VERSION` 1 → 2 in `BuildOne.xcodeproj/project.pbxproj`, archive + validate + upload + submit Beta App Review.
- [ ] **Module/Role/Project cache scoping (deferred from v0.1.1, plan for v0.1.2 or later).** Same state-bleed pattern in `ModuleService` / `RoleService` / `ProjectService` cache pre-populate, but the user-scoped API endpoints wired in iOS commit `655a69c` correctly replace in-memory state on response. Visible flash of stale data is brief and not a security issue. Full fix requires CoreData model migrations (CDModule / CDRole / CDProject have no `userId` attribute today) — defer until either (a) field reports complain about the flash, or (b) we're already touching the model for another reason.
- [ ] **Pre-release multi-user smoke test in iOS release checklist.** Sign in as User A → populate state (clock in, edit) → sign out → sign in as User B → verify clean state. Single-developer single-device testing missed the v0.1.0 state-bleed bug. Codified in memory `feedback_ios_multi_user_testing.md`.
- [ ] **Carousel must show before permission API loads (UX bug from 2026-04-30 launch).** Current `RootView` in [BuildOneApp.swift](../build.one.ios/BuildOne/App/BuildOneApp.swift) gates the carousel on `permissionsReady` — if any startup API call 403s during `loadCorePermissions()`, the user sees a red toast over a "Loading…" spinner instead of the onboarding carousel. Austin Rogers hit this on first login (Owner role lacked USERS + VENDORS module access; iOS calls `GET /user/{public_id}` and `GET /contacts/user/{user_id}` at startup, both module-gated). **Fix**: reorder the conditional to `if !hasCompletedOnboarding → OnboardingView` first, then `if !permissionsReady → PermissionsLoadingView`, then `if hasEntityAccess → TabView`. Carousel fires immediately on first login while permissions load in parallel. By the time the user finishes Location/Notifications/Face ID slides, permissions are usually loaded; any 403 then surfaces on the Time tab where the user has actionable next steps, not a black "Loading" screen.
- [ ] **Self-read endpoints should be auth-only, not module-gated.** `GET /api/v1/get/user/{public_id}` is gated on Modules.USERS and `GET /api/v1/get/contacts/user/{user_id}` on Modules.VENDORS — wrong gates for self-data. Field workers shouldn't need broad USERS/VENDORS read just to load their own profile + contacts at app startup. **Workaround applied 2026-04-30**: granted Owner + Project Manager roles read-only access to USERS and VENDORS RoleModule rows. Cleaner long-term: relax these endpoints to auth-only when the requested `user_id` matches the caller's `current_user["user_id"]`, fall back to module check only when reading another user's data. Same pattern for the Contact-by-user endpoint. After the fix lands, drop the workaround grants from Owner / PM (or leave as harmless additive read).

## Cross-repo: Idempotent CREATE pattern via client UUID (v0.2, spans API + iOS)

Filed 2026-05-20 after the iOS-sync duplicate-TimeLog incident on 2026-05-19 was patched in iOS build 4 (commit pending). Build 4 closed the immediate field-worker pain via per-entity `hasAttemptedCreate` flags + natural-key pre-check on TimeEntry/TimeLog, plus the DB `UX_TimeLog_TimeEntry_ClockIn` unique index. That works but is fragile and per-entity bespoke. The proper fix is a universal client-UUID idempotency pattern.

**Problem.** Today an offline-or-poor-coverage iOS client that POSTs a CREATE can lose the response (network drop, app backgrounded mid-await, OS killing a background task). The local CD row still carries `hasPendingCreate=true`, so the next drain retries — and if the original request actually succeeded server-side, the retry creates a duplicate row. Real incident: 3 field workers got duplicate TimeLog rows on 2026-05-19. Cleanup required manual SQL across 5 users.

**Build 4 mitigation (shipped, narrow).** `CDTimeLog.hasAttemptedCreate` + `CDTimeEntry.hasAttemptedCreate` boolean flags persist across app launches. On retry, iOS pre-queries the server's existing rows for the entry/date and claims a matching one (by `(timeEntryId, clockIn, logType)` for TimeLog, `(userId, workDate)` for TimeEntry) instead of re-POSTing. Plus duplicate-key error recovery in the catch block. Plus the DB unique index on `(TimeEntryId, ClockIn)` as defense-in-depth.

**Limits of the build-4 approach:**
- **Byte-exact natural-key matching is fragile.** Any clockIn timestamp serialization drift (timezone `Z` vs `+00:00`, milliseconds present/absent) → pre-check misses → re-POST → unique-index rejection → claim path runs, but it's a brittle round-trip.
- **Only TimeLog + TimeEntry are protected.** Bill, BillLineItem, Project, Role, Module, Company, Organization, Contact, BillLineItemAttachment all have the same structural race — lower volume but same bug shape. iOS will eventually need to grow more sync surface area for field workers.
- **Per-entity matching logic.** Every new entity needs its own natural-key shape in iOS + pre-check fetch wired in.

**Proposed deeper fix — client-UUID idempotency:**

1. **API: Add `ClientId UNIQUEIDENTIFIER NOT NULL` column to every entity with user-driven CREATE.** Lightweight migration: `ALTER TABLE … ADD ClientId UNIQUEIDENTIFIER NOT NULL DEFAULT NEWID()` then a one-time backfill, then drop the default once iOS is sending it. Tables: TimeEntry, TimeLog, TimeEntryStatus (the manual-submit case), Bill, BillLineItem, BillCredit, BillCreditLineItem, Expense, ExpenseLineItem, Invoice, InvoiceLineItem, ContractLabor, ContractLaborLineItem, Project, Customer, Vendor, Contact, Role, RoleModule, Module, UserModule, UserProject, UserRole, Organization, Company, OrganizationCompany, UserOrganization, UserCompany, Attachment, BillLineItemAttachment.

2. **API: Update every CREATE sproc to be MERGE-shaped on ClientId.**
   ```sql
   IF EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM dbo.X WHERE ClientId = @ClientId)
       SELECT existing row by ClientId
   ELSE
       INSERT … VALUES (@ClientId, …);
       SELECT inserted row;
   ```
   Plus unique index per table on ClientId. Retried CREATEs with the same ClientId are deterministic no-ops.

3. **API: Accept `client_id: UUID` on every CREATE endpoint body.** Pydantic schema; pass through to service → repo → sproc.

4. **iOS: Send `cd.publicId` (already a generated UUID) as `client_id` in every CREATE payload.** The local publicId becomes the canonical idempotency key. The server's response still returns its own `public_id` for downstream FK relationships — iOS just upserts as today.

5. **iOS: Drop the `hasAttemptedCreate` flags + per-entity natural-key matching once #4 is in.** Simpler, no bespoke code per entity.

**Cost estimate.** ~1 day API (column + sproc + endpoint payload threading on ~25 tables, mostly mechanical), ~half day iOS (CREATE payload threading + drop the bespoke pre-check logic), ~half day testing + migration. Worth doing in one focused project, not piecemeal — partial coverage doesn't help because the bug is per-entity.

**Recommended timing.** Before second customer onboards, or before substantially expanding iOS field-worker mutations (e.g., letting them create Expenses from the field, edit Projects, etc.). Not blocking, not urgent post-build-4. Note: keep the build-4 narrow protections in place until this lands — they're cheap and provide belt + suspenders.

**Pair entry in iOS TODO.md** (same section header) — keep them in sync if scope or timing changes.

## iOS service architecture review (v0.2.0+)

- [ ] **CoreData reads should happen on background contexts, not the main actor.** Paired with the lazy-cache refactor below. Today every service's cache-populate happens on `@MainActor` because `AppEnvironment` is `@MainActor @Observable` and every service init runs there. Cumulative 30 services × 30ms fetch = ~900ms of main-thread blocking before LoginView renders. Refactor: use `NSManagedObjectContext.perform { ... }` on a background context for the fetch, hop back to main actor with the results for UI binding. Do this at the same time as the lazy-cache refactor below — they touch the same code.
- [ ] **Move cache pre-populate out of service `init` to first-access.** Today every service in iOS `AppEnvironment.init` does a synchronous `CDX.fetchAll(in: coreData.viewContext)` to pre-populate its in-memory state. With 16 services on `@MainActor` this is fine (~100-400ms cold launch), but the cost is linear in service count + cache size. At v0.2 with 30+ services and bigger caches, this crosses the user-perceptible 1s threshold and starts blocking the LoginView from appearing. **Fix**: convert each service's init-time `fetchAll` into a `private func ensureCachePopulated()` called lazily at the top of any `loadX()` / computed-property read. Init becomes ~zero cost; cache pre-populate happens when the relevant view actually appears. Field workers who never touch Bills/Expenses/Invoices stop paying for those caches every cold launch. ~30 min per service when already touching the file. Zero behavior change for end users — they only pay for caches they navigate to.
- [ ] **Don't introduce a DI framework yet.** For 30 services, plain init injection in `AppEnvironment` is still cleaner than Swinject/Factory overhead. Revisit at ~100 services or when cold-launch construction exceeds 2s after the lazy-cache fix above.
- [ ] **Defer feature-container split until forced.** Splitting `AppEnvironment` into `CoreEnvironment` (always) + per-tab `FeatureContainer` (instantiated on tab open) is the right shape if memory ever becomes a constraint or service count reaches ~50. Until then, the eager-init pattern is right — predictable, view bodies don't nil-check, closure capture works at init time. Don't refactor pre-emptively.
- [ ] **Service init failure recovery (CoreData corruption).** Today if `CoreDataStack.init` fails to load the persistent store — corrupted file, schema-migration failure, disk error, post-iCloud-restore mismatch — the iOS app crashes inside `AppEnvironment.init` before LoginView renders. User has no in-app recovery path; only fix is delete + reinstall, which **destroys every unsynced offline edit**. For a field-worker app that's offline-first, that's the worst possible outcome. **Fix**: wrap `loadPersistentStores` in a recovery path — on failure, log the error (via a crash reporter, see next item), delete the corrupted store file, recreate fresh. User loses local cache for that session but the app launches. Pair with telemetry so we know when it's happening in the wild. Consider an "in-memory CoreData fallback" so writes stay usable for the current session even if persistence is broken. **Add a crash reporter** (Sentry / Firebase Crashlytics / a simple `POST /api/v1/admin/crash-log` endpoint) — today we have no visibility into launch crashes on testers' phones.
- [ ] **Company-switch reset must sync offline edits first.** `AppEnvironment.companyService.onCompanySwitch` (lines 174-198) calls `resetForLogout()` on every service — clears in-memory state but doesn't sync pending edits before context flips. User-initiated logout (`authService.onUserLogout`) correctly uses `prepareForLogout()` which syncs first; company switch should be at least as careful. **Scenario at Phase 5+** when CompanyId scoping is API-enforced: user in Company A clocks in offline → switches to Company B (JWT cid=B) → network returns → `observeConnectivity` fires `syncPendingUpdates` → pending clock-in POSTs with cid=B → API either rejects (edit stuck forever in CoreData) or accepts and writes the row into the wrong tenant (silent corruption). Neither outcome is good. **Fix two layers**: (1) call `prepareForLogout()` not `resetForLogout()` on company switch — same chain `onUserLogout` uses, syncs first if connected; (2) tag every pending CoreData row with a `pendingCompanyId: Int64` set at the moment of the edit, so the sync path can defer pending rows until the right Company is active again. Lands alongside Phase 5 sproc-level CompanyId enforcement on the API. Today: not a bug — only one Company exists. The moment a second Company arrives, becomes a real risk.

## RBAC architectural review (v0.2.0+)

- [ ] **Decide whether to keep role-based module grants.** User direction during the v0.1.0 launch session: "no blanket access for anyone — manually select Projects, Modules, CRUD/Review/Approval per user." Current model: `User → UserRole → Role → RoleModule → Module` with role granting all its modules to every user holding the role. Alternative: roles become labels, actual grants live on `UserModule` per-user-per-module with explicit CRUD/Review/Approval flags. **Touches**: API repos/services for RBAC, web UI module assignment forms, iOS auth flow, DB rows, the 4 user-scoped sprocs added 2026-04-29 (`ReadProjectsByUserId`, `ReadRolesByUserId`, `ReadModulesByUserId`, `ReadUserRolesByUserId`). Multi-day project. Sketch a migration path before scoping. UserModule additive-grant pattern (read-only layer on top of RoleModule, shipped 2026-04-29) is the partial step in this direction.

## Access Control Rebuild — Phase 5b + follow-ups (post-2026-05-06)

All 5 phases of the rebuild shipped 2026-04-30 → 2026-05-06. Carry-over items:

- [x] ~~**`ReadProjectsByUserId` doesn't honor `IsSystemAdmin` bypass**~~ (filed 2026-05-27). Caught while testing the new iOS time-tracking implementation — Christopher (`IsSystemAdmin=1`) saw only the projects he had explicit `UserProject` rows for, despite the bypass primitive being live everywhere else (Phase 3 TimeEntry, Gap 1 Bill/Expense/Invoice/ContractLabor service-layer access checks all bypass for system admins). The sproc at `entities/project/sql/dbo.project.sql` (or wherever the user-scoped read lives) is a hard `INNER JOIN dbo.UserProject` with no admin escape. **Workaround applied 2026-05-27**: backfilled `UserProject` rows so every `IsSystemAdmin=1` user (Christopher + Claude Agent) is on all 148 Projects — 159 new rows inserted. This needs re-running every time a new Project is created. **Permanent fix**: patch `ReadProjectsByUserId` to accept `@ActorIsSystemAdmin BIT` and skip the join when set (same shape as Phase 3 TimeEntry's `@ActorIsSystemAdmin` param). Repo-side: thread `current_is_system_admin` ContextVar through `entities/project/persistence/repo.py`. Drop the workaround backfill rows for `Claude Agent` after the sproc fix lands; Christopher's are harmless. Alternative: trigger on `dbo.Project` INSERT that auto-creates `UserProject` rows for every `IsSystemAdmin=1` user — simpler in SQL but adds a sproc footgun for anyone bulk-inserting projects.
- [ ] **Phase 5b — Multi-tenant enforcement** (deferred until 2nd Company arrives or preemptive enforcement is wanted). The 24 tenant-data tables now have `CompanyId NOT NULL` with `DEFAULT (1)` so existing sprocs/services keep working unchanged. To deliver actual isolation: (a) update Create sprocs to accept explicit `@CompanyId BIGINT` param; (b) thread CompanyId through services from `current_company_id` ContextVar; (c) drop the `DEFAULT (1)` constraints once explicit values are required; (d) update read sprocs with `@ActorIsSystemAdmin BIT` + filter clause (same pattern as Phase 3's TimeEntry). Plus reference-entity scoping decisions (Vendor/Customer/SubCostCode etc. — per-Company vs per-Organization for shared catalogs).
- [ ] **Phase 4c — React + iOS Company picker UI**. API endpoints already shipped (`/auth/me` `companies[]`, `POST /auth/switch-company`, `POST /mobile/auth/switch-company`). Land in `build.one.web` (header dropdown reading `me.companies[]`) and `build.one.ios` (Swift element using mobile variant). Today system admins can switch via direct API calls only.
- [ ] **Drop legacy fallback in `_enrich_payload_with_authz`.** Phase 2 set `JWT_CID_GRACE_DAYS=0` (default) — tokens missing `uid`/`cid` claims no longer fall back to a DB lookup. After ~1 week of stable prod operation, remove the `legacy_fallback_allowed` branch entirely. Keeps the auth path simpler.
- [x] ~~**Drop the inert legacy `Admin` Role row.**~~ Resolved 2026-05-07 by renaming instead of dropping. The legacy `Admin` row (Role.Id=1) carried 23 fully-populated RoleModule grants (full CRUD on every module) — exactly the shape Phase 2's seeded `Tenant Admin` (Role.Id=15) was meant to have but never got. Cleaner than drop + re-seed: deleted the empty seeded Tenant Admin row and renamed Role.Id=1 from `Admin` → `Tenant Admin`. Preserves the 23 grants and the 2 UserRole assignments (Christopher + Apple Reviewer; harmlessly redundant since they bypass via `IsSystemAdmin=1`). Migration: `scripts/migrations/rename_legacy_admin_role_to_tenant_admin.sql`.
- [ ] **Phase 1b — Drop `OrganizationCompany` + `UserOrganization` tables/entities.** Was deferred from Phase 1 because React `UserProfile.tsx` still reads them (Organizations section). Schedule once the frontend section is removed or moved to derive from `UserCompany → Company.OrganizationId` (the 1:N model is in place since Phase 0).
- [ ] **Assign RoleModule grants for the 6 non-admin human roles seeded in Phase 2.** Controller, AP Specialist, AR Specialist, Reviewer, Time Clerk, Auditor were seeded as Role rows with no module grants. Configure per-role grants via React `/role/:id` Modules tab when those roles get assigned to real users. (Tenant Admin has 23 full-CRUD grants from the legacy Admin rename, 2026-05-07. Project Manager already has grants from the review-notification work.)

Three gaps from the multi-user audit shipped 2026-05-06 (commits 64a2a05, 361d248, e2d3afb). Carry-over items:

Gates `BillService.read_by_*`, `BillCreditService.read_by_*`, `ExpenseService.read_by_*`, `InvoiceService.read_by_*`, `ContractLaborService.read_by_*` plus the four line-item child services. Includes Bill/BillCredit/Expense alt-key lookups (read_by_*_number_and_vendor_*) and ContractLabor list-returning methods (read_by_vendor / billing_period / status / import_batch — filtered post-fetch in Python). Mutations inherit gating via `read_by_public_id`. `EntityNotAccessibleError` → HTTP 404 (not 403). Fix: `QboOutboxWorker._process` and `MsOutboxWorker._process` now wrap their body in `set_authz_context(is_system_admin=True)` (save → set → restore), and all 10 sync scripts call `assert_cli_system_admin()` (new helper in `scripts/sync_helper.py`) under `if __name__ == "__main__":`. 7 dead-lettered Bill outbox rows replayed successfully post-deploy. Migrations: `scripts/migrations/gap2_{core,adjacent,reference}_threading.sql`. Skipped: `ReviewEntry` (decommissioned, no Create sproc).

Re-running a stale base file rolls back its migrations — bit me during Phase Gap2-T. Cleanup: for each entity with migrations, consolidate base + migrations into a fresh base file representing the current sproc shape, then archive the migrations under `entities/<x>/sql/migrations/_applied/`. ## iOS Profile redesign — battle-test follow-ups (2026-05-11)

Items audited and deferred from the pre-ship battle test. Six HIGHs + four MEDIUMs were fixed in the same session; the entries below are the ones we deliberately punted because they're rare, recoverable, or design-call.

- [ ] **M2 — Concurrent token-write race between `changePassword` + `AuthenticatedSession.refresh`.** Both write to `tokenStore.accessToken/refreshToken` without coordination. Realistic conditions: user is mid-modal typing while a background refresh fires AND the responses arrive in inverted order. Worst case = one extra forced re-login. Proper fix: a shared write lock between `AuthService.changePassword` and `AuthenticatedSession.refresh`, OR funnel both through a single token-write coordinator actor. File: `build.one.ios/BuildOne/Services/Auth/AuthService.swift::changePassword`.
- [ ] **M3 — Fast-nav race might surface stale `initialValue` in `TextFieldEditScreen`.** `.onDisappear` fires-and-forgets `Task { await onCommit }`. If the user pops + re-pushes the same field row before the optimistic local write lands on the MainActor queue, the new screen reads stale state. Self-correcting on next refresh. Proper fix: maintain a per-field "pending write" map in the VM so subsequent reads see in-flight values. File: `build.one.ios/BuildOne/Features/Profile/TextFieldEditScreen.swift`.
- [ ] **M5 — `TextFieldEditScreen` has no Cancel.** Chevron-back always commits the typed value (after validation in the caller). Per the Profile spec ("iOS Settings-style: tap row → push → edit inline → back auto-saves"), but iOS convention often pairs that with an explicit Cancel that pops without committing. Design call. If we add Cancel: ToolbarItem(.cancellationAction) that sets `value = initialValue` and then dismisses.
- [ ] **M7 — Theme + biometric-enabled `UserDefaults` keys persist across logout.** `build.one.theme.override` and `build.one.biometric.enabled` are device-scoped. User A picks Dark + enables Face ID → User B logs in fresh and inherits both. Theme is arguably correct as device-preference; biometric-enabled flag is more debatable (the underlying credential is `biometryCurrentSet`-bound so the security boundary is fine — it's the *visual indicator* that mis-states reality for User B's first peek at Profile → Security). Pre-existing pattern, not introduced by the Profile redesign. Fix would be either scoping the keys by userId or clearing them in `resetForLogout()`.
- [ ] **L2 — Verify `.preferredColorScheme` propagation to the iOS status bar** in the simulator across all three Appearance settings. Likely fine — SwiftUI's `.preferredColorScheme` is documented to drive `UIStatusBarStyle` — but worth eyeballing under each Theme override.

## iOS Profile Sessions list (deferred from 2026-05-11 Profile redesign)

The Profile redesign's Security screen draws a "Sessions" section (`This iPhone · Active now · San Francisco` + `Web · Chrome · 2 hours ago · Oakland`) per `design/HANDOFF-2026-05-11/.../screenshots/05-security.png`. **Shipped today as a single read-only "This iPhone" row** because there's no backend support for listing or revoking individual sessions. Picking this up properly requires:

- [ ] **Schema: capture device metadata on refresh tokens.** Add `DeviceLabel NVARCHAR(64) NULL`, `UserAgent NVARCHAR(256) NULL`, `LastSeenAt DATETIME2 NULL`, `LastSeenIP VARCHAR(45) NULL` to the refresh-token table. Migration via `scripts/migrations/sessions_metadata.sql` (additive, no DEFAULT needed since columns are nullable).
- [ ] **Issuance + refresh paths populate metadata.** `mobile/auth/login` + `auth/login` populate these on token issuance (label derived from `User-Agent` header — "iPhone" / "Web · Chrome" / etc.; IP from request). Refresh path bumps `LastSeenAt` + `LastSeenIP` so the "Last seen" timestamp stays accurate without per-request writes elsewhere.
- [ ] **New endpoint: `GET /api/v1/mobile/auth/sessions`** — returns `[{public_id, label, last_seen_at, last_seen_ip, is_current}]` for the caller's Auth row. `is_current` derived by matching the caller's refresh-token JTI.
- [ ] **New endpoint: `POST /api/v1/mobile/auth/sessions/{public_id}/revoke`** — revokes one session by ID. Existing `RevokeAuthRefreshTokenByPublicId` sproc or equivalent; reuse the per-token revoke path.
- [ ] **iOS: `SessionService` + `SessionEndpoints`** — list + revoke. Replace the single-row "This iPhone" placeholder in `Features/Profile/SecurityScreen.swift` with a real list + per-row revoke action (destructive confirm alert).
- [ ] **Optional v2: GeoIP labels.** Free MaxMind GeoLite2 DB gives "San Francisco" / "Oakland" style labels from the `LastSeenIP`. Adds a deploy artifact (~50MB) + monthly DB refresh. Ship the feature without it first — "Active now" / "2 hours ago" without location is still useful. Revisit if the visual gap matters in field feedback.

The existing `POST /api/v1/mobile/auth/logout-all-devices` (added 2026-05-11) covers the bulk-revoke case from the Sign out of all devices row — sessions UI is incremental on top of that.

## Dev/prod backend split (revisit before second customer)

- [ ] **Stand up a dev backend.** Currently web + iOS + API all share the prod Azure App Service + SQL DB. Apple-reviewer test data, schema changes, and breaking API changes all hit prod cold. Stand up `buildone-dev` (App Service + SQL) + build-time URL switching in iOS (Debug → dev URL, Release → prod URL); web is already env-driven so cheap. Cost ~$50-100/mo. **When**: just before a second customer signs on (currently single-tenant, mostly Chris's data). Test-org-in-prod pattern + additive-only schema discipline covers the gap until then.



## Bill/attachment follow-ups (post-2026-05-06 reshape)

- [ ] **Multi-invoice transmittal PDFs: per-page vendor parsing.** When DI returns a multi-page document where each page has its own `send to:` / `INVOICE NO:` block, **treat each as a separate invoice and resolve the vendor per page** (not per document header) — a combined PDF can carry different legal vendors (e.g., a freight subsidiary on one page, main vendor on the others). Bridge each page to its own Attachment, resolve the vendor per page independently, create separate Bills. Any future intake path that hands multi-invoice payloads to a bill-parsing agent needs an explicit rule for this, or it silently mis-categorizes the sub-invoice(s) to the wrong vendor.

- [x] **`Vendor.IntakeNotes` editing UI.** Done 2026-05-07. Renamed `Vendor.IntakeNotes` → `Vendor.Notes` (sp_rename, idempotent guard) for end-user clarity, threaded through model → repo → service → schemas → router, added textarea on Vendor Create + Edit pages (gated `Modules.VENDORS, "can_update"`) + display on View page. Bundled with the `Project.Notes` work below as a single Wave 1.
- [x] **Sweep other admin handlers for the same wrapped-200 anti-pattern. DONE 2026-07-12.** Audited every scheduler-facing admin handler in `shared/api/admin.py` against the criterion "sync_fn return carries a *numeric* `status_code` (or per-part status) that CAN be ≥400 and is currently returned as HTTP 200." **Exactly one handler needed the fix: `sync/qbo/{entity}`** — all 10 `sync_qbo_*` scripts return `{"result": {…}, "status_code": 200|500}` where the 500 is a *caught-internally* exception (so `_timed`'s 500-on-exception never fires) → passed through as HTTP 200 → scheduler saw failed QBO syncs as success. Fixed with the standing template: after `_timed`, if `envelope["result"]["status_code"] >= 400`, raise `HTTPException(502)` with the inner `error`/`upstream_status` detail. Only the total-failure 500 raises; partial per-record failures still return `status_code:200` (watermark held + retried) and correctly pass through as 200. `_timed` left untouched. Test: `tests/test_admin_wrapped_200.py` (non-2xx→502, 2xx→200 envelope). Codex gpt-5.5/xhigh review PASS. **Audit result — handlers that did NOT need the fix, and why:**
  - `outbox/drain`, `/drain/qbo`, `/drain/ms` — return `{qbo/ms: "ok" | {"error":…}}` markers (deliberate per-integration failure isolation), no numeric `status_code`. LEFT (a separate design choice from wrapped-200; out of this unit's scope).
  - `box/drain` — `BoxOutboxWorker.drain_all` returns totals / `{skipped}` / `{paused}` / `{error}`; no numeric status_code. LEFT.
  - `reconcile/qbo`, `reconcile/ms` — services **raise** on error → `_timed` 500-on-exception already covers it. LEFT.
  - `reconcile/box` — `BoxReconcileService.run()` returns a canary report / `{error}`; no numeric status_code. LEFT.
  - `bill-folder/tick` — `process_single_item` returns `{"status":"skipped"/"completed"}` (string marker, no numeric status_code); MS-Graph non-200 **raises** → caught → `mark_failure` records the error on the item row by design (parent run keeps draining). LEFT.
  - `bill-folder/enumerate` — **already raises `HTTPException(502)`** on `BillFolderEnumerationError` (@931); its normal return has no numeric status_code. LEFT (already fixed — not double-wrapped).
- [x] **Wave 3 Phase D — Bill list ReviewStatus column shipped 2026-05-07.** `/get/bills` response now includes `review_status`, `review_status_is_final`, `review_status_is_declined` per bill (batched via new `ReadCurrentReviewsByBillIds` sproc + `ReviewRepository.read_current_by_bill_ids` — single SQL call per page, no N+1). React Bills list shows Draft + Review columns side-by-side with color-coded badges (declined / approved / in-review / dash for no review). AP can now spot `Draft=true AND ReviewStatus=approved` (= "PM signed off, ready for AP to Complete") at a glance.
- [x] **Pre-existing `dbo.bill.sql` migration breakage.** ~~Running `python scripts/run_sql.py entities/bill/sql/dbo.bill.sql` fails with `Invalid column name 'ExpiresAt'` on the `BillCompletionResult` query.~~ **RESOLVED 2026-07-12** (same root cause as the "Bill SQL drift" item below — see there). Root cause: `dbo.bill.sql` carried a stale *duplicate* `BillCompletionResult` block (old `ExpiresAt`/1h-TTL design); the canonical, prod-matching definition already lived in the sibling `entities/bill/sql/dbo.bill_completion_result.sql` (CompletedAt + idempotent ExpiresAt→CompletedAt migration). Deleted the duplicate from `dbo.bill.sql`; no prod migration needed (prod already correct). Codex xhigh review PASS.
- [ ] **Bill memo template expansion if minimal proves insufficient.** Current template (2026-05-06): `"DOC#:{raw_invoice_number} | Ref:{po_or_job_or_reference}"` — minimal, only fields not in typed columns. If users find themselves wanting to grep memos for vendor name / total / project / etc. (because cross-system search via QBO export, Excel, etc. doesn't have access to the joined columns), revisit and switch to the denormalized format. Track real usage; don't speculatively expand.
- [x] ~~**Server-side guard: `Bill.DueDate >= Bill.BillDate`.**~~ **Shipped 2026-07-12 as the stronger EQUAL rule.** The `>=` phrasing here is superseded by the authoritative rule (umbrella memory `feedback_bill_duedate_equals_billdate.md`, confirmed 2026-05-27): **`Bill.DueDate` must ALWAYS EQUAL `Bill.BillDate`.** Enforced at the sproc choke point (not the service) so ALL callers — manual UI, email/bill agents, QBO sync, scripts — are covered by one change. `dbo.CreateBill` + `dbo.UpdateBillById` now persist `DueDate = @BillDate` and **ignore** the passed `@DueDate` (param kept for back-compat; force/mirror, not reject). Migration `entities/bill/sql/migrations/005_bill_duedate_mirror_billdate.sql`, ported into **both** base `CreateBill` definitions (`dbo.bill.sql` + `dbo.bill_create_source_email.sql`) and `UpdateBillById`. Codex xhigh review PASS. (Note: existing `DueDate != BillDate` rows — 4,940 at ship time — are NOT backfilled; see the backfill follow-up below.)
- [x] ~~**Agent due-date default when DI doesn't extract one.**~~ **Largely mooted 2026-07-12** by the sproc-level DueDate mirror above. Since `dbo.CreateBill` / `dbo.UpdateBillById` now force `DueDate = @BillDate` regardless of what the agent supplies, whatever the agent computes (null, copy-from-DI, or a `bill_date + payment_term.days` fallback) is ignored for the persisted `DueDate` — the produced value is always valid by construction. `PaymentTermId` is still stored separately and remains the source of truth for when payment is actually owed; no agent change is needed for DueDate correctness. Close.
- [ ] **Backfill existing bills where `DueDate != BillDate` (data cleanup, separate unit).** Filed 2026-07-12 alongside the migration-005 sproc guard, which only fixes NEW create/updates. A read-only prod count at ship time found **4,940** `dbo.Bill` rows with `CONVERT(date,DueDate) <> CONVERT(date,BillDate)`. Backfill would `UPDATE dbo.Bill SET DueDate = BillDate WHERE DueDate <> BillDate` — but treat as its own reviewed unit: scope/verify the row set first, run in batches, and confirm no downstream consumer keys off the historical DueDate before mutating. Explicitly OUT OF SCOPE of the migration-005 unit.
- [ ] **Reconcile pre-existing `CreateBill` drift across the two base files (low-risk, latent).** Surfaced 2026-07-12. `CreateBill` is defined in BOTH `entities/bill/sql/dbo.bill.sql` (older variant, **lacks** `@CreatedByUserId`) and `entities/bill/sql/dbo.bill_create_source_email.sql` (live variant, has `@CreatedByUserId` + `COALESCE(@CreatedByUserId,17)`). A full base re-run applies whichever runs last; if the older `dbo.bill.sql` variant wins, new bills' `CreatedByUserId` falls to the table `DEFAULT 17` rather than the passed user (harmless today — 17 = Christopher — but silent). Both now carry the DueDate=BillDate fix, so this is NOT a DueDate risk. Fix later by collapsing to a single canonical `CreateBill` definition (drop the stale one from `dbo.bill.sql`, keep the source-email variant).
- [x] ~~**`FindVendorForInvoice` + `FindProjectForInvoice` sprocs return phantom rows with NULL Name/PublicId.**~~ Closed 2026-05-26 — could not reproduce. Direct `EXEC FindVendorForInvoice @VendorName='Puente''s Drywall', @SenderDomain=NULL` returned a single fully-populated row (`VendorId=749`, `VendorName='Puente's Drywall, Inc'`, `VendorPublicId='A48AE4DD-...'`, `Strategy='prefix_name'`, `Confidence=0.85`). Same shape on `FindProjectForInvoice @AddressHint='4005 Franklin Pike'` — two fully-populated rows (`ProjectId=35` FP + `ProjectId=36` FP-POOL). Verified via the production service path (`VendorService.find_for_invoice`) as well. Deployed sproc `OBJECT_DEFINITION` exactly matches the canonical `entities/{vendor,project}/sql/` files; git log shows no edits since 2026-05-07 (12 days before the bug was reported). Likely explanation: transient state during the prior agent walk (concurrent scheduler interleaving, or the prior session misread a different unrelated row). Locked contract via new `scripts/verify_find_for_invoice_sprocs.py` — exercises both sprocs across known cases, asserts every row with non-null Strategy carries non-null Name + PublicId + Confidence. If the bug ever recurs, that script reproduces it.

## Bill list page review — `build.one.web/src/pages/bills/BillList.tsx` (2026-05-06)

Findings from a focused review of the Bills list page. Listed in priority order; first three are real bugs, rest are UX / hardening.

- [ ] **Vendor / Project names render blank past the dropdown's first page.** [BillList.tsx:134-135](../build.one.web/src/pages/bills/BillList.tsx#L134-L135) calls `useIdNameMap` against `/api/v1/get/vendors` and `/api/v1/get/projects` with no `?page_size=`; [useIdNameMap.ts:14](../build.one.web/src/hooks/useIdNameMap.ts#L14) just `getList`s and builds the map from one page. Any bill whose vendor/project ID isn't on that first page falls back to `""` — both the cell *and* the sort-by-vendor key go blank silently. Fix options: (a) pass `?page_size=10000` (cheap; tenant has ~1100 vendors); (b) add a slim id+name endpoint; (c) join `vendor_name` / `project_name` into the bill list response server-side and drop the maps entirely (best long-term).
- [ ] **Sort is page-local, not global.** [BillList.tsx:146-165](../build.one.web/src/pages/bills/BillList.tsx#L146-L165) sorts `resolvedItems`, which is the current 50-row page only. Clicking a `sortable-th` header looks like a global sort but page 2 sorts independently of page 1 — misleading. Fix: lift sort to the API (`?sort=column&dir=asc|desc` query params on `GET /api/v1/get/bills` → through service → into the paginated sproc's `ORDER BY`). Pairs naturally with #1 if the list response gains `vendor_name` / `project_name`.
- [ ] **Folder-processing poll never cancels on unmount.** [BillList.tsx:92-119](../build.one.web/src/pages/bills/BillList.tsx#L92-L119) uses recursive `setTimeout(poll, 2000)` with no AbortController, no `useRef` cancel handle, and no `useEffect` cleanup. Navigating away during folder processing keeps polling and calls `setState` on an unmounted component (React warns). Fix: store the timer id in a `useRef` + clear in a `useEffect` cleanup, or convert to an `AbortController` and check `signal.aborted` before each setState.
- [ ] **Search input has no debounce.** [BillList.tsx:386-388](../build.one.web/src/pages/bills/BillList.tsx#L386-L388) calls `setSearch` on every keystroke. `usePaginatedList` re-fetches on each change — typing "vendor" fires 6 requests against the prod API. Add a 250–300ms debounce inside `usePaginatedList` (or at the input). Same fix would benefit other list pages that consume the hook.
- [ ] **Disallowed file types are dropped silently in the drop zone.** [BillList.tsx:170](../build.one.web/src/pages/bills/BillList.tsx#L170) filters by `ACCEPTED_TYPES` and skips anything else with no feedback. Drop a `.docx` and nothing happens. Add a per-file error row (`error: "Unsupported type"`) so the user knows why their file disappeared.
- [ ] **Brittle sort comparators on `_date` and `is_draft`.** [BillList.tsx:158-162](../build.one.web/src/pages/bills/BillList.tsx#L158-L162) does `Number(aStr)` first, falls through to `localeCompare`. Booleans (`"true"`/`"false"`) and ISO dates work *coincidentally* — if `bill_date` ever becomes `MM/DD/YYYY` the date sort silently breaks. Move to typed comparators per column (a `getSortValue(col, row)` map returning `number | string`). Lower priority — wait until #2 lifts sort to the server, which makes this moot.
- [ ] **Inconsistent persistence: `sortKey` / `sortDir` use sessionStorage, `statusFilter` does not.** [BillList.tsx:49](../build.one.web/src/pages/bills/BillList.tsx#L49) vs [:50-55](../build.one.web/src/pages/bills/BillList.tsx#L50-L55). Pick one: persist all three, or persist none. Persisting all three is the user-friendlier choice (matches how `usePaginatedList` already persists `page` + `search` via `sessionPersistenceKey`).

## Jinja purge — migration waves (started 2026-04-23)

Phase 0 audit done. Phase 1 scaffolding (`/auth/me` + SSE `/auth/me/changes` + React `useCurrentUser` + Sidebar gating) shipped.

Phase 2 — delete Jinja per entity in waves, one commit each, verify in UI before next wave:

- [x] **Wave A — leaf entities:** address_type, payment_term, review_status, vendor_type, taxpayer, classification_override, dashboard. (2026-04-24, `f34b63b`; 21 routes retired, 622 → 601)
- [x] **Wave B — reference entities:** cost_code, sub_cost_code, customer, vendor, address, organization, project, company, module. (2026-04-24, `f6cecf2`; 36 routes retired, 601 → 565)
- [x] **Wave C — identity surface:** user, role, role_module, user_module, user_project, user_role, vendor_address, project_address. (2026-04-24, `e0ddc32`; 28 routes retired, 565 → 537. `project_address` controller was dead code — never registered in `app.py`.)
- [x] **Wave D — transactional:** bill, bill_credit, expense, invoice. (2026-04-24, `7758405`; 17 routes retired, 537 → 520. Extracted `_enrich_line_items` → `entities/invoice/business/enrichment.py` as `enrich_line_items` since the invoice packet PDF generator depends on it; dropped legacy `/expense/edit/{id}` redirect.)
- [x] **Post-Wave-E admin rip (2026-04-24):** removed the React admin surface E4 had just built and the dead workflow-approval plumbing. Deleted `entities/admin/` (api + schemas + empty business/persistence stubs), `core/workflow/business/admin.py` (`WorkflowAdmin`), `core/workflow/business/pending_actions.py`, `core/workflow/business/execute_pending_action.py`, `core/workflow/api/pending_action_router.py`, React `pages/admin/AdminDashboard.tsx` + `WorkflowDetail.tsx`, and their App.tsx routes. All action buttons (retry/approve/reject/cancel) gated on workflow states (`awaiting_approval`, `failed`, `needs_review`) that no production code transitions to anymore — the email-intake workflow that used them was decommissioned months ago and agents use their own approval system on `AgentSession`. Kept the workflow **data layer** (`orchestrator`, `instant`, `models`, `WorkflowRepository`, `WorkflowEventRepository`, `execute_synchronous`) — every entity CRUD still records a `Workflow` + `WorkflowEvent` row for audit. Routes: 484 → 472 (−12). If email-intake returns (planned "inbox rebuild"), the admin UI gets rebuilt for the new workflow shape anyway.
- [x] **Wave E — shared-infra deletion (2026-04-24):** deleted all remaining Jinja web controllers (auth, integration, 5 QBO under `integrations/intuit/qbo/*/web/`, sync, plus 4 empty-stub controllers on taxpayer_attachment / invoice_line_item / invoice_attachment / invoice_line_item_attachment), the entire `templates/` tree, the `static/` directory, and all Jinja helper code — `get_current_user_web`, `require_module_web`, `WebAuthenticationRequired`, `RefreshRequired`, the two FastAPI exception handlers in `app.py`, and the `StaticFiles` mount. Dropped `Jinja2==3.1.6` from `requirements.txt` + `requirements-prod.txt`. The QBO OAuth callback now returns a self-contained HTML landing page instead of redirecting — since React runs locally only, there's no public web host to bounce to, so the API renders a "QuickBooks connected, close this tab" page inline via `HTMLResponse`. CSRF helpers kept (React's silent refresh relies on them). `entities/auth/business/service.py` pruned (~100 lines gone, 7 orphan service imports removed). Routes: 508 → 484 (−24). Password reset deferred — ship as its own small wave when needed; it doesn't block anything now.
  - [x] **E4 — admin in React (2026-04-24):** new `AdminDashboard.tsx` (6 stat cards + searchable/filterable workflows table via `/api/v1/admin/stats` + `/admin/recent-workflows` + `/workflows/search`) and `WorkflowDetail.tsx` (meta header, related-entities links, full event timeline, and retry / approve / reject / cancel actions with inline modals). Dropped the Jinja "Inbox Quality" tab entirely — it referenced `/api/v1/admin/inbox-stats` + `/api/v1/classification-overrides*` endpoints that were never implemented (email-intake surface was decommissioned earlier). Also deleted the 4 orphan React `ClassificationOverride*` pages + their App.tsx routes + the `ClassificationOverride` type — the entity was purged from the API months ago. Deleted `entities/admin/web/` + `templates/admin/` (view.html, workflow_detail.html, overrides.html). Routes: 510 → 508 (−2). `static/css/admin.css` is now orphaned and will go with Wave E5.
  - [x] **E3 — contract_labor Import + Bills in React (2026-04-24):** new React pages `ContractLaborImport.tsx` (drag-and-drop Excel upload, results display) and `ContractLaborBills.tsx` (per-vendor card grid, per-vendor + bulk generate, collapsible day summary, auth-aware PDF preview). Added Import/Bills links to `ContractLaborList`. Extracted ~200 lines of vendor-aggregation business logic from the old Jinja `bills_page` into `entities/contract_labor/business/bill_summary.py::build_bills_summary`; exposed at `GET /api/v1/contract-labor/bills-summary?billing_period=...`. Deleted entire `entities/contract_labor/web/` + `templates/contract_labor/` (including the already-redundant `/list`, `/edit`, `/{id}` Jinja routes). Routes: 515 → 516 (+1 summary endpoint) → 510 (−6 web routes) = **510 net**.
  - [x] **E2 — React signup (2026-04-24):** added `SignupPage.tsx` + `signup()` on `AuthContext`, routed at `/signup`, cross-linked from `LoginPage`. Reuses existing `POST /api/v1/signup/auth`; on success redirects to `/user/create` to complete the User entity. Two stale `/auth/signup` links remain in `templates/shared/partials/header.html` + `templates/auth/login.html` — both go with Wave E5 shared-infra deletion, low impact since nobody lands on those Jinja pages.

Routes 520 → 516 (−4). (An OAuth `?success=&message=` toast was also added to `IntegrationList` but removed later the same day — React runs locally only, so the callback renders a self-contained HTML landing page on the API host rather than cross-redirecting.)
- [x] **Contact UI re-implementation (2026-04-24).** `<InlineContacts>` React component is wired into all 5 parent edit pages (Vendor, Company, Customer, Project, User) and all 5 parent view pages (readOnly). Old `shared/partials/contacts_*.html` partials are unreachable and will be removed with shared-infra deletion.

Follow-ups / post-purge:

- [ ] **Promote SSE profile events from B-lite to B-full** (cross-worker). Add `[auth].[ProfileChangeEvent]` table + 2s poll in the SSE handler. Needed once we scale past `-w 2` single instance, or once we have a user base that hits cross-worker edges often.
- [ ] **Add `IsNavigable BIT NOT NULL DEFAULT 1` to `dbo.[Module]`.** Today every Module row that exists for RBAC permission gating also shows up in the sidebar. `Attachments`, `Pending Actions`, and `Time Tracking` are legitimately-scoped permission modules but have no top-level UI page; today they render as broken sidebar links. Splitting the flag lets the backend keep RBAC scopes distinct from navigation. `_resolve_me_payload` should then omit non-navigable modules from the `modules[]` array (or the sidebar should filter on the flag).
- [x] **Admin-driven password reset.** (2026-04-29, API `81443a1`, web `311c3bc`) New `POST /api/v1/admin/auth/set-credentials/{user_public_id}` (Modules.USERS, can_update) lets an admin create-or-update Auth (username + password ≥ 8) for any user and link it via `Auth.UserId` in one call, plus `GET /api/v1/admin/auth/by-user/{user_public_id}` returns `{username, has_auth}`. Wired as a Credentials card on the new UserProfile page. Closes the onboarding gap (admin creates User → admin sets credentials → user logs in).
- [ ] **Self-service password reset / "forgot password".** Public flow on the login page — email a reset token, accept the new password from an unauthenticated form. No implementation anywhere (no token schema, no email send, no React page). Ship as its own wave when needed.
- [ ] **Self-service "change my password" while logged in.** No settings page yet. Only an admin can change a user's password today. Add when there's a real user-facing settings surface to drop it into.
- [x] ~~**Revoke refresh tokens on password change.**~~ ✅ **DONE** (Gap 3, commit `64a2a05`, 2026-05-06; re-verified 2026-07-11 — `set_credentials_for_user` now calls `revoke_all_refresh_tokens_for_auth` on every password change). Historical rationale: `AuthService.set_credentials_for_user` updated the password hash but didn't touch existing `[auth].[RefreshToken]` rows for that user. A user whose password is reset stays logged in (their access token works for ~15min, refresh token for ~30d) until tokens expire. Security best practice: on password change, revoke all refresh tokens for the target Auth. One sproc + one call inside `set_credentials_for_user`. Not blocking onboarding but worth shipping before launch.

## User Profile / authz follow-ups (2026-04-29)

## Review entity follow-ups

- [ ] **Decide whether to gate completion on review approval** (per parent: Bill, Expense, BillCredit, Invoice). V1 of the `Review` entity ships records-only — `complete_*` endpoints are NOT guarded by `ReviewService.is_approved(...)`. The hook is a 1-line change at each completion router (`if not review_service.is_approved(parent_type, parent_public_id): raise 409`). Decision is per-parent, not all-or-nothing — a customer may want to require review on Bills but not on Expenses. Round back once the entity is in production use and we've seen real review data.
- [ ] **`Review.is_approved` semantics with soft-deleted parents.** When the four parents (Bill/Expense/BillCredit/Invoice) move to soft-delete-only, decide whether `is_approved` should require parent `IsDeleted=False`. My take: yes — a voided/soft-deleted bill shouldn't read as "approved" for completion-gate purposes. Wire when the gate lands, not before.

## Bill SQL drift

- [x] **`dbo.BillCompletionResult` schema drift.** **RESOLVED 2026-07-12 — aligned base file to prod.** Investigation (read-only prod queries) confirmed prod is the correct evolved design: table `CompletedAt DATETIME2(3) NOT NULL DEFAULT SYSUTCDATETIME()`, permanent (no TTL); `UpsertBillCompletionResult(@BillPublicId, @ResultJson)` and `GetBillCompletionResult(@BillPublicId)` carry no `ExpiresAt`. The canonical, prod-faithful definition **already existed** in the sibling file `entities/bill/sql/dbo.bill_completion_result.sql` (incl. an idempotent ExpiresAt→CompletedAt migration); the drift was purely a **stale duplicate block** left behind in `dbo.bill.sql` (old `ExpiresAt`/1h-TTL design). Fix (Option B): **deleted the duplicate block from `dbo.bill.sql`**, leaving the sibling file as the single source of truth — fits the dir's one-object-per-file pattern and removes the duplicate-definition drift hazard for good. Repo code (`repo.py`) is column-name-agnostic (passes only `{BillPublicId, ResultJson}`, reads only `ResultJson`), so zero code change. **No prod migration** — prod is already correct; applying the sibling file is a CREATE-OR-ALTER no-op. Codex (gpt-5.5 xhigh, read-only) review PASS; `run_sql.py` GO-split of `dbo.bill.sql` now parses clean with zero `ExpiresAt` refs. **FOLLOW-UP (separate unit, out of scope here):** a full `run_sql.py entities/bill/sql/dbo.bill.sql` re-run audit — other batches in that file may carry their own independent drift; only the `BillCompletionResult` batch was reconciled here.

## Data hygiene

- [ ] **Audit which entities should use soft delete vs hard delete (cross-cutting).** The model is inconsistent today — `Vendor` soft-deletes (`IsDeleted` column + `SoftDeleteVendorByPublicId` sproc), but `CostCode` / `SubCostCode` / `Customer` / `Project` hard-delete. Some hard-delete entities probably want soft: anything FK-referenced from historical records (customers with old projects, sub-cost-codes referenced from old line items, projects on completed bills, etc.). For each entity decide: what FKs point at it, what's the audit/history value of keeping the row, what's the UX cost of orphaned references on hard-delete. Apply uniformly. The agent delete tools' approval gate stays the same either way — only the underlying behavior changes.

## Box Excel follow-ups (2026-07-06)

- [ ] **`InvoiceInvoiceConnector`: reconcile adds AND removals; survive partial inserts (HA-04).** (a) A mid-loop DB disconnect left 3/89 ILIs uninserted, and the connector's don't-re-sync-a-populated-invoice guard then refused all later adds — make the guard diff-aware (sync missing mapped lines) instead of all-or-nothing. (b) QBO line removals delete the mapping but strand the dbo ILI — the connector should delete the orphaned ILI (+ un-bill its source) and update the header. Playbook delta re-run section documents the manual recovery meanwhile.
- [ ] **Packet generator: dedupe identical attachments** — one PDF supporting multiple lines (NES statement, HA-04) prints once with a note listing the lines it supports, instead of once per line. Cosmetic.
- [ ] **SP↔Box DETAILS auto-reconcile (KI-39).** The two workbooks are one logical ledger synced only by outbox writes; human edits don't cross-propagate. Periodic col-Z-keyed comparison flagging divergent rows (values, H-tags, presence) into ReconciliationIssue.
- [ ] **Box packet re-version leaves stale `box.File` rows on repeated regens (HA-04).** Symptom: `box.http.request.failed` / non_retryable on the packet's 409 path + dangling registry rows, though the physical file stays singular. Registry upsert keys on BoxFileId (sound); suspicion: per-regen variance in the dbo `Attachment.filename` fed to the enqueue (each regen mints a new Attachment) producing distinct Box names/identities. Needs a live repro: capture the failing request log + the box.File rows for one invoice, then key the packet's Box identity on the invoice, not the per-regen attachment.


- [ ] **Box invoice path must refresh col N, not just col H (KI-46, OHR2-36 2026-07-13).** A DETAILS row first written while the bill's Price was NULL froze at N=$0 in the Box workbook: SharePoint's invoice sync rewrites N, but Box's `stamp_draw_request` stamps only column H and `apply_rows_to_details` skips existing col-Z keys — Box diverged $18,630 from SP/QBO with every outbox row `done`. Fix options (decide jointly with the KI-36 self-heal below, same skip-freeze root): (a) invoice stamp path also writes `{13: <amount>}` for each stamped row (cheap, invoice-scoped), or (b) `apply_rows_to_details` updates value columns in place on col-Z match instead of skipping (general, heals all frozen values). Interim: Step 10 now requires a Box VALUE read; surgical `stamp_columns_by_key` remediation recipe in playbook KI-46.
- [ ] **One-time sweep: SP↔Box draw-total divergence across existing draws (KI-46 latent class).** Any draw whose bill rows were written to Box while Price was NULL (pre KI-16 fix, 2026-07-03) may carry the same frozen-$0 divergence OHR2-36 hit. Sweep every `[box].[ProjectWorkbook]`-mapped project: per draw tag in col H, compare Box SUM(N) vs SharePoint SUM(N); report divergent (project, draw, col-Z) rows for surgical fill. Declined during the OHR2-36 session — still outstanding. (The durable periodic version is the KI-39 auto-reconcile item above.)
- [ ] **Self-healing `apply_rows_to_details` for blank-cost-code rows (KI-36).** When an incoming row's col-Z key already exists but the EXISTING row's col B/C is blank and the incoming row now carries a code, update B/C in place instead of skipping — the current skip freezes uncoded rows forever (WVA-18 root cause; SHT-22 $4,030 live AIA under-report). Companion: a periodic sweep (reconcile job or drain-time pass) that re-codes any blank-B DETAILS row whose col-Z public_id resolves to a now-coded dbo line, and flags stale-Z rows whose public_id no longer resolves (QBO re-pull leftovers) for manual clearing. Fill-in-place only — never insert_rows; never touch G702/G703 (fullCalcOnLoad recalcs them from DETAILS).
- [ ] **Gate Box Excel enqueue on SubCostCodeId.** `_enqueue_box_excel` (Bill/Expense/BillCredit) enqueues rows for uncoded lines that then land blank-B and freeze (KI-36). Either skip uncoded lines with a distinct log (they enqueue on a later re-sync once coded — requires the self-heal above so the re-sync isn't skipped), or hold the row until the line is coded. Decide jointly with the self-heal.

## Invoice pull-sync follow-ups (2026-04-26)

- [x] **✅ CLOSED — architecturally superseded; verified 2026-08-29.** Source-linking is now a **dedicated post-pull engine** (`entities/invoice/business/reconciliation.py:368` sets `bill_line_item_id`/`expense_line_item_id`), lineage U-177 → U-301c Tier 0c/0d provenance. The codebase deliberately keeps the QBO pull connector "dumb" (creates `Manual` ILIs) and does source-resolution in that separate engine — folding fingerprinting BACK into the pull connector would reverse that chosen separation. (The connector DID grow `_find_and_match_manual_by_fingerprint`, but that re-adopts orphan Manual ILIs to QBO lines, not to source Bill/Purchase lines.) ~~**Connector auto-linking of Manual ILIs.**~~ Original ask: Match keys: Description + Amount + ServiceDate=TxnDate + CustomerRefValue + RealmId. LineNum-align for ambiguous descriptions. Leave `Manual` only when both Bill and Purchase staging miss. **Doing this also closes the keyspace footgun from the OHR2-GUEST-09 incident** — the `qbo.Bill.Id` value would no longer surface to playbook callers, and Step 4 wouldn't need to query `qbo.*` in the first place.

Microsoft Graph supports `POST /workbook/worksheets/{name}/range(address='A{r}:Z{r}')/delete` with body `{"shift": "Up"}`. Wire it through `MsGraphClient`, accept a list of row indices, sort descending, and delete bottom-up so indices stay stable. Useful any time an outbox-driven enqueue races a human's "wait, that's wrong" reaction.

Options: (a) add a `ReadyAfter` delay knob to the enqueue helpers (`enqueue(ready_after_seconds=N)`) so an operator can opt into a 5-min holding pattern for human runs; (b) add a `Status='paused'` value the drain skips, plus an admin endpoint to cancel paused rows; (c) document operator workflow as "disable the scheduler timer in Azure for the duration of risky runs, then re-enable." Pick one and ship before the next manual playbook run.

Low urgency (a log-noise investigation, not a known data bug).** **Investigate `InvoiceLineItemConnector` "Mapping exists but InvoiceLineItem N not found" log line** — fires during re-run even when the referenced ILI is clearly present in `dbo.InvoiceLineItem`. The connector's lookup may be running through a service that applies the data-isolation UDF and filtering out rows that ContextVar should have bypassed. Worth a closer read of `InvoiceLineItemConnector.sync_from_qbo_invoice_line` to confirm it's using the system-admin path.
- [x] **✅ CLOSED — fixed by U-293b (verified 2026-08-30):** the connector now writes QBO-derived fields onto the existing MATCHED ExpenseLineItem (update-in-place — "apply QBO fields onto an existing, matched ELI", shared by the fast path + legacy mapping-found branch) instead of the create+delete dance, so no orphan ELI / `FK_InvoiceLineItem_ExpenseLineItem` error / stale blob. ~~Fix `PurchaseExpenseConnector` (and `BillBillConnector`) orphan-ELI/BLI side effect on mid-cycle upsert~~ — surfaced MR2-MAIN-07 / Home Depot Purchase 67915, 2026-05-19. When the user assigns an Item to a previously-uncategorized line in QBO and the playbook force-pulls + `upsert_from_external` that purchase, `qbo.PurchaseLine` gets a fresh PK (staging row is replaced, not updated). The connector treats the new line as new, CREATES a new `dbo.ExpenseLineItem` (with the new `SubCostCodeId` populated), and tries to DELETE the old ELI — which fails on `FK_InvoiceLineItem_ExpenseLineItem` because the active invoice still references the old ELI. Worse, the cascade-style delete inside `ExpenseLineItemService.delete_by_public_id` runs in order `blob → Attachment row → ELA → ELI` with each step in its own try/except; the blob delete *succeeds* before the FK error kills the rest, leaving the dbo.Attachment row pointing at a 404 BlobUrl. Net state: orphan new ELI (has SCC, on no invoice), old ELI patched onto the invoice but with no SCC and no ELA link, and an Attachment row whose Azure blob is gone. Today's workaround is documented in InvoiceAgent playbook CRITICAL #6 (patch in-place + re-link attachment + re-point mapping + delete orphan) and Known Issue #21; the blob loss is self-healed on the next `QboAttachableService.sync_attachables_for_*` call because the AttachableAttachmentConnector detects the missing blob and re-downloads from QBO. **Proper fix**: connector should UPDATE the existing `dbo.ExpenseLineItem` in place when the new `qbo.PurchaseLine` semantically corresponds to it (e.g., same parent QboPurchase + same LineNum, or matched by amount+description). Avoid the create+delete dance entirely. Same shape on the Bill side via `FK_InvoiceLineItem_BillLineItem`. Without this fix, every uncategorized-purchase-fix flow will leak a stale Azure blob and require a manual re-sync.

## TimeLog `CreatedByUserId` actor threading (audit-trail integrity, 2026-05-12)

- [ ] **Thread `current_user_id` through TimeLog Create sproc invocations.** Production `dbo.TimeLog` rows currently all show `CreatedByUserId = 17` (Christopher), regardless of which user actually created the log — meaning the Gap 2-T threading (`commit a80cff3`) landed on TimeEntry creates but missed TimeLog creates. The Create sproc accepts `@CreatedByUserId BIGINT = NULL` with `COALESCE(@CreatedByUserId, 17)` fallback; every TimeLog insert is silently using the fallback. Effect is audit-trail integrity only — TimeLog scoping uses parent `TimeEntry.UserId` (which is correct), so this isn't a data-leak issue. Find the TimeLog Create call site(s) in `entities/time_entry/business/time_log_service.py` (or wherever), thread `current_user_id.get()` through. Verify on next non-Christopher user create: row's `CreatedByUserId` matches the actor.

## iOS TimeEntry cache user-scoping (deferred to next MARKETING_VERSION, 2026-05-12)

- [ ] **Fix `TimeEntryService.loadFromCache()` to filter by `tokenStore.userId`.** The v0.1.1 planned fix shipped half-done: `CDTimeEntry` has the `userId` column + `upsert` tags rows correctly + `fetchByUser(_:)` filtered query exists, but `TimeEntryService.loadFromCache()` (`BuildOne/Services/TimeEntry/TimeEntryService.swift:91`) calls the unfiltered `CDTimeEntry.fetchAll(in:)`. Result: on a multi-user device where user A's cache is present and user B signs in, user B briefly sees user A's entries (~1-2 seconds) until the server response replaces in-memory state. **Server-side data is fully scoped** (RBAC Phase 3 sproc filter); this is a local-cache visual leak only. **Bundled with next MARKETING_VERSION bump** since fixing it under 0.1.0 doesn't justify a fresh Beta App Review cycle on its own. Fix is ~5 lines: swap `fetchAll(in:)` → `fetchByUser(tokenStore.userId, in:)` with a nil-guard. Same pattern we used for `CDProject` in the redesign cutover.
