## Time Tracking auto-submit (deferred — field-test first, 2026-05-09)

Context: iOS v0.2.0 ships the slate Time Tracking redesign with a deliberate two-step Clock-out → Submit flow. Risk: a super clocks out at end of day, drives home, never opens the app again — the day rots in draft, the PM never sees it. Discussed Phase 1g (in-app banner on next-day open + EOD local notification) as a recovery UX, then decided on the simpler path: server-side auto-submit at midnight cutoff, with no iOS-side change. **Holding off on implementation** until field testing tells us how often the gap actually causes problems. If field reports show supers routinely forget to submit, build this. If they don't, drop the plan.

When picking this up, the agreed plan:

- [ ] **`AutoSubmitStaleDrafts` stored procedure** in `entities/time_entry/sql/dbo.time_entry.sql` (idempotent `CREATE OR ALTER`). For each TimeEntry with `current_status='draft'` (resolved via most-recent `TimeEntryStatus` row, same pattern as existing read sprocs) AND `work_date < CAST(GETDATE() AS DATE)` AND has at least one TimeLog AND every TimeLog has `clock_out IS NOT NULL`: insert a new `TimeEntryStatus` row with `Status='submitted'`, `UserId=17` (Christopher; matches the established `COALESCE(@CreatedByUserId, 17)` system-context pattern), `Note='Auto-submitted by midnight cutoff'`. Single transaction. Returns count of submitted entries. Skip already-non-draft entries (idempotent).
- [ ] **Apply the new sproc** via `python scripts/run_sql.py entities/time_entry/sql/dbo.time_entry.sql` (or as a fresh migration file once base-rebake from the Gap 2 follow-up TODO is done).
- [ ] **Admin endpoint** `POST /api/v1/admin/time-tracking/auto-submit-stale` — `X-Drain-Secret` gated (returns 503 when unset, 401 on mismatch — match existing admin pattern in `shared/api/admin.py`). Calls the sproc; returns `{"status": "accepted", "count": N}`. File: `entities/time_entry/api/router.py` (or sibling `admin.py` if convention prefers).
- [ ] **Scheduler timer** in `build.one.scheduler/function_app.py`. Cadence: daily at **06:00 UTC** (= 02:00 ET) — past midnight ET, before any super reopens the app the next morning. POSTs to the admin endpoint with `DRAIN_SECRET` header. Document alongside the existing 18 timers.
- [ ] **Verification**: (a) run sproc once manually, confirm draft test data transitions and second call is no-op; (b) `curl -X POST -H "X-Drain-Secret: $DRAIN_SECRET" $BASE_URL/api/v1/admin/time-tracking/auto-submit-stale` returns expected envelope; (c) check `az functionapp function list` shows the new timer; (d) Application Insights confirms the next fire.

**Decisions deferred until pickup:**
- Whether to use `UserId=17` (Christopher) for the audit row or build a dedicated `system-auto-submit` agent user (cleaner audit, slight extra setup). The `Note` field already disambiguates; user 17 is consistent with the established system-context pattern. Likely fine to defer the dedicated user until needed.
- Whether to ship a "preview / dry-run" admin endpoint that returns the count of would-be-submitted entries without actually submitting. Useful for first-week validation; minor extra code.
- Multi-timezone handling: today single-customer in Brattleboro VT (Eastern). 06:00 UTC works. If we expand westward (CT/MT/PT), bump the timer to **09:00 UTC** so it fires safely past midnight in all US timezones.

**Fallback path if auto-submit proves wrong:** Phase 1g (banner + notification) was scoped at ~150-200 lines of iOS code — see session transcript or memory. Larger scope, preserves the user's intentional review beat, costs more to maintain. Auto-submit is the simpler bet; revisit if PMs complain about being unable to edit auto-submitted days from the back office, or if supers want the review-before-submit beat back.

## Side discoveries from bill-review session (2026-05-18)

Surfaced during the per-bill review + Complete of 42 Draft+(no-review) bills via `POST /api/v1/complete/bill/{public_id}`. None blocked the session; all worth fixing.

- [x] ~~**`POST /api/v1/create/bill-credit-line-item` returns 400.**~~ Fixed 2026-07-11 (option (b)). Root cause: the instant-workflow dispatcher (`core/workflow/business/instant.py:359`) injects `tenant_id` into every service call as a keyword, but `BillCreditLineItemService.create` (`entities/bill_credit_line_item/business/service.py`) lacked the param and had no `**kwargs` → `TypeError` → `success=False` → router 400. Fix: added `tenant_id: Optional[int] = None` as the first keyword-only param on `create()` (accepted-but-ignored, matching sibling `update_by_public_id`; `CompanyId DEFAULT` handles tenancy — no behavior change). **A DB-free audit (AST scan of all 47 instant-workflow entities) found the sibling `delete_by_public_id` on the SAME entity broken by the identical omission** (`delete`→`delete_by_public_id`, dispatcher injects `tenant_id`) → `DELETE /api/v1/delete/bill-credit-line-item/{id}` also 400'd; folded its one-line fix into the same unit (`public_id` positional, then `*, tenant_id`). Regression test `tests/test_bill_credit_line_item_signature.py` locks the convention for both methods (present, keyword-only, default `None`). It was the only other at-risk method in the audit. Codex PASS; `pytest` 54 green.
- [x] ~~**Make the instant-workflow dispatcher signature-aware (shared-infra follow-up, from the 2026-07-11 bill-credit-line-item fix).**~~ Done 2026-07-11. `core/workflow/business/instant.py` now uses `_build_service_kwargs()` (`inspect.signature`) to inject `tenant_id` only when the target method accepts it (explicit `tenant_id` param or `**kwargs`); otherwise kwargs pass through unchanged — a future service omission degrades gracefully instead of `TypeError` → 400. Zero behavior change for current code: the 2026-07-11 AST audit found all 47 registry entities × 135 dispatched methods already accept `tenant_id` or `**kwargs`. Regression tests: `tests/test_instant_dispatch_tenant_injection.py`. Per-service `tenant_id` params are now belt-and-suspenders rather than load-bearing.
- [ ] **HP2 project naming inconsistency** — DB has `Project.Name = "HP2 - 4406 Harding Pike"` but every invoice (Walker Lumber DOC#s 802791/191612/193416/192967/195415/195997/etc., JTA `26-8778`, Liaison Home Automation `9329`) ships to "4406 Harding **Place**" / "4406 HARDING PL". Likely a DB typo at project creation. Verify with the customer / Austin which street name is correct (Pike vs Place), then update `Project.Name` and `Project.AddressLine1` (if separately stored). The wrong suffix mostly cosmetic today, but it makes the SharePoint folder prefix "HP2" inconsistent with the actual street name on archived invoice filenames.

Root cause: `MsOutboxWorker._link_attachment_if_requested` (`integrations/ms/outbox/business/worker.py:750-789`) passes `uploaded_item.get("item_id")` (a MS Graph item_id string like `017ZKYN5ZCTDWCW7UPQNFJHBZC6XZYUWJC`) to `DriveItemAttachmentConnector().link_driveitem_to_attachment(attachment_id, ms_driveitem_id)` — but that connector's `ms_driveitem_id` parameter is typed `int` (local DB PK to `ms.DriveItem.Id`), not a string. The INSERT either fails the FK check or coerces garbage, then the `except Exception` swallows the error per the "non-fatal" docstring. **Fix**: the worker needs to (1) INSERT a `ms.DriveItem` row from the `uploaded_item` payload first, capture its local int PK, then (2) pass *that* PK to `link_driveitem_to_attachment`. Or, simpler: change the linking to be keyed off `MsDriveItemId` (the string) rather than `ms.DriveItem.Id` (the int). Not blocking anything today because nothing reads `ms.DriveItemAttachment`. Becomes blocking if/when SharePoint-link surfaces are wired (e.g. "show me the SharePoint location for this attachment" UI button).

## Mobile auth — refresh token UX (2026-06-02)

Surfaced during the iOS BuildOneApp.swift failure-mode review pass. Two related but distinct issues with the mobile auth flow; neither was blocking the iOS work but both are worth fixing before more field workers come online.

- [ ] **Bump `refresh_token_expire_seconds` from 86400 (24h) to ~30-90 days for the mobile flow.** Current value forces field workers to re-log in roughly every shift — 24h is a "kiosk / high-security web app" TTL, not a "field iPhone" TTL. Industry norm for mobile refresh tokens is 30-90 days because mobile devices spend long stretches in airplane mode / dead zones / closed. Biometric (Face ID) softens the re-login UX into a single tap, but only for users who opted in during onboarding AND whose biometric Keychain item hasn't been invalidated (Face ID re-enrollment invalidates it). One-line change in App Service Application Settings: `refresh_token_expire_seconds=2592000` (30d) or `7776000` (90d). The iOS code already handles whatever expiry the server returns and stores the rotated refresh_token on each `/mobile/auth/refresh` response — no client-side change needed.
  - **Web auth path is unaffected**: web uses the cookie-based refresh flow on a different endpoint (`/auth/refresh`), and the cookie's `max_age` is set from the same `refresh_token_expire_seconds`. If you want a different TTL for web vs mobile, the cleanest split is a separate `mobile_refresh_token_expire_seconds` setting + `MobileAuthService.refresh_access_token()` using that one; today they share the value.
  - **Verification after bump**: log in on the iOS app, then check the JWT's `exp` claim on the refresh token returned by `/mobile/auth/login` — should equal `now + 2592000` (30d). Optionally, verify a 25-hour-old refresh token still works by manually setting the `RefreshToken.CreatedAt` back in the DB and hitting `/mobile/auth/refresh`.

- [ ] **Mobile refresh-token rotation race on app SIGKILL (lower priority).** `POST /mobile/auth/refresh` rotates: server creates new refresh token `R2`, invalidates `R1`, returns `R2`. iOS persists `R2` to Keychain after the response lands ([AuthenticatedSession.swift:89-91](https://github.com/chriszubrod/build.one.ios/blob/master/BuildOne/Services/BuildOneAPI/AuthenticatedSession.swift#L89-L91)). If the app is killed between (a) the server's rotation and (b) the Keychain write committing, the user is locked out — next launch sends `R1`, server says "rotated out", iOS surfaces `tokenRefreshFailed` → critical alert → login screen. **In production** this requires a crash / OOM kill / user force-quit during the ~10-50ms refresh window — rare but real. **In dev** it's far more common: Xcode's Stop button is SIGKILL, and we hit this during the iOS work pass (session was observed routed to login on 2026-06-02 after heavy rebuild iteration). Two possible fixes, both server-side:
  1. **Grace window (preferred)**: keep `R1` valid for ~5 minutes after `R2` is issued so a client that didn't persist `R2` can still authenticate with `R1` once. Requires `RefreshToken.RotatedFromId` + `RotatedAt` columns + a `RevokeOldRotation` background sweep that purges grace-window tokens after the window. Standard OAuth pattern.
  2. **No-op no-rotate response**: detect "already rotated" specifically (vs "expired") and return the previously-issued `R2` instead of 401, with idempotency keyed off the request body. Simpler but breaks the rotation invariant (caller could replay forever within the grace).
  - Option 1 is the correct shape long-term; defer until refresh failures appear in prod logs OR the iOS team builds a dev-loop tool that needs it.

- [ ] **Device-token rebind cleanup on `/mobile/device-token/register` (push leak prevention).** Today the iOS app calls `POST /mobile/device-token/deactivate` during `userInitiatedLogout` to clean up the `(deviceToken → userId)` mapping server-side. That call requires the just-logged-out user's auth token to authenticate, and silently fails if the network is down at the moment Sign Out is tapped (logged via `os.Logger` subsystem `one.build.app` category `push` from 2026-06-02). The premise that this is "non-fatal because the token expires naturally" is **wrong** — APNs device tokens don't expire on a schedule (they're stable per device + app), and the server-side mapping persists indefinitely. Without cleanup, when User B logs in on the same device, the dormant `(token, UserA)` mapping can leak pushes addressed to UserA into UserB's notification center once the push sender ships. **Fix shape**: `/mobile/device-token/register` should atomically clear any prior `(token, *)` mappings before/while inserting the new `(token, newUser)` row. One device-token has at most one user at a time; the server is the only place that has authoritative knowledge to enforce that invariant (the client can't retry post-logout because its auth context is already cleared). Touches `dbo.DeviceToken` (likely a new `UpsertDeviceTokenAndClearPriorMappings` sproc or an `INSTEAD OF INSERT` trigger) and possibly the `entities/device_token/` service to thread the cleanup through. Higher priority **before** the push sender ships — once notifications start going out, this becomes a privacy bug. Today (no sender) the failure surface is purely latent.



- [ ] **BillCredit completion never writes the `DriveItemAttachment` mapping** (found during QBO Step 5 review, 2026-06-22). `BillCreditCompleteService` (completion path) enqueues the SharePoint upload with `attachment_id`, but the credit-side link write extracts the DriveItem id with a different key than the outbox worker's `uploaded_item.get("item_id") or uploaded_item.get("id")`, so the `attachment → DriveItem` link is never persisted for completed credits. Effect: `DriveItemAttachmentConnector.is_attachment_synced()` always returns False for credit attachments; any feature relying on that link (unlink, sync-state checks) is blind to credit uploads. Bills/Expenses are unaffected (worker links them correctly). Low urgency — SharePoint upload itself still works (conflictBehavior=replace); only the back-link is missing. Fix: align the credit completion's DriveItem-id extraction with the worker's, or let the worker's `_link_attachment_if_requested` be the sole linker. NOT a QBO-pull bug (Step 5 removed its dependency on this link).

- [ ] **QBO-pulled entities have no Workflow/WorkflowEvent audit row + default to CreatedByUserId=17** (found during QBO Step 7, 2026-06-22). `ProcessEngine.execute_synchronous` (the only writer of Workflow/WorkflowEvent rows) is invoked ONLY in the API routers (`entities/{bill,expense,bill_credit}/api/router.py`); the QBO pull goes scripts → connectors → `service.create/update` directly, bypassing it. So pulled bills/expenses/credits get NO audit-trail rows, and because `assert_cli_system_admin()` sets `current_user_id=None`, their entity-row `CreatedByUserId` falls to the column DEFAULT (17 = Christopher) — mildly misleading (a person who didn't create them). **Currently latent**: Workflow/WorkflowEvent tables are internal-only (no React surface / admin endpoint / report consumes them as of 2026-06), and the pull batch IS tracked via `Sync` records + `intake_source`. **Decision (Step 7): ACCEPT the audit gap, DEFER the attribution fix.** The attribution fix is NOT trivial: `current_user_id` is the SAME ContextVar that drives RBAC user-scoping, so setting it to a real user to fix attribution also changes the pull's access-scoping identity. Pull reads are almost all by-id/by-mapping (not user-scoped per "list-only first"), so it's *probably* safe, but it should be done deliberately: (1) create a dedicated `qbo_sync` (or reuse `claude_agent`) system user, (2) audit every read in the post-Steps-4-6 pull path to confirm none is user-scoped, (3) set `set_authz_context(user_id=<sync_user>, is_system_admin=True)` in the pull. Full Workflow-audit integration (writing Workflow rows per pulled entity) is over-engineering while nothing reads those rows — revisit if a Workflow-audit surface or compliance requirement appears.

- [ ] **Push-to-QBO (app→QBO) is Bill-only despite field_ownership declaring Expense + BillCredit bidirectional** (found during QBO Step 8, 2026-06-22; OUT OF SCOPE for the pull audit — logged for a future OUTBOUND / round-trip review). Current outbound state:
  - **Bill — fully wired (LIVE, load-bearing — do NOT remove):** `complete_bill` → `_enqueue_qbo_sync` (`entities/bill/business/service.py:1557,1606`) → `QboOutbox` kind `sync_bill_to_qbo` → worker `_handle_sync_bill` (`integrations/intuit/qbo/outbox/business/worker.py:64`) → `BillBillConnector.sync_to_qbo_bill`.
  - **Expense (Purchase) — machinery COMPLETE but DORMANT (trigger never wired):** connector `PurchaseExpenseConnector.sync_to_qbo_purchase` exists (`integrations/intuit/qbo/purchase/connector/expense/business/service.py:260`) + worker handler `_handle_sync_expense` dispatches kind `sync_expense_to_qbo` (`outbox/worker.py:65,460`), but NOTHING enqueues it (`complete_expense` has no `_enqueue_qbo_sync`; no push endpoint). One wiring line away from live.
  - **BillCredit (VendorCredit) — NOT implemented:** no `sync_to_qbo` connector in `integrations/intuit/qbo/vendorcredit/`, no worker handler (dispatch table is bill/expense/invoice only). Only the declaration exists in the field-ownership registry.
  - **field_ownership.py declares all four bidirectional** (Bill:88, Invoice:123, Purchase=Expense:143, VendorCredit=BillCredit:170) with APP_OWNED/QBO_OWNED rules — but only Bill is actually wired. The declarations look aspirational, not a wired contract. (Invoice push connector+handler also exist but completion deliberately doesn't push per CLAUDE.md.)
  - **Circular-push safety (why the asymmetry hasn't bitten):** push only fires on COMPLETION, and QBO-pulled entities are projected is_draft=False and never go through complete_*(), so a pulled entity never pushes back. Enabling expense push would therefore only affect APP-completed expenses, not pulled ones — but the connector must be verified mapping-aware (upsert, not blind-create) first.
  - **Decision:** treat enabling expense push (small — machinery exists) and building BillCredit push (larger lift) as DELIBERATE outbound features gated on a real "we create these in-app and need them in QBO" need + mapping-aware idempotency — NOT a mechanical "make them match the pull" change. Do NOT remove the working Bill push.

## Expense coding cockpit (U-005) — Pass-2 deferrals (logged 2026-07-14, Phase A)

Quality-pass findings the Phase-A reviewers surfaced but that were deliberately deferred (all non-blocking; the code-grounded fixes from Pass-1 correctness already landed):

- [ ] **[U-005][efficiency] Set-based seed for `get_expense_coding_queue`.** The first-ever queue load lazily seeds a `dbo.ExpenseCodingItem` per unseeded 58999 line via a per-row `upsert_from_queue` (each ~3 sproc round-trips incl. `_resolve_vendor_id`), then re-reads the whole queue → up to ~1,300 round-trips on the cold ~440-row first load. **Cold path / one-time**: steady-state inflow is ~1–2/day and `needs_reseed` short-circuits once every line has a row. Fix = a single `INSERT dbo.ExpenseCodingItem (...) SELECT ... WHERE eci.PublicId IS NULL` seed sproc that resolves `VendorId` inline via the qbo-vendor→vendor join the read-model already does (eliminates the per-row `_resolve_vendor_id`), seed-then-select so the trailing re-read folds in. Best done when Phase B builds the seed+suggest flow together. (Flagged at Gate 2 as a known one-time first-load cost.) **U-284v note (2026-08-21):** `_resolve_vendor_id`'s per-row cost went from 2 round trips to up to 3 (direct dbo attempt + verify, falling back to the original 2-hop on a miss/disagreement — see the `[reuse]` item below) on the still-common case where a `VendorVendor` mapping exists. Doesn't change this fix's shape — the set-based seed sproc eliminates the Python-side resolver entirely regardless of its internal hop count — just makes the cold-path number a bit worse until this ships.
- [ ] **[U-005][altitude] Shared status-vocabulary constant.** The 8-value lifecycle (`pending|suggested|flagged|confirmed|enqueued|written|changed_in_qbo|error`) lives in the DDL default, the metrics sproc enumeration, and the metrics repo keys. Phase A Python does NOT hardcode the values (`record_suggestion` passes `status` through; model types it as free `str`), so drift is latent until Phase B/C emit `'enqueued'`/`'written'`/`'error'` from Python. Anchor then via a small `shared/expense_coding_status.py` (constants + `ALL_STATUSES`), mirroring `shared/labor_status.py`. Optional DB-side `CHECK` on `[Status]` (precedent: `dbo.expensefolderrunitem`).
- [ ] **[U-005][reuse] Shared `resolve_dbo_vendor_id(qbo_id, realm_id)` helper — now a SECOND duplicated layer, not just one.** The legacy `qbo_id → QboVendor → VendorVendor → vendor_id` chain was inlined in 4 places (`ExpenseCodingItemService._resolve_vendor_id` + 3 connector services); no shared helper exists, flagged as a future cross-cutting cleanup. **U-284v (2026-08-21)** added a SECOND near-identical layer on top of all 4 (plus a 5th, Bill's push-side `_get_qbo_vendor_ref`) — "try `dbo.Vendor`'s native QboId directly, verify via the shared `verify_vendor_qbo_identity`, fall back to the original chain on a miss/disagreement" — deliberately hand-copied 5x rather than extracted, mirroring the identical, already-accepted precedent for `_get_project_public_id` (see the `[U-060]` follow-up above at "Follow-up (U-022 out-of-scope)" — same rationale: "Lift into one shared resolver when multi-realm lands," not a Phase-4/repoint-time change). Each of the 5 call sites now carries a breadcrumb comment naming the other four, mirroring `_get_project_public_id`'s own breadcrumb convention, so a future consolidation pass can find all 5 by name (the exact failure mode `_get_project_public_id`'s 4th copy hit before U-060 — "inline and unnamed," undercounted by every prior inventory). Call sites: `entities/expense_coding_item/business/service.py::_resolve_vendor_id`, `integrations/intuit/qbo/bill/connector/bill/business/service.py::_get_vendor_public_id` (pull) + `::_get_qbo_vendor_ref` (push), `integrations/intuit/qbo/purchase/connector/expense/business/service.py::_get_vendor_public_id`, `integrations/intuit/qbo/vendorcredit/connector/bill_credit/business/service.py::_get_vendor_public_id`. **U-299 (2026-08-22)** added a 6th and 7th copy, `resolve_local_vendor_id`, in `scripts/generate_payment_remittance.py` and `scripts/backfill_qbo_bills.py` — the last two raw-SQL vendor-ref consumers, same dbo-first→verify→legacy-fallback shape, same breadcrumb convention naming their siblings. One future unit should fold BOTH layers into one shared resolver for both the Vendor and Project families together, once whatever "when multi-realm lands" was waiting for actually lands.
- [ ] **[U-005][consistency] Confidence serialization differs across the two read paths.** The queue row-mapper returns `suggestion_confidence` as `Decimal`; the model's `to_dict` stringifies it. Harmless for the web client (FastAPI encodes both), but align when Phase D consumes both surfaces.
- [ ] **[U-005][efficiency] Vendor-history per-batch cache (Phase B suggest).** `ExpenseCodingSuggestionService.suggest_pending` re-runs `ReadVendorDominantSubCostCode` per item; the same vendor recurs many times in a Ramp feed (Home Depot, Amazon…), so the identical aggregation repeats within one batch. A batch-scoped `dict[vendor_id → result]` memo collapses the one-time ~441 run's hundreds of aggregations to ~#distinct-vendors. Premature at ~1–2/day steady state; adopt if the first-run window or a hot path matters.
- [ ] **[U-005][B.2 trigger-surface] Move the suggest trigger off the sync `can_update` POST when Haiku lands.** Phase B's `POST /expense-coding/suggest` runs the deterministic resolver batch on the request thread — fine now. When Phase B.2 swaps a Haiku hint-extractor into `extract_hints`, each item becomes a blocking HTTP call; a synchronous batch (up to `max_items`) on a user-triggered POST won't scale. Every comparable external-I/O batch here is scheduler-driven behind `DRAIN_SECRET` (email `process_one`, bill-folder `tick`). B.2 must migrate the trigger to a scheduler tick / outbox job (admin/DRAIN_SECRET-gated), not the RBAC POST — fold into the B.2 plan so it isn't a surprise.
- [ ] **[U-146][campaign] Derive remaining-dup counts mechanically in `test_sproc_single_source.py`.** The module docstring's "remaining entities" tally (`contract_labor=7, email_message=7, …`) is hand-edited every conversion unit and drifts (U-146 found the previous `contract_labor=10` was stale inventory, measured 7). The guard's own `_sql_index()` scanner + the ledger could compute the per-entity remainder; emit it from a tiny helper/test instead of hand-maintaining prose. Campaign-wide, not per-unit.
- [ ] **[U-146][campaign] Align sibling `sql/README.md` shape.** user_company's README narrows the single-source claim to "table bootstrap + sprocs" (attribution columns stay authoritative in migration 001); user_role/company/organization have the identical 001-adds-columns situation but claim the whole table. Backport the narrowing (or standardize a template) across converted entities in one sweep.

## U-238a follow-ups (found during Pass 1 hunt, deferred)

- [ ] **P2: `backfill_qbo_identity_headers.py` stamps one row at a time** via a per-row sproc EXEC instead of a single set-based UPDATE; fine at current scale (~32K rows total, RCSI is on so reads aren't blocked) but worth a set-based rewrite before this pattern is reused for line-item/reference sub-units with larger row counts.
- [ ] **P3: `backfill_qbo_identity_headers.py`'s `--entity all` has no per-entity isolation** — one entity's exception aborts all entities after it in iteration order with no explicit "N of 5 skipped" accounting.
- [ ] **P2: `classify_qbo_identity_drift()` has no distinct classification for "mapping row exists but its staging join is empty"** (dangling FK to a deleted/missing staging row) — it's silently folded into `pending_backfill` or `drift`, obscuring the real root cause from an operator reading the drift report.
- [ ] **P2: no test exercises `classify_qbo_identity_drift`'s fallthrough branch with a None staging value** (the exact shape above) — a future refactor could silently change this classification without failing any test.
- [ ] **P3: the 5 new `Set<Entity>QboIdentity` sprocs were not registered in `tests/test_update_read_column_parity.py`'s `_NULL_GUARD_EXCEPTIONS`** — a hermetic (no-DB) regression test that would have caught an inverted/dropped CASE-WHEN guard wasn't reused for this unit's new sprocs; register them there as a followup.
- [ ] **P3: `backfill_qbo_identity_headers.py`'s `_batch_select_sql` doesn't exclude staging rows with NULL QboId/RealmId** — a mapped-but-incomplete-staging-identity row is silently miscounted as "no mapping" rather than "mapping exists but staging data is incomplete" in the post-run summary.
- [ ] **P2 (found by Pass 2 /simplify, efficiency lens): `set_qbo_identity()` opens its own `get_connection()` on every call, including at live steady-state dual-write call sites that already used/use another connection for the same row a few lines away** (e.g. `create_mapping()` calling it right before `mapping_repo.create()`'s own connection; the update-branch dual-write calling it right after `<entity>_service.update_by_public_id()`'s own connection). Not the backfill's already-logged per-row-EXEC issue — this is the ongoing per-pull-tick cost, forever, of a second connection-open/EXEC/commit/close cycle per entity processed. Cheapest fix: an optional `cursor` param on `set_qbo_identity()` so callers that already hold one can pass it through instead of opening a second.
- [ ] **P2 (found by Pass 2 /simplify, efficiency lens): `check_qbo_identity_drift_headers.py` pulls every matching row over the wire and classifies it in a Python loop** (`_scan_entity`) when the classification (`match`/`drift`/`pending_backfill`/`orphan_dbo_value`) is a pure function of already-JOINed columns and could be a `CASE` expression inside a `SELECT classification, COUNT(*) ... GROUP BY classification` — one round trip returning ≤4 rows per entity instead of transferring every row's columns for a result that collapses to a handful of integers. Row-level detail is only actually needed for the ≤5 drift/orphan samples printed, which could be a small follow-up `TOP 5` query. Related to, but distinct from, the already-logged full-table-scan-shape item above (that one is about query pagination; this one is about where the classification computation happens).
- [ ] **P2 (found by Pass 2 /simplify, altitude lens): the sproc "steal identity from whoever else holds it" step does two different jobs, and only one is a real concurrency fix.** Job A (legitimate, required): makes the `CustomerProject` repoint/heal flow's clear-then-stamp safe against the new filtered unique index when a stale row still holds the identity being moved — keep this. Job B (NOT achieved): it does **not** serialize two genuinely racing pull invocations. No `sp_getapplock` (or any lock) wraps `create_mapping()`/the pull → create-or-update-mapping sequence in any of the 5 connectors or their pull scripts — `qbo_app_lock()` (`integrations/intuit/qbo/base/locking.py`) is only used by the outbox drain and auth-token refresh today. Two overlapping pull invocations (scheduler tick + manual re-run, retry overlap) can still independently bind the same never-before-seen QBO id to two different local rows; the *only* thing preventing a silent double-bind today is the filtered unique index applied by `scripts/migrations/238a_qbo_identity_headers.sql` (which converts the race into a loud constraint-violation exception on the loser, cleanly rolled back by Fix 3's stamp-before-mapping-create ordering — **not** silent corruption, but also not prevented). Real fix is `sp_getapplock` serialization of the pull → create/update-mapping path per entity type, matching the existing pattern — real design work (lock granularity: per-qbo-id vs per-entity-type vs per-realm), out of scope here. **Load-bearing note: this is why SQL-FIRST deploy order matters more than usual for this unit** — the migration's unique index must be live before the dual-write code is, or the race is silently unprotected instead of loudly caught.
- [ ] **P3 (found by Pass 2 /simplify, altitude lens): no shared mechanism enforces "call `set_qbo_identity` at both the create branch AND every update/re-pull branch."** This unit's Fix 5 added the update-branch calls by hand at the specific sites the Pass-1 hunt found stale; nothing stops a future line-item/reference sub-unit (Phase 2's remaining scope) from forgetting to replicate this at one of its own update branches, re-introducing the same "SyncToken freezes forever after first mapping" bug U-238a fixed here. Not a base-class/mixin — this codebase's established idiom for cross-connector concerns is composition via free functions in `integrations/intuit/qbo/base/*.py` (`field_ownership.py`, `pull_race.py`, `reconciliation_recorder.py`), not inheritance; retrofitting a base class onto 5 already-shipped, materially different connectors risks a leaky abstraction. Right-sized fix: a code comment at each `set_qbo_identity` call site (mirroring the existing U-006 cross-reference comment style in `invoice/connector/invoice/business/service.py`) plus this TODO note, so the next sub-unit's Map phase greps for the pattern deliberately instead of rediscovering it the hard way.

## U-252 follow-up (booked, not shipped, 2026-08-17) — QBO Vendor DisplayName rename drift signal

- [ ] **(h) No drift signal for a QBO-side Vendor DisplayName rename after the local Vendor is already mapped + curated.** `VendorVendorConnector._apply_vendor_fields_and_sync` (`integrations/intuit/qbo/vendor/connector/vendor/business/service.py`) calls `preserve_human_edited_name(vendor.name, incoming_name)`, which — per `field_ownership.py`'s documented "ACCEPTED RESIDUAL" — silently keeps the curated `Vendor.Name` forever once it's non-blank, ignoring any later QBO-side rename. That's the *correct* behavior (never clobber W-9/DBA curation), but there's no observability today when it actually happens. **Why this wasn't built in U-252:** the only in-scope comparison (`connector/**`, no SQL/schema changes) is curated-local-name vs incoming-QBO-name — and those are *expected* to differ permanently for every vendor with a DBA (per `feedback_vendor_name_dba_convention.md`), not just on a genuine rename. Recording an issue on that comparison would write a `ReconciliationIssue` row for a large fraction of vendors on **every single pull, forever** — noise, not signal, and a materially different (and worse) volume profile than every other connector-level detector in this file (duplicate/orphan), which only fire on genuinely anomalous, human-resolvable states. **Right-sized fix:** persist a "last-seen QBO DisplayName" (e.g. a new column on the `qbo.Vendor` staging row, or on the `VendorVendor` mapping row) so the pull can diff *this pull's* DisplayName against *last pull's*, and only flag on an actual change — a real change (not "differs from curated"). That's a schema change spanning the staging tier (`vendor/business/service.py`, `vendor/persistence/repo.py`, `vendor/sql/`) in addition to the connector, all outside U-252's `connector/**`-only scope. Needs its own unit with a migration.

## U-266 follow-ups (billing-period-start fix, 2026-08-18) — deferred, non-blocking

`ContractLabor.calculate_billing_period_start()` was fixed to return the period START (1st/16th) instead of the END (15th/last-day) it wrongly returned before — the whole system (existing rows, `ReadContractLaborsByStatus`'s exact-match filter) keys on START. Caller audit + a live read-only DB check confirmed the fix is safe (no migration needed for billing correctness — see U-266 commit), but the hunt + `/simplify` surfaced real, deliberately-deferred items:

- [ ] **P2 (data, found via live DB check at Gate 2): 402 already-`billed` `dbo.ContractLabor` rows still carry the pre-fix END-date convention** (day 15/28/31) in `BillingPeriodStart`, mixed with rows using the START convention (1st/16th) — out of 1137 total rows, 35% are END-convention, virtually all (402/403) already `billed`. This is historical, not something U-266 introduces or worsens (billed rows are no longer queried by future billing runs), but it means: (1) the billing-period dropdown (`ReadContractLaborDistinctBillingPeriods`, a plain `SELECT DISTINCT`) will keep showing the same logical period twice — once under each convention — with no normalization; (2) `pdf_service.py::generate_pdfs_for_billed_entries`'s exact-match `billing_period_start` filter will keep missing END-convention rows when queried with a START-convention value. Purely cosmetic (both are historical/already-billed), not urgent. Fix, if ever warranted by UX complaints: a one-time backfill normalizing all `billed`-status END-convention rows to their equivalent START value (day 15→01, last-day→16, same month) — deliberately NOT done in U-266 per its HARD OUT (no migration).
- [ ] **P3 (altitude, found by Pass 1 hunt + Pass 2 `/simplify`): three independent hardcoded copies of the "day ≤ 15 → first half" boundary** — `ContractLabor.calculate_billing_period_start` (`entities/contract_labor/business/model.py`, period START), `ContractLaborBillService._period_end_for` (`entities/contract_labor/business/bill_service.py`, period END — U-266 already consolidated this one from 3 copies down to 1 shared method, used by 4 call sites), and `ContractLaborBillService.get_due_date` (`bill_service.py`, period-end → due-date, a different computation but sharing the same day-15/last-day classification, unchanged by U-266). If the mid-month cutoff day (15/16) ever changes, all three need updating in lockstep and nothing enforces that. Deeper fix: a single boundary constant/classifier shared by all three — not done in U-266 because it would require touching `get_due_date` (untouched, working, pre-existing) and reaching across from `bill_service.py` into `model.py`'s unrelated `ContractLabor` class — judged well outside this diff's scope.
- [ ] **P3 (altitude, found by Pass 2 `/simplify`): `preview_pdf_for_vendor`/`_generate_combined_pdf` groups a vendor's ready entries by `project_id` alone, with no period dimension** — unlike `generate_bills_for_vendor`, which groups by `(project_id, period_end)`. U-266 repointed this path's `billing_period` derivation from the now-repurposed `entry.billing_period_start` field to `self._period_end_for(work_date)` off one representative ("first") entry in the group — correct for a single-period group, but if a vendor's ready entries for one project span two billing periods, the combined preview PDF shows one invoice-number/due-date derived from only the first entry's period, silently blending two periods into one preview page. Preview-only (never persisted — the real `generate_bills_for_vendor` bill/invoice-number IS period-scoped correctly), so low severity, but a genuine gap. Deeper fix: group `preview_pdf_for_vendor` by `(project_id, period_end)` the same way `generate_bills_for_vendor` already does, and carry the real per-group period through instead of re-deriving from one representative entry — not done in U-266 because it changes preview-PDF grouping/invoice-count behavior, out of scope for a correctness-only unit.

## U-297 follow-ups (customer-parent reference-resolver repoint, 2026-08-22) — deferred, non-blocking

`CustomerProjectConnector`'s parent-Customer lookup was repointed off the `qbo.Customer` →
`qbo.CustomerCustomer` two-hop onto `dbo.Customer`'s native `QboId`/`RealmId`, using the canonical
dbo-first → verify → legacy-fallback shape. Live-prod equivalence proven clean (136/136 job
sub-customers resolve identically, every direct hit through the strict `TRUST_AGREES` verify arm).
These were surfaced during the unit and deliberately not built:

- [x] **P2 (H1 — the verify engine is LOCAL-SIDE ONLY; shared by all three wrappers). CLOSED by U-306
  (2026-08-23).** Neither candidate fix below was taken as-is — U-306's JOIN'd-read redesign (the
  efficiency item at the bottom of this list) made a real reverse-direction check free instead of
  "strictly more than the legacy path," so the engine now checks it directly rather than choosing
  between REFUSE-on-unmapped (a) or a `trust_unmapped` posture flag (b). See
  `base/identity_consistency.py::_verify_dbo_qbo_identity`'s docstring + `IdentityCheckResult`.
  `base/identity_consistency.py::_verify_dbo_qbo_identity` asks only "does this dbo row's *own*
  mapping row agree?" and **TRUSTS when the row has no mapping at all** (`:57-58`). It is therefore
  structurally blind to the opposite direction — the mapping table still binding this external id to
  a **different** local row, which is exactly what `identity_fastpath.resolve_mapping_state` exists
  to catch (`:75-79`). Concretely, if a `dbo.Customer` ever carries a parent's QboId with no
  `CustomerCustomer` row while `qbo.CustomerCustomer` still binds that QboCustomer to a *different*
  `dbo.Customer`, the resolver would silently re-parent a Project on the next 4-hourly tick, with no
  reconciliation issue recorded and no undo path (`UpdateProjectById`'s CASE WHEN guard means
  `CustomerId` can be replaced but never cleared). **Why not fixed here:** closing it inside a
  *reference resolver* is self-defeating — reading the qbo side requires the staging hop the
  resolver exists to skip, so a both-directions verify costs strictly more than the legacy path it
  replaces. **Measured population is zero** (live 2026-08-22: 0 stamped `dbo.Customer` rows lack a
  `CustomerCustomer` row; 73/73 stamped; 0 half-stamped). Two candidate right-depth fixes if this
  ever acquires a population: (a) make *this* resolver's verify strict — treat "no mapping row" as
  REFUSE rather than TRUST, which costs nothing extra (same two reads) and would make every ledger
  row equivalent, not just the populated ones — at the cost of diverging from the nine sibling
  sites; or (b) give the shared engine an explicit `trust_unmapped: bool` so each family declares
  its own posture in one place. Note the sibling `CustomerCustomerConnector`'s own conflict text
  (`customer/connector/customer/business/service.py:258-263`) asserts the *pre-U-297* behavior
  ("CustomerProjectConnector's parent-Customer lookup will keep resolving to Customer {qbo_side}")
  — update that message whenever this is revisited.
- [ ] **P3 (realm asymmetry, latent — single-realm prod only).** The repointed primary is
  realm-EXACT (`ReadCustomerByQboIdAndRealmId`), while the legacy fallback it falls through to stays
  realm-BLIND (`ReadQboCustomerByQboId` has no realm predicate). Deliberate — realm-scoping the
  fallback would be a behavior change beyond this repoint, and would start *missing* parents whose
  staging row carries a NULL/other realm. Inert today (prod is single-realm `9130353016965726`, 0
  child/parent realm mismatches), but a second realm activates it immediately: the two paths key
  differently and could resolve different parents. Revisit as part of whatever unit lands
  multi-realm. Related: `SetCustomerQboIdentity` has **no** `@RealmComplete` atomic-pair guard (that
  exists only on the four line-item sprocs), so a half-stamped `QboId`-without-`RealmId` row is
  structurally possible and the fast path would never self-heal one.
- [ ] **P3 (consolidation — now a SIXTH hand-copy).** `_resolve_parent_customer_id` is a deliberate
  hand-copy of the same dbo-first/verify/legacy-fallback recipe as the four `_get_project_public_id`
  resolvers and the five vendor-ref resolvers, per the precedent already booked in the
  `[U-005][reuse]` entry above. It is the first one that returns a **local INT** written to a FK
  rather than a `public_id` used as a lookup key. Fold it into the same consolidation sweep — the
  breadcrumb comment above the method names its siblings so a future pass can find them all.
- [ ] **P3 (unbooked reader).** `docs/staging_removal_phase4_5_scoping.md:239` inventories only the
  four `_get_project_public_id` resolvers as readers of the `qbo.Customer` → `qbo.CustomerCustomer`
  hop; this parent-Customer lookup was a fifth, unlisted one. The verify step still reads BOTH
  staging tables on a direct hit, so U-297 does **not** by itself retire `qbo.Customer` for this
  connector — the scoping doc's Phase-6 DROP preconditions should reflect that.
- [ ] **P3 (Pass-2 deferrals, U-297).** Four behavior-preserving cleanups were identified and
  deliberately NOT applied because each reaches outside this unit's file scope into files other
  in-flight lanes are editing: (a) `tests/test_qbo_customer_project_heal.py:91-95` hand-rolls the
  fast-path stub while already importing `stub_qbo_identity_fastpath_miss` at line 19 — swap it
  (U-276-era code, untouched by this unit beyond constructor kwargs); (b) that same file lacks the
  `sys.path.insert(0, tests/)` preamble its `from conftest import ...` needs, so it **errors at
  collection when run standalone** and only passes in a full-suite run — pre-existing, reproduced at
  HEAD; (c) `_make_qbo_customer` in this unit's test file is a third copy of the same QboCustomer
  factory (`tests/test_u276_...:28-45`) — lift to `conftest.py` with the consolidation entry; (d)
  **CLOSED by U-306 (2026-08-23)** — the efficiency win described here (2 reads → 1 JOIN'd read,
  213 → 142 prod steady-state reads for this resolver) shipped via
  `base/sql/identity_consistency_reads.sql` + `read_identity_check` on all 4 mapping repos, and also
  closed the H1 item above for free once the read was redesigned.
- [ ] **P3 (U-306 follow-up — drop the now-dead `qbo_{customer,vendor,bill}_repo` verify-wrapper
  params).** `verify_project_qbo_identity` / `verify_vendor_qbo_identity` / `verify_bill_qbo_identity`
  / `verify_customer_qbo_identity` (`base/identity_consistency.py:158,189,220,267`) each still accept
  a `qbo_*_repo` kwarg that U-306's JOIN'd-read redesign made entirely unused — kept only so the ~6
  existing production call sites' kwargs didn't need to change (zero blast radius was this unit's
  explicit Gate-1 constraint). Once the staging-removal program (U-294 Phase-6 map) has retired
  enough of those call sites that updating the rest is cheap, drop the dead params from all 4
  wrapper signatures and their callers' kwargs (and the now-redundant `qbo_vendor_repo`/
  `qbo_bill_repo`/`qbo_customer_repo` construction at each call site, if nothing else there needs
  it). `tests/test_u306_identity_verify_engine.py::test_verify_never_touches_the_now_unused_qbo_repo`
  is the regression test proving they're genuinely dead today.
- [ ] **P3 (eager resolution, pre-existing shape).** `customer_id` is resolved at the top of
  `sync_from_qbo_customer` but four reachable paths never read it — the name-match bind (which
  creates the mapping, syncs addresses and returns WITHOUT writing CustomerId), and the three raise
  paths (inactive-unmapped guard, missing-Project, duplicate-QboCustomer). The memo caps the waste at
  once per distinct parent per run. Not changed here: deferring the call alters which DB reads happen
  on the raise paths and reorders debug logging, i.e. it is not behavior-preserving. Separately worth
  a correctness look in its own unit: **the name-match bind path never writes the resolved
  CustomerId onto the Project it binds**, so the same QBO sub-customer lands with or without a parent
  depending on which branch caught it.

  the three call sites' differing tails (self-heal/mapping-create, line's direct return, dbo-only's
  direct return) untouched — do NOT merge the three outer functions themselves, they are genuinely
  different algorithms.

  manual audit, not by construction. A 5th future line connector (or an edit to one of the 2 existing "warn"
  connectors) has no structural guard against reintroducing it. Right-depth fix: extract a shared
  `create_mapping_then_stamp(...)`-style helper (or two thin variants, one per failure-handling shape) in
  `integrations/intuit/qbo/base/`, so the invariant is enforced by the call signature rather than by
  convention. Own unit, needs its own Gate-1 — not U-339's scope, which was fix-the-live-bug +
  confirm-siblings-don't-share-it.

## U-355 follow-up (qbo.BillBill mapping retirement, 2026-09-01) — deferred, non-blocking

  Its manual-recovery sections (draft-collision recovery, zombie-header recovery) contain literal
  `INSERT INTO qbo.BillBill (...)` recipes for hand-repairing a bill's QBO mapping — qbo.BillBill is
  retired (U-355); dbo.Bill.QboId/RealmId (stamped via `BillService.repo.set_qbo_identity`) is the sole
  identity store now. An agent following this playbook verbatim post-DROP would either error (INSERT
  into a dropped table) or, pre-DROP, write to a table nothing reads anymore. Not fixed in this unit —
  it's a substantial content edit across a large, actively-used agent prompt (multiple recovery
  sub-cases reference the mapping table), out of proportion to a mechanical mapping-table retirement.
  Needs its own pass: read the current recovery flows end-to-end and rewrite each `qbo.BillBill` step
  as a direct `UPDATE dbo.Bill SET QboId = ?, RealmId = ? WHERE Id = ?` (mirroring
  `Set BillQboIdentity`'s theft-clear semantics — don't hand-write a raw UPDATE that skips it).

## U-362 follow-ups (qbo.InvoiceLineItemInvoiceLine retirement, 2026-09-02) — deferred, non-blocking

- [ ] **`scripts/repair_invoice_line_duplicates.py`'s `IsMapped` classification is now stale.** It derives
  `IsMapped` from `LEFT JOIN qbo.[InvoiceLineItemInvoiceLine] map ON map.[InvoiceLineItemId] = ili.[Id]`
  (the retired connector-level mapping) rather than `dbo.InvoiceLineItem.QboId IS NOT NULL`. Post-U-362,
  every newly-synced line reads `IsMapped=0` regardless of its real dbo-native identity — a legitimate new
  line sharing a content fingerprint with an older, still-mapped Manual sibling under the same invoice could
  now misclassify as a `repair` (deletion) candidate the script was built to prune. Mitigated by the script's
  own dry-run-by-default + `--expect-count` human-review gate (this is a manually-run maintenance tool, not
  an automated path), so booked here rather than fixed in-unit — mirrors the equivalent stale-script residual
  U-361 booked for vendorcredit (not found there since no analogous one-off repair script exists for that
  family). Fix: repoint the `IsMapped` derivation onto `ili.[QboId] IS NOT NULL` directly (no JOIN needed).
- [ ] **`entities/invoice_line_item/persistence/repo.py:164` and `entities/invoice_line_item/business/
  service.py:117`** still say "bypassing the qbo.InvoiceLine/qbo.InvoiceLineItemInvoiceLine staging/mapping
  tables" in their `read_by_qbo_identity` docstrings — accurate history (describes what U-293b's repoint
  bypassed) but slightly imprecise now that the mapping half is retired, not just bypassed. Cosmetic; reword
  in passing next time either file is touched.
- [ ] **`_clear_legacy_invoice_line_item_invoice_line_mapping` (entities/invoice_line_item/business/
  service.py) and `_clear_legacy_invoice_line_item_invoice_line_mapping_by_qbo_line_id`
  (integrations/intuit/qbo/invoice/business/service.py) become permanent no-ops once /em applies the
  `qbo.InvoiceLineItemInvoiceLine` DROP** — same U-353/U-365 lifecycle as the other deploy-gap bridges.
  Delete both (and their call sites) in the same pass that eventually cleans up U-361's analogous bridges,
  not before the DROP actually lands.

## U-362b follow-ups (source-linked line recognition fix, 2026-09-02) — deferred, non-blocking

Own adversarial `/code-review high` pass (8 angles) found 2 CONFIRMED P0/P1s, fixed in-unit (type-safety
theft-guard normalization; ambiguous-recognition-match refusal instead of a silent arbitrary pick — see
`entities/invoice_line_item/persistence/repo.py::read_by_linked_txn` and the connector's
`_recognize_source_linked_line`). The following PLAUSIBLE findings were assessed and deliberately deferred
— narrower/compounding-condition risk, or scope that would touch foundational already-reviewed code, both
inappropriate to fold into an emergency P0 patch:

- [ ] **`UpsertInvoiceLineItemSourceProvenance`'s unconditional mirror-not-merge write (pre-existing U-272
  code, not modified by U-362b) can null out a good `InvoiceLineItemSourceProvenance` row on a transient QBO
  omission of `LinkedTxn`** — this codebase documents exactly this QBO behavior (KI-32, see
  `integrations/intuit/qbo/invoice/external/client.py:317`). The new U-362b recognizer now depends more on
  that data staying populated than any prior consumer did. Requires TWO compounding conditions to actually
  manifest a duplicate (a transient LinkedTxn omission on some pull, THEN a Line.Id regeneration on a LATER
  pull before LinkedTxn reappears) — narrower than the primary bug this unit fixes, but real. Fix direction:
  make the provenance upsert preserve existing `LinkedTxnType`/`LinkedTxnId` when the incoming pull's values
  are null, mirroring the `preserve_human_edited_ref`/`preserve_human_edited_name` "don't clobber on a blank
  incoming value" pattern already established elsewhere in this codebase — but this changes already-shipped,
  already-audited U-272 write semantics with OTHER consumers (`ProposeInvoiceSourceLinks`,
  `ReadInvoiceSourceLinkLines`) that need re-verifying against the changed semantics; own unit.
- [ ] **`_recognize_source_linked_line` bypasses the `caches_preloaded=True` batch-pull optimization** —
  unlike the sibling `_manual_line_candidates`, it always issues a live DB round trip via
  `InvoiceLineItemService.read_by_linked_txn`, reintroducing the per-line round-trip cost
  `InvoiceInvoiceConnector.preload_caches()` exists to eliminate for large invoices. Fix direction: extend
  `preload_caches()` to also bulk-load `InvoiceLineItemSourceProvenance` into an in-memory
  `{(invoice_id, linked_txn_type, linked_txn_id): line_item}` index, mirroring `_manual_line_candidates`'s
  existing `caches_preloaded` branch.
- [ ] **The same new DB round trip runs INSIDE the per-line `qbo_dbo_line_identity_create:*` applock**
  (`base/identity_fastpath.py`'s `readopt_candidate()` call site), extending lock-hold duration for every
  source-linked MISS. Under contention (a large invoice pull, or several concurrent syncs on the same
  invoice) this raises the odds of hitting the 15s `lock_timeout_ms` on a sibling line. Same fix as the item
  above (an in-memory index) removes this too — both dissolve together once the cache is extended.
- [ ] **`ReadInvoiceLineItemByInvoiceIdAndLinkedTxn` (new sproc) hand-copies a 3rd independent SELECT
  column-list** already duplicated in `ReadInvoiceLineItemById` and `ReadInvoiceLineItemByInvoiceIdAndQboId`
  in the same file — the exact drift risk that already bit this file once (a missing `QboId`/`RealmId`
  projection made every CREATE self-rollback, fixed in U-362 proper). Own unit: extract a shared column-list
  (a SQL view, or at minimum a documented "keep these N sprocs' SELECT lists in sync" comment anchor).
- [ ] **Source-linked recognition (`_recognize_source_linked_line`) is connector-local, not a shared
  `base/` primitive** — unlike the pre-existing Manual-fingerprint matcher, which was deliberately extracted
  into `base/line_orphan_adopt.py::find_stale_identity_orphan` specifically so U-362/363/364 share one
  matcher instead of hand-copying it. If `bill_line_item`/`expense_line_item` (U-363/U-364) never grow an
  analogous "created by another entity's flow, linked after the fact" shape (their SQL has no `SourceType`/
  `InvoiceLineItemSourceProvenance` concept — checked, they don't today), this may never need lifting. Revisit
  if a future family does grow one; don't lift preemptively for a hypothetical second consumer.
- [ ] **Unverified against prod: does the runtime recognition fix alone already heal all 70 known
  mapped-but-unstamped rows, making the restored backfill/registry-row/3-script capability partially or
  fully redundant?** The runtime fix self-heals any row that still carries a valid
  `InvoiceLineItemSourceProvenance` entry on its next routine pull; the backfill's only unique value is
  healing a row that ALSO lacks a provenance row (unverifiable from the codebase alone). Kept the backfill
  as a belt-and-suspenders safety net per the original assignment's explicit instruction rather than assume
  redundancy. **/em: before or instead of running the backfill, consider querying prod for `COUNT(*)` of
  the 70 rows that have NO matching `InvoiceLineItemSourceProvenance` row — if 0, the runtime fix alone is
  sufficient and the backfill run (and the temporary registry restoration) can be skipped entirely.**
- [ ] **No automated invariant stops a future line-family retirement (U-363/U-364) from repeating this
  exact mistake** (removing backfill/recognition capability in the same commit that needs it, found only by
  a post-hoc Gate-2 adversarial pass). Recommendation for the U-349 program's remaining units: before
  deleting a `LineEntitySpec`/`FlatEntitySpec` registry row, require a one-line prod query proving zero
  "unstamped but still-mapped" rows exist for that family — not prose-only "TEMPORARY, re-remove later"
  discipline. Process/tooling change, not a code fix; own unit if adopted.

## Found during iOS Simulator QA sweep (2026-09-02) — not fixed, scope was test-data cleanup only

- [ ] **`DeleteTimeEntryById` has no child cascade — `DELETE /time-entries/{public_id}` would 500 on any real
  entry.** The sproc (`entities/time_entry/sql/dbo.time_entry.sql`) is a bare `DELETE FROM dbo.TimeEntry WHERE
  Id=@Id`, but every entry gets a `dbo.TimeEntryStatus` row at creation (append-only, no delete method exists
  anywhere in `TimeEntryStatusRepository`) and any clocked segment adds a `dbo.TimeLog` row — both FK-reference
  `TimeEntry` with no `ON DELETE CASCADE` (standard for this repo — app code must cascade). So the "official"
  delete path (`TimeEntryService.delete_by_public_id` → `repo.delete_by_id` → the sproc) would hit an FK
  violation on any entry that ever received its initial 'draft' status stamp, i.e. every real entry — verified
  live 2026-09-02 while cleaning up a QA test entry (had to delete the `TimeLog` child via `TimeLogService`
  first, then leave the now-empty parent `TimeEntry` row in place, since there's no sanctioned way to remove
  its `TimeEntryStatus` history row at all). Fix needs a design decision: either the sproc deletes children
  in-transaction (breaks the append-only-history intent) or the delete endpoint is retired/replaced with an
  explicit archive-style flow. Out of scope for the QA session that found it.

## Stale `qbo.InvoiceLineItemInvoiceLine` mappings after a QBO invoice gets recreated with the same DocNumber — found during U-362b's 70-row backfill, 2026-09-03

- [ ] **`qbo.Invoice` has 24 DocNumber collisions in the live realm** (`9130353016965726`) — a QBO invoice getting
  voided/recreated (new `QboId`, same human-readable `DocNumber`) is a known, recurring pattern this codebase
  already has header-level machinery for (`DRIFT_DUPLICATE_QBO_INVOICE_NUMBER`, U-034's "rule of three"
  fingerprint-adopt), but at least one invoice shows the machinery didn't fully repoint everything downstream.
  Full collision list (`DocNumber → qbo.Invoice.Id`s, all same realm):
  `BR-MAIN-18 (29,30)`, `CC-02 (796,799)`, `FP-07 (416,417)`, `FP-14 (277,278)`, `HA-01 (9 rows: 948,949,921,
  914,915,916,917,918,919)`, `HA-02 (920,925)`, `HE12-Initial (883,892)`, `HE7-12 (440,441)`, `HP2-01 (112,113)`,
  `HP2-12 (998,991)`, `HP-22 (31,32)`, `HRL-04 (614,579)`, `MR2-MAIN-04 (34,35)`, `MR2-MAIN-05 (950,905)`,
  `MR2-SITE-04 (951,906)`, `OHR2-25 (107,108)`, `OHR2-30 (27,28)`, **`OHR2-37 (992,1000)` — the one confirmed
  affected, see below**, `OHR2-GUEST-03 (105,106)`, `OL-09 (21,22)`, `OL-10 (931,932)`, `SD2-01 (192,193)`,
  `TB3-17 (926,927)`, `WVA-11 (5,6)`, `WVA-14 (3,1)`. Only OHR2-37 has been checked for the downstream symptom
  below — the other 23 are unverified and may or may not be affected.
- [ ] **Confirmed symptom on `dbo.Invoice 1080` (OHR2-37, `QboId=75290` — the current invoice; `75707` is the
  old/superseded one, same DocNumber, different total: $271,800.48 vs $235,083.66):** 15 of its
  `dbo.InvoiceLineItem` rows have a `qbo.InvoiceLineItemInvoiceLine` mapping row that still points at a
  `qbo.InvoiceLine` staging row under the OLD `qbo.Invoice.Id=1000` (`75707`), not the current `992` (`75290`).
  Ids: `34055,34056,34057,34058,34059,34060,34061,34062,34063,34064,34065,34066,34067,34068,34070`. Verified
  their `dbo.QboId` values (101-114, plus one that was "16" before U-362b's backfill touched it) are the
  CORRECT, current ones — matching their actual position among the invoice's 114 current lines, almost
  certainly stamped correctly by the still-live connector's normal dual-write on their last real pull against
  the current invoice (`992`). It's the MAPPING ROW that's stale, not the dbo identity — so
  `check_qbo_identity_drift_lines.py`'s `identity_match=FAIL` finding for these 14 (`34070` isn't flagged
  because it currently reads `QboId IS NULL`) is real but backwards-pointing: trust `dbo`, not the mapping.
- [ ] **What happened during U-362b's `--mode missing --apply` run (2026-09-03):** row `33346` ("Grill
  Kitchen", $4,611.86) was one of the genuine 70 mapped-but-unstamped rows fixed by that backfill — its
  mapping correctly points at line `16` under the CURRENT invoice (`992`), verified directly against
  `qbo.InvoiceLine` staging content (description + amount match exactly). Stamping it correctly triggered
  `SetInvoiceLineItemQboIdentity`'s theft-clear against row `34070`, which was WRONGLY holding `QboId=16`
  (inherited from ITS stale mapping to the OLD invoice, where line `16` was a different line entirely — a
  "14%" fee, $28,869.92). The theft-clear worked exactly as designed; net result is strictly better than
  before (33346 now correctly identified; 34070 correctly reset to unstamped instead of squatting on someone
  else's identity). **Do NOT re-run the backfill for row 34070 specifically** — its mapping is the stale one,
  so a naive re-run would immediately steal `QboId=16` back from 33346, ping-ponging the identity between the
  two rows depending on run order. Left as `QboId IS NULL`; expected to self-heal correctly once U-362/U-362b
  deploy, via the NEW provenance-based recognizer (`InvoiceLineItemSourceProvenance`/`LinkedTxnType`/
  `LinkedTxnId`), which doesn't go through the mapping table at all and was almost certainly stamped correctly
  on 34070's own last real pull (2026-08-27, the same batch as the other 14).
- [ ] **Fix direction (own unit, not touched here):** for each of the 24 DocNumber-collision invoices, find any
  `dbo.InvoiceLineItem` rows whose `qbo.InvoiceLineItemInvoiceLine` mapping points at the OLDER/superseded
  `qbo.Invoice.Id` (not the one the row's own parent `dbo.Invoice.QboId` currently resolves to) and either (a)
  repoint the mapping to the correct current staging line (if one exists with matching content), or (b) simply
  clear the stale mapping and let it re-resolve on the next pull — U-362/U-362b's dbo-only path plus the
  provenance recognizer should handle re-identification correctly either way once deployed. Query to find
  affected rows for a given `dbo.Invoice`: join `dbo.InvoiceLineItem` → `qbo.InvoiceLineItemInvoiceLine` →
  `qbo.InvoiceLine` → `qbo.Invoice`, and compare that `qbo.Invoice.Id` against the one the header's own
  `dbo.Invoice.QboId`/`RealmId` resolves to.
